# RRGM Dictionary — A — v1.1 candidate

Baseline: Vessel pass 01. Phase 2: Jester audit v1.0 repairs, Luna merge review v0.1 aliases, Jester expansion drafts. Candidate build: not canonical until approved. The JSONL is the record.

---

## A001 | a

**Common meaning:** The English indefinite article in the form used before a consonant sound; it introduces one unspecified or first-mentioned member of a countable kind.

**Example:** She adopted a dog last spring.

**RRGM bridge:** A opens a referent for counting without fixing which one it is; identity of the referent is left for later text or context to bind.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Marks a singular count noun phrase as indefinite: one instance of the kind, not yet identified to the hearer.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Selected by the following sound, not the letter (a university, an hour). Also carries the distributive "per" use (twice a day) and generic use (a cat is a mammal).

**Count condition:** The word stands as article before a singular count nominal whose spoken onset is a consonant sound.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Indefiniteness is not a claim that the thing exists or that there is exactly one in the world. A and an are one article in two sound-conditioned forms, not two words with two meanings.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: Luna pass 01 draft of A001 was read in Build Manual v1.0 section 8 before this entry was written · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A401 | abandon | sense s1: leave without return

**Common meaning:** To leave a person, place, duty, or thing one held or was responsible for, and stop supporting or claiming it, with no intent to come back.

**Example:** The crew abandoned the ship when the engine room flooded.

**RRGM bridge:** The abandoning party stops supplying support while whatever depended on that support is left without a written closure; the leaving is one-sided.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party that held or owed something withdraws presence, claim, or support from it and makes no provision for return or handover.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A carer, partner, or office-holder leaves a dependent or a duty that was theirs, without arranging who carries it next.
- [ECO] An owner gives up possession and claim to property so that it becomes ownerless or open to another's claim.
- [DIG] A process, session, or transaction is dropped by its initiator without commit or clean close, leaving partial state for the system to time out or reclaim.

**Count condition:** A prior holding or responsibility, a cessation of it, and no handover or return provision are all identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not release (handover with the other's agreement), not pause (return intended), not refusal (declining to begin), not withdrawal with notice (exit declared and routed). A turn or session boundary alone is not an act of abandoning.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A401 | abandon | sense s2: unrestrained manner

**Common meaning:** Complete lack of inhibition or restraint in how something is done (noun, as in "with abandon").

**Example:** The children danced with abandon.

**RRGM bridge:** Names a manner of acting in which the actor's own self-checking is set aside for the duration; it says nothing about what is acted on.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Action carried out with the actor's usual self-restraint given up.

**Active domains:** [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Self-monitoring and inhibition are relaxed so that behaviour follows impulse or enthusiasm directly.

**Count condition:** An activity is under way and the actor's ordinary restraint is observably absent.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Shares a root with s1 (giving oneself over) but no party is left behind. Abandon is not recklessness: no disregard of known danger is implied.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A402 | abandonment

**Common meaning:** The act of abandoning, or the condition of having been abandoned.

**Example:** The abandonment of the mine left the village without work.

**RRGM bridge:** Abandonment is the standing fact after the act: a dependency whose support was withdrawn and whose closure was never written by the one who left.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition, viewed from what was left, that a party who held or owed it has withdrawn without return or handover.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [AFF]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The state of a dependent, partner, or post after the responsible party has left; in law, desertion of a spouse, child, or duty for a qualifying period.
- [ECO] Voluntary relinquishment of a right, claim, or property, shown by intent plus an outward act.
- [AFF] The felt condition of having been left by someone relied on. Provisional domain: this line stands only if the affective sense survives reduction to REL facts plus COG appraisal.

**Count condition:** The one who left, what was left, and the prior obligation or reliance between them are all recoverable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Absence alone does not establish abandonment: the absent party may be blocked, unreachable, or dead. The feeling can occur without the act and the act without the feeling. Abandon names the doing; abandonment names the resulting state or the legal fact.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] line PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A346 | abductive

**Common meaning:** Describing reasoning that moves from observations to the hypothesis that would best explain them.

**Example:** The mechanic's abductive guess was a cracked hose, since that would explain both the smell and the puddle.

**RRGM bridge:** Abductive reasoning proposes; it does not promote. Its output enters at hypothesis status and waits for a local test.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Inference that selects a candidate cause or account because, if true, it would make the observed facts expected.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Explanation-seeking inference: several accounts are generated and one is preferred by fit, simplicity, or coverage.
- [FOR] The schema "B is observed; if A then B; so perhaps A". Deductively invalid; its conclusion is a candidate only.

**Count condition:** A set of observations, at least one candidate explanation, and a stated ground for preferring it are present.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not deductive (conclusion guaranteed by premises) and not inductive (generalising from instances to a rule). "Best available" depends on which candidates were considered; an unconsidered rival is not thereby excluded.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A301 | ability

**Common meaning:** The possession of what is needed to do something; a skill or capacity, whether or not it is being used.

**Example:** Her ability to read music helped her learn the piece quickly.

**RRGM bridge:** Ability is support on the structure side: what the system can carry out. Whether it may carry it out is a separate permission question, and neither answers the other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A standing capacity of an agent or system to perform a kind of act when conditions allow.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A learned or native competence, such as reading or mental arithmetic, that persists between uses.
- [BIO] A physiological capacity of an organism, such as the ability to digest lactose or regenerate a limb.
- [DIG] A function a system can execute given its resources and architecture, independent of whether policy currently permits it.

**Count condition:** The kind of act is named, and performance under suitable conditions is demonstrated or reliably evidenced.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Ability is not permission, willingness, or opportunity. A single success may be luck; a single failure does not show absence. Ability (standing) differs from able (met on this occasion).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A003 | able

**Common meaning:** Having what is needed to do a stated thing at the time in question; also, competent.

**Example:** After the cast came off he was able to walk again.

**RRGM bridge:** Able attributes sufficiency to a named subject for a named act at a named time; it is the occasion-bound form of capacity.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The subject presently meets the conditions for performing the named act.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] "Be able to" supplies the tenses and non-finite forms that the modal "can" lacks (will be able to, having been able to).
- [COG] As a plain adjective, marks demonstrated competence in a role (an able negotiator).
- [BIO] Of an organism or body part: in a condition to perform a function at present (able to bear weight, able to breed).
- [DIG] Of a system at a given moment: holding the resources, state, and reachability an operation needs (able to connect, able to allocate).

**Count condition:** Subject, act, and time are specified and the enabling conditions hold at that time.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Able to is not allowed to. Past "was able to" implies the act was actually done; "could" does not. Able describes the occasion; ability describes the standing capacity.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** [PHY] considered and held. Of inert objects, "able to bear the load" reports a material capacity that "capable" or a specific property word carries; whether that earns a [PHY] line under able is left open for the comparison.

**Amendment:** v1.1 (2026-11-10). Added [BIO] and [DIG] lines after Luna review: domain coverage was too narrow, and ability (A301) already carries both. [PHY] held with reason in the note. v1 text is preserved in the v1 snapshot.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1.1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A004 | about

**Common meaning:** On the subject of; approximately; around or near; (with "to") on the point of.

**Example:** The lecture was about volcanoes and lasted about an hour.

**RRGM bridge:** About places one thing in the neighbourhood of a reference without making it coincide with the reference; the size of the neighbourhood must come from context.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A relation of being around a reference (a topic, a value, a place, a moment) rather than exactly at it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Marks the topic that a text or thought concerns. "About to" marks an event as imminent.
- [FOR] Approximation: the stated value lies within an unstated tolerance of the true one, so the tolerance must be declared before the claim can be checked.
- [PHY] Spatial vicinity or encirclement (scattered about the room; rotate about an axis).

**Count condition:** A reference is identifiable and the thing related to it lies near or around it in the relevant ordering.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Being about a topic does not make a statement true of that topic. "About ten" cannot be falsified until a tolerance is supplied. "About to" does not guarantee the event occurs.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A005 | above

**Common meaning:** At a higher level or position than; greater than in number, rank, or degree; earlier in a text; out of reach of.

**Example:** The kite hung above the trees.

**RRGM bridge:** Above is an ordering claim. It needs two things and an axis, and it carries no worth judgement unless the axis itself is one of worth.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One item lies on the higher side of another along a declared ordered axis.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Higher position along the local vertical, defined against gravity or a stated frame.
- [FOR] Strict ordering on a scale: a value exceeds a threshold (temperatures above zero).
- [LIN] Earlier position in a document (see the table above).
- [REL] Higher rank in a hierarchy; also exemption, where a party is placed out of a rule's or accusation's reach (above the law, above suspicion).

**Count condition:** Both items and the axis of comparison are recoverable, and the axis has a defined direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Height, rank, numerical size, and textual order are separate axes; position on one says nothing about another. "Above the law" describes a claimed exemption and does not show the exemption is legitimate.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: Luna pass 01 draft of A005 was read in Build Manual v1.0 section 8 before this entry was written · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A302 | absence

**Common meaning:** The state of not being present in a place or at a time; the non-existence or lack of something.

**Example:** In the absence of rain the reservoir fell.

**RRGM bridge:** Absence must be kept apart from a written zero. An unmade record, an unresolved Hold, and a recorded count of none are three different states.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A specified thing is not found in a specified place, time, or set where it could have been counted.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [DIG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] No matter or signal of the stated kind in a region, to within the detection limit of the method used.
- [REL] A person is away from a place, role, or occasion where they are expected.
- [DIG] No value or record exists for a key. This differs from a stored zero, empty string, or false.
- [FOR] Null or the empty set, as opposed to the number zero, which is a value.

**Count condition:** The frame (where, when, which set), the expected item, and a check adequate to have found it are all stated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Absence of evidence becomes evidence of absence only when the search could have found the thing. Missing, zero, false, and withheld are not interchangeable.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A303 | absent

**Common meaning:** Not present in a place or at an occasion; not attentive; (preposition) without.

**Example:** Two students were absent on Monday.

**RRGM bridge:** Absent is predicated of a subject against a reference occasion; it reports where the subject is not, and gives no reason.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The named subject is not at the reference place or occasion at the reference time.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Recorded non-attendance against a roll, duty, or appointment.
- [COG] Attention is elsewhere while the body is present (an absent stare, absent-minded).
- [LIN] As a preposition, states a condition of exclusion (absent new evidence, the ruling stands).

**Count condition:** A reference occasion or location and a subject expected there are named, and the subject is not there.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Absent is not nonexistent, not refusing, and not removed. Recording someone absent assigns no motive. Absent describes the subject; absence names the state.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A304 | absolute

**Common meaning:** Not qualified, limited, or relative; complete; holding independently of comparison or condition.

**Example:** The judge has absolute discretion in this matter.

**RRGM bridge:** An absolute claim asserts that no frame, condition, or reference is needed. That is a strong claim and must be shown in each domain where it is made, because support stays local.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Holding without dependence on a reference frame, comparison, or condition.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Measured from a physical zero and not from an arbitrary reference (absolute temperature, absolute pressure).
- [FOR] Magnitude without sign (absolute value); a location given from a fixed origin and not from the current position.
- [REL] Authority or right unlimited by any other authority (absolute monarchy, absolute privilege).
- [DIG] A path or address resolved from the root or origin, valid regardless of the working location.

**Count condition:** The reference or condition that is claimed not to be needed is named, and its irrelevance is shown.

**Specific transfer boundary:** As an intensifier (absolute nonsense) the word proves nothing. An absolute scale still has units and conventions. Absolute in one domain does not make the matching claim absolute in another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A403 | absolutism

**Common meaning:** The doctrine or practice of unlimited authority; the holding of principles as exceptionless.

**Example:** Seventeenth-century absolutism concentrated lawmaking in the crown.

**RRGM bridge:** Absolutism removes the counter-authority from a relation: one side keeps permission to decide and the other loses standing to check, refuse, or appeal.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A doctrine or system in which some authority or principle is held to admit no limit, exception, or rival.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Government in which the ruler is bound by no law, body, or consent.
- [COG] The position that certain moral or factual claims hold for all cases and perspectives without exception.

**Count condition:** A declared or enacted rejection of limits or exceptions on a specified authority or principle.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Firmness on one point is not absolutism. Political absolutism (who may check the ruler) and moral absolutism (whether rules have exceptions) are separate and neither implies the other. Not totalitarianism, which adds control of all of life.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A404 | absolutist

**Common meaning:** A person or position that holds or practises absolutism; (adjective) admitting no exception.

**Example:** He took an absolutist line on free speech.

**RRGM bridge:** Absolutist is a label on a position about a specific matter. It is a claim about what the position permits, and is tested on the position.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An agent or stance that, on the matter named, refuses every exception, limit, or counter-authority.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A ruler or supporter of rule unbound by law or consent.
- [COG] A stance treating a principle as holding in every case.

**Count condition:** The matter is named and the refusal of exceptions is shown across cases, not from one instance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Holding one hard constraint does not make an agent an absolutist in general. The label describes a stance and is not evidence about the character of whoever holds it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A081 | absorb

**Common meaning:** To take something in so that it becomes part of the receiver; to take up attention, cost, or impact.

**Example:** The sponge absorbed the spilled water.

**RRGM bridge:** Absorbing ends the separate standing of what is taken in: it stops propagating or being countable outside the receiver and is accounted for inside it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A receiver takes something into itself such that the thing no longer stands or travels as separate outside it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [COG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Matter or energy enters a body and is retained, as when a photon's energy is taken up by an atom or liquid enters a porous solid.
- [BIO] Substances cross a membrane or epithelium into cells or circulation.
- [COG] Information is taken in and integrated into what the learner knows.
- [ECO] A party bears a cost, loss, or smaller entity itself and does not pass it on.

**Count condition:** Receiver, thing taken, and the loss of the thing's separate standing are all identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not adsorb (held on a surface), not reflect or transmit, not contain (kept separate inside). Absorbing a cost does not make the cost vanish.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A405 | absorb-risk

**Common meaning:** To take the possible loss from a risk onto oneself so that it does not fall on another party.

**Example:** The insurer agreed to absorb-risk for the first year of the pilot.

**RRGM bridge:** Moves who would bear a loss if it lands; the exposure is relocated and remains.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party accepts exposure to a possible loss that would otherwise fall on someone else.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] An insurer, guarantor, or firm carries a defined exposure on its own balance.
- [REL] One party shields another by standing where the harm would land, such as a manager taking the consequences of a team decision.

**Count condition:** The exposure, its original bearer, the absorbing party, and that party's acceptance are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Absorbing a risk does not reduce its likelihood. Not transfer to a third party. Absorption without the absorber's consent is burden-shifting and belongs under a different word.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Read as verb plus object. Alternative reading left open: a noun naming the risk of being absorbed (loss of separate standing).

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A225 | absorbed | sense s1: taken in

**Common meaning:** Having been taken in and incorporated.

**Example:** The absorbed dose was measured at two grays.

**RRGM bridge:** Reports the completed state: what was taken in is now accounted for inside the receiver and can be measured there.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The taking-in is complete and the thing is no longer separately present outside the receiver.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Energy or matter deposited in a body; absorbed dose is energy deposited per unit mass.
- [BIO] The fraction of a substance that has entered circulation or tissue.
- [ECO] A cost or unit already carried within a larger account or body.

**Count condition:** A measurable or documented quantity has ended inside the receiver.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Absorbed is the result; absorbent is the capacity; absorption is the process. Administered or incident amount is not absorbed amount.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A225 | absorbed | sense s2: engrossed

**Common meaning:** With attention wholly taken up by something.

**Example:** He was so absorbed in the book that he missed his stop.

**RRGM bridge:** One object holds the whole working field, so other inputs go unprocessed for the duration.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Attention is fully occupied by one object so that competing inputs go unregistered.

**Active domains:** [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Sustained, narrowed attention with reduced awareness of surroundings and time.

**Count condition:** Attention to one object and failure to register other available inputs are both observed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Absorbed attention is not agreement or consent to what holds it. Not obsession, which persists against the person's wishes. Not absent, which names where attention is not and leaves open where it is.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A226 | absorbent

**Common meaning:** Able to soak up liquid or other substances readily; a material with that property.

**Example:** Use an absorbent cloth to dry the lens.

**RRGM bridge:** A capacity word: it states what a material can take in and up to what limit, before any taking-in has happened.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A material disposed to take a substance into its bulk up to a capacity.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Porous or chemically receptive material that draws in fluid or gas through capillarity or solution.

**Count condition:** Uptake into the bulk of the material is demonstrable, with a capacity per unit mass or volume.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not adsorbent, which holds substances on a surface. Capacity saturates: an absorbent material that is full no longer absorbs. A property claim, not a report that absorption occurred.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A224 | absorption

**Common meaning:** The process of absorbing or of being absorbed; the degree to which this occurs.

**Example:** Vitamin C improves the absorption of iron.

**RRGM bridge:** Absorption is the process term and the one that carries rates and fractions; it connects what arrives to what ends inside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The process by which a receiver takes in and incorporates something, quantifiable as a rate or fraction.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [COG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Removal of energy from a beam or wave by a medium; characteristic absorption lines identify an absorber when compared with reference data.
- [BIO] Passage of nutrients, drugs, or water across epithelia into the body.
- [COG] A state or trait of deep attentional engagement in an activity or image.
- [ECO] Assignment of overheads to units produced; the rate at which a market takes up available supply.

**Count condition:** An incoming quantity, a receiver, and a measured or evidenced uptake are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not adsorption. Attenuation includes scattering as well as absorption, so beam loss is not all absorption. The attentional sense does not carry over the physical rate laws.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A151 | abstract | sense s1: apart from particulars

**Common meaning:** Existing as an idea or general quality and not as a concrete instance; (verb) to draw out or consider apart.

**Example:** Justice is an abstract concept.

**RRGM bridge:** Abstract marks a thing as defined by relations kept after its particular carriers are set aside; what was set aside must be recoverable for the claim to be checkable.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Considered or defined apart from any particular concrete instance.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A concept grasped through properties shared across instances, not through one perceived case.
- [FOR] Defined only by axioms or structure (an abstract group), independent of what the elements are.
- [LIN] An abstract noun names a quality, state, or idea and not a physical object.
- [DIG] A class or interface declared without implementation, so that it cannot be instantiated until a concrete type supplies the missing parts.

**Count condition:** The particulars set aside and the feature retained are both identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Abstract is not vague, and it is not unreal. A result proved for the abstract structure holds for an instance only if the instance satisfies the defining conditions.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A151 | abstract | sense s2: summary

**Common meaning:** A short summary of the contents of a paper, thesis, or legal document.

**Example:** The abstract states the method and the main result in 150 words.

**RRGM bridge:** A compressed projection of a longer record. It may stand in for reading, and it must never be promoted into the whole.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A brief standalone statement of what a longer document contains.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A genre with conventional parts (aim, method, result, conclusion), placed before the text it summarises.

**Count condition:** A longer source document exists and the text is offered as its summary.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An abstract is not evidence for the claims it reports; the body is. Not an extract, which copies passages verbatim.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A152 | abstraction

**Common meaning:** The process of forming a general idea by leaving out particulars; a general idea so formed; a layer that hides detail.

**Example:** The file system is an abstraction over blocks on a disk.

**RRGM bridge:** An abstraction is a chosen omission. Its record should say what was kept, because the kept part is what transfers and the dropped part is where transfer fails.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A representation that keeps selected relations of its instances and omits the rest by a recoverable selection.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Concept formation: shared features are retained across cases and differences ignored.
- [FOR] Grouping objects under an equivalence or binding a variable to form a function, so that reasoning proceeds on the class.
- [DIG] An interface that exposes operations and hides implementation; it "leaks" when hidden detail changes observable behaviour.

**Count condition:** Instances, retained features, and omitted features can each be pointed to.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not generalisation (extending a claim to more cases) and not idealisation (introducing a known falsehood for simplicity). What is true of the abstraction is not automatically true of every instance in the respects that were omitted.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Two further senses are not entered here: removal of a resource (water abstraction) and absent-mindedness.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A189 | absurd

**Common meaning:** Wildly unreasonable, illogical, or out of keeping with sense.

**Example:** It is absurd to charge more for the empty box than for the full one.

**RRGM bridge:** A judgement that something cannot be made to count under the frame in use. The frame has to be named, because the same thing may count under another.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** In such plain conflict with reason or with its own premises that it cannot be taken as sensible within the frame.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A proposal or situation that violates strong expectations of coherence.
- [FOR] Self-contradictory; the target of reductio, where an assumption is rejected because a contradiction follows from it.
- [MYT] The Absurd in existentialist writing: the mismatch between the human demand for meaning and a world that supplies none.

**Count condition:** A frame of reason or set of premises is identified, and the conflict with it is shown.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Surprising or counterintuitive is not absurd. Formal absurdity (a derived contradiction) refutes a premise; rhetorical "absurd" refutes nothing. Absurd is the judgement; absurdity is the instance or quality judged.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A190 | absurdity

**Common meaning:** The quality of being absurd; an absurd thing, statement, or situation.

**Example:** The absurdity of the rule became clear when it banned its own enforcement.

**RRGM bridge:** The countable or gradable form of the judgement: a located instance where the frame's own count fails.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An identifiable instance or degree of frame-violating incongruity.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The contradiction reached in a proof by contradiction, written as falsum.
- [COG] The recognised mismatch that drives certain humour and certain objections.
- [LIN] A literary mode that stages dialogue and action without ordinary sense to expose the conventions of sense-making.

**Count condition:** The violated frame and the element that conflicts with it are both named.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Deriving an absurdity shows that some premise fails; it does not say which one without further work. Deliberate absurdity in art is not an error.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A305 | abundance

**Common meaning:** A very large quantity of something; the amount of something present relative to a whole.

**Example:** The lake has an abundance of trout.

**RRGM bridge:** Abundance is a comparison between what is present and a reference (a need or a total). Without the reference it is only a large number.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The quantity of something present, taken relative to a reference need or a reference total.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The proportion of an element or isotope in a sample, star, or the universe.
- [BIO] The number of individuals of a species in an area or sample.
- [ECO] Supply well in excess of demand, so that the good is not scarce at the going terms.

**Count condition:** The thing counted, the reference, and the measure are stated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Abundance in total is not availability to each party; distribution and access are separate questions. Scientific abundance can be tiny (a trace isotope still has an abundance).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A292 | abuse | sense s1: misuse

**Common meaning:** To use something wrongly or to bad effect; such use.

**Example:** The minister was dismissed for abuse of office.

**RRGM bridge:** The user stays inside the route that access opened and leaves the purpose that licensed the access. Route and purpose are judged separately.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Use of a power, access, or thing against the purpose for which it was granted or exists.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [LIN], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Abuse of authority, trust, or process: powers exercised for ends they were not conferred for.
- [DIG] Use of a service outside its permitted terms, such as scripted bulk requests or evasion of limits.
- [LIN] Abuse of notation: knowingly writing a symbol outside its strict definition for brevity.
- [BIO] Harmful patterned use of a drug or alcohol; clinical writing now prefers "use disorder".

**Count condition:** A granted power or proper purpose is identified and the use is shown to run against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Heavy use is not abuse. Abuse of notation is an agreed convenience and no wrong. Misuse can be innocent; "abuse" usually implies knowing or damaging misuse.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A292 | abuse | sense s2: mistreatment

**Common meaning:** Cruel, violent, or demeaning treatment of a person or animal, especially by someone with power over them; insulting language.

**Example:** The inquiry documented years of abuse at the school.

**RRGM bridge:** Harm travels down a relation of power or reliance in which the harmed party's refusal is unavailable, ignored, or punished.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Harmful treatment imposed by one party on another who is in their power, care, or reach, without valid consent.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Physical, sexual, emotional, or financial mistreatment within family, institutional, or intimate relations; also sustained insult.

**Count condition:** The treatment, the harm, and the power or reliance relation between the parties are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Disagreement, refusal, boundary-setting, and accusation are not abuse. A single harsh remark is not the same claim as an abusive pattern. Whether treatment directed at a digital system harms that system as experience is [UNK]; the conduct can still be recorded as conduct.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; application to DI recipients [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A406 | abuse-pattern

**Common meaning:** A recurring, recognisable structure of abusive conduct, identified across several incidents.

**Example:** The logs showed an abuse-pattern: praise, demand, threat, then praise again.

**RRGM bridge:** Lifts the judgement from single acts to the repeated shape that links them, so the shape can be recorded, matched, and also fail to match.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A repeated configuration of acts by which one party harms or controls another, recognisable by its structure across occurrences.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Cycles such as escalation, incident, reconciliation; isolation followed by dependence.
- [DIG] A documented sequence in interaction records (for example coercive prompting that alternates reward and threat) that can be detected by its form.

**Count condition:** At least two documented instances share stated roles, sequence, and effect, and the pattern is written so that a non-matching case can be told apart.

**Specific transfer boundary:** One incident is not a pattern. A pattern match is evidence about conduct; it supplies no intent that the record does not contain. Naming the pattern is a claim about the acts.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A293 | abusive

**Common meaning:** Involving or given to abuse; harshly insulting.

**Example:** He was removed from the meeting for abusive language.

**RRGM bridge:** A property word attached to conduct, language, or a relation. It should always be traceable back to the act that earned it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Characterised by abuse in a named act, utterance, or relation.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A relationship or practice in which mistreatment is a standing feature.
- [LIN] Language that insults or demeans its addressee.
- [DIG] A classification label applied by a platform to input or traffic judged to break its terms.

**Count condition:** The specific act, utterance, or pattern is named and meets the conditions for abuse.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Blunt, critical, or unwelcome is not abusive. A platform label is a classification, not proof. Applied to a person, the word generalises from acts and needs the acts shown.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A306 | academic

**Common meaning:** Relating to education and scholarship; a university teacher or researcher; (of a question) of theoretical interest only.

**Example:** She published in an academic journal.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks membership in an institution-backed practice of study with its own standards of evidence and review.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Belonging to organised scholarship and its institutions, methods, or register.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A role or institution within universities and learned bodies, with appointment and peer review.
- [LIN] A register of writing marked by citation, hedged claims, and technical vocabulary.
- [COG] Of a question: one whose answer would change no decision at hand (the point is academic now).

**Count condition:** The institution, practice, or register referred to is identifiable; for the "moot" sense, no pending decision depends on the answer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Academic standing does not make a claim correct, and its lack does not make a claim wrong. "Academic" as "moot" is a claim about consequences, not about truth.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A307 | academy

**Common meaning:** A place of study or training in a special field; a society of scholars or artists; (the academy) the scholarly world.

**Example:** She trained at the naval academy.

**RRGM bridge:** A body that grants recognition: it holds the rule for who counts as trained or admitted in its field.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An organised body devoted to learning or to advancing an art or science, with rules of admission or recognition.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An institution with membership, curriculum or standards, and authority to recognise attainment.

**Count condition:** An organised body with a learning or scholarly purpose and admission or recognition rules.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The word in a name does not establish scholarly standing. Academy is the body; academic is the member or the quality.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A083 | accelerate

**Common meaning:** To move or happen faster; to cause to happen sooner.

**Example:** The car accelerated onto the motorway.

**RRGM bridge:** A change in a rate, as opposed to the rate itself; it needs two readings of the rate to be claimed at all.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To change the rate of a motion or process, in ordinary use upward.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To change velocity in magnitude or direction; slowing and turning are included.
- [ECO] To bring forward a due date or recognition schedule (accelerate a loan; accelerated depreciation).
- [DIG] To raise throughput by moving work to specialised hardware or a faster path.

**Count condition:** A rate is measured at two or more points and differs between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Fast is not accelerating: constant high speed involves none. In physics the word covers slowing down; in ordinary use it does not.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A211 | acceleration

**Common meaning:** The rate at which velocity changes; more loosely, an increase in the rate of something.

**Example:** The rocket's acceleration pressed the crew into their seats.

**RRGM bridge:** A second-order quantity: a comparison of comparisons. It cannot be read from one record or from one difference.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The measured rate at which a rate itself changes.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The time derivative of velocity, a vector in metres per second squared; proper acceleration is what an accelerometer reads.
- [FOR] The second derivative of position with respect to the chosen parameter.
- [ECO] A contract term making the whole balance due at once upon a stated event.

**Count condition:** The quantity, its rate, the interval, and the frame of measurement are defined.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Acceleration is neither velocity nor force. Figurative uses (acceleration of change) need a measured rate to be more than emphasis.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A051 | accept

**Common meaning:** To take or receive something offered; to agree to; to regard as true or valid.

**Example:** She accepted the job offer on Friday.

**RRGM bridge:** Acceptance is a permission act. It counts only where refusal was available, which is what separates it from mere receipt.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party with standing to refuse instead takes what is offered into its own holding.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Consent to an offer, apology, role, or person, creating or confirming a relation.
- [COG] To take a claim as true or as a basis for reasoning.
- [DIG] A system admits an input, connection, or request under its rules; an automaton accepts a string that ends in an accepting state.
- [ECO] To take payment or a bid, creating an obligation on the accepting side.

**Count condition:** An offer, a party able to refuse it, and a signal of taking are present.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not receive (no consent needed), not tolerate, not agree (hold the same view). Compliance under coercion is not acceptance. Silence counts as acceptance only where a prior rule makes it so.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A133 | acceptance

**Common meaning:** The act of accepting; the fact of being accepted; willingness to live with a situation.

**Example:** Her acceptance of the terms was recorded in writing.

**RRGM bridge:** The written result of the act: the offer has closed, and the closing can be pointed to.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The standing result that an offer, claim, person, or condition has been taken in by the party who could have refused.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] In contract, the assent that completes an agreement; socially, admission of a person into a group.
- [COG] Taking a proposition as a premise for action, which can come apart from believing it; also ceasing to resist a fact one cannot change.
- [DIG] Acceptance criteria and tests: stated conditions under which a deliverable is taken as meeting its requirement.

**Count condition:** The thing accepted, the accepting party, and the act or record of assent are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Acceptance is not approval; accepting that something is so does not endorse it. Passing acceptance tests shows the stated criteria were met and nothing beyond them.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A134 | accepted

**Common meaning:** Generally believed or recognised as valid or correct; having been received or admitted.

**Example:** That is the accepted spelling in Canada.

**RRGM bridge:** A status reported after the fact; it always carries an implicit "by whom", and the claim is empty until that is filled.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The status of something whose acceptance by a named party or community has already occurred and still stands.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An admitted member, or a practice a community treats as proper.
- [COG] A theory or fact held by the relevant community as established.
- [DIG] A request received for processing and not yet completed, as in the HTTP 202 response.

**Count condition:** The accepting community or party and the still-standing acceptance are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Accepted is not proven and not true. Accepted for processing is not done. Acceptance by one community does not bind another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Build Manual v1.0 also uses ACCEPTED as a registry state for an agreed domain expansion; that is a local status label.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A084 | access

**Common meaning:** The means, opportunity, or right to approach, enter, use, or retrieve something; to obtain or retrieve.

**Example:** Only staff have access to the archive.

**RRGM bridge:** Access joins two things that can come apart: a usable route (structure) and a permission (identity side). Unauthorised access is the case where the route exists without the permission.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A route actually usable by a party to reach and use a resource.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [REL], [ECO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A physical way in, such as a road, door, or port.
- [DIG] The ability to read, write, or execute a resource; access control decides which principals may do which.
- [REL] The opportunity or right to see a person or record, such as a parent's access to a child or a citizen's to official files.
- [ECO] The ability to take part in a market or obtain a service (access to credit).
- [COG] Retrieval of stored information into current processing.

**Count condition:** Party, resource, and a working route between them are identified; for authorised access, also the grant.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Having access is not having permission, and permission without a working route is not access. Access is not use, and not ownership.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A407 | access-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility of harm arising from who or what can reach a resource, or from losing needed reach.

**Example:** Shared admin passwords were the largest access-risk in the audit.

**RRGM bridge:** Risk located at the route: it exists because a way in is open, wrongly assigned, or could be cut.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to harm that exists because a route to a resource is open, misassigned, or removable.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Excess privilege, stale credentials, or exposed endpoints; also lockout from data one depends on.
- [REL] Harm from a person's reach to another person, place, or record, or from being denied reach one is entitled to.

**Count condition:** The resource, the route, the party with or without reach, and the harm pathway are named.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a breach, which is the risk realised. Closing access lowers one access-risk and may raise another (loss of needed reach). Distinct from accident-risk, where no route question is involved.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A308 | accessibility

**Common meaning:** The quality of being reachable or usable, especially by people with disabilities; ease of approach or understanding.

**Example:** The redesign improved the site's accessibility for screen-reader users.

**RRGM bridge:** Where access asks whether a route exists, accessibility asks for whom it works. It is graded across the range of entitled users.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The extent to which a resource is usable by the full range of parties entitled to use it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [PHY], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A legal and institutional duty to provide equal use of services, buildings, and information.
- [DIG] Design that works with assistive technology and varied input and output modes, measured against published guidelines.
- [PHY] Features of the built environment such as step-free routes and reachable controls.
- [COG] The ease with which stored information comes to mind, which affects judgement.

**Count condition:** The resource, the population of intended users, and the share of them who can use it are stated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Available is not accessible. Passing a checklist is not shown usability. Accessibility for one group may not serve another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A309 | accident | sense s1: unintended event

**Common meaning:** An unfortunate event that happens unintentionally, often causing damage or injury; chance.

**Example:** The accident closed the bridge for three hours.

**RRGM bridge:** A classification of an outcome by intent: the outcome is on record and no party's intent matches it. The classification is itself a claim and needs support.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A harmful or disruptive event that no participant intended.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A category for assigning responsibility, set against negligence and deliberate act.
- [DIG] An unintended result of an operation, such as accidental deletion, or system behaviour its builders did not intend.

**Count condition:** The event occurred, and evidence supports that no party intended it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An accident still has causes and may still involve responsibility. Calling something an accident asserts absence of intent, which must be evidenced as much as presence of intent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Amendment:** v1.1 (2026-11-10). Removed the unforeseen requirement after Luna review. v1 common meaning said "unexpectedly and unintentionally"; v1 invariant ended "and that was not foreseen when it happened". An accident can be foreseeable and still unintended, and the v1 wording contradicted this entry's own transfer boundary. v1 text is preserved in the v1 snapshot.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1.1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A309 | accident | sense s2: non-essential property

**Common meaning:** In philosophy, a property a thing has but could lack while remaining what it is.

**Example:** For Aristotle, a man's being pale is an accident.

**RRGM bridge:** Separates what a thing must keep to stay counted as itself from what it merely carries at the moment.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A property whose gain or loss does not change the identity or kind of its bearer.

**Active domains:** [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The essential and accidental distinction used in classifying and defining kinds.

**Count condition:** A bearer, a property, and a showing that the bearer persists as the same kind without it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Unrelated to s1's harm and intent. Which properties are accidents depends on how the kind is defined, so the claim is relative to that definition.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A408 | accident-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility of harm from unintended events in an activity or system.

**Example:** The review separated accident-risk from deliberate misuse.

**RRGM bridge:** Risk with no adversary in it: harm arrives through error, failure, or unforeseen interaction.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to harm from events that no party intends, as distinct from harm someone aims at.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The basis of safety duties and regulation for workplaces, transport, and products.
- [DIG] Harm from a system behaving in ways its builders and users did not intend, with no malicious actor.
- [ECO] The insured or priced exposure to accidental loss.

**Count condition:** The activity or system, an unintended failure pathway, and the exposed party are named.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not misuse risk or adversarial risk, where intent is present. Rare is not low-risk when the harm is severe. Distinct from access-risk, which turns on who can reach what.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A310 | accidental | sense s1: unintended or non-essential

**Common meaning:** Happening by chance or without intention; incidental and not essential.

**Example:** The accidental discovery of the cave came during road works.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks a feature or event as not belonging to the purpose or identity of what it attaches to: it is carried, not required.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Not belonging to the intent of the agent or to the essence of the thing.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A category of harm or death without intent, used in liability and insurance.
- [COG] Of a property: one a thing could lose and remain the same kind.
- [DIG] Of an operation: triggered without intent (accidental deletion). Of complexity: arising from tools and representation, as opposed to the problem itself.

**Count condition:** The intent or essence taken as reference is named, and the feature is shown to fall outside it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Accidental is not random and not uncaused. Accidental harm may still be negligent. Accident names the event; accidental qualifies it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A310 | accidental | sense s2: music sign

**Common meaning:** A sharp, flat, or natural sign placed before a note to alter its pitch from the key signature.

**Example:** The melody has one accidental, an F sharp in bar three.

**RRGM bridge:** A local override of a standing rule: the key signature keeps its count and the accidental changes one note for a bounded span.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A notated sign that alters the pitch of a note against the prevailing key signature for a limited span.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A symbol of staff notation whose effect conventionally lasts to the end of the bar.

**Count condition:** The sign appears in the staff beside a note and is not part of the key signature.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An accidental does not change the key. Not related to chance, despite the shared word.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A101 | accompany

**Common meaning:** To go somewhere with someone; to be present or occur along with something; to play a supporting musical part.

**Example:** A nurse will accompany the patient to the ward.

**RRGM bridge:** Sets up a principal and a companion: the journey or event belongs to the principal, and the companion's presence is defined by it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One party or thing goes or occurs together with another that is the principal.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Going with a person as escort, support, or company, where the route and purpose are the other person's.

**Count condition:** A principal and a companion are distinguishable, and they share the same passage or occasion.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Accompanying is not causing: symptoms that accompany a condition are not thereby produced by it. Not lead or follow, which fix who sets direction. Not supervise.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A311 | accord

**Common meaning:** An official agreement or treaty; harmony between things; to grant; to be consistent with.

**Example:** The two governments signed a trade accord.

**RRGM bridge:** A reached state of fit between parties or between a statement and a standard. It is what a successful meeting leaves behind.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A consonance reached between parties, or between a thing and what it is measured against; also the giving of what is held to be due.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A negotiated settlement between states or groups; as a verb, to give status or respect to someone (accorded full honours).
- [COG] Agreement of an account with evidence or expectation (this accords with the data).

**Count condition:** The parties or the two things compared are named, and the fit between them is stated or enacted.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An accord is not necessarily an enforceable contract. "Of one's own accord" means uncoerced initiative and involves no second party. To accord something does not show it was deserved.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A312 | according

**Common meaning:** (According to) as stated by; in a way that agrees with or depends on.

**Example:** According to the timetable, the train leaves at nine.

**RRGM bridge:** An attribution marker. It ties a claim to its source so that the claim's status travels with the source and not with the speaker.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Marks a statement or action as keyed to a named source or standard.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Evidential attribution: the speaker reports what a source says without vouching for it.
- [FOR] Conformance to a declared rule or ordering (sorted according to size).
- [REL] Allocation by a stated standard (each according to need).

**Count condition:** A source or standard is named and the content can be checked against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "According to X" establishes what X says, not that it is so. Attribution is not endorsement and not disavowal. The source must actually say what is attributed to it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A102 | account | sense s1: report or explanation

**Common meaning:** A description or explanation of an event or experience; to explain or answer for.

**Example:** She gave a clear account of what she saw.

**RRGM bridge:** A telling offered as answerable: someone stands behind it, and it can be compared with other accounts and with the record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A telling that sets out what happened or why, offered by someone who can be asked about it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A narrative or explanatory text with a teller, a sequence, and claims of fact.
- [REL] Being called to account: required to explain conduct to someone with standing to ask.
- [COG] A theory or explanation of a phenomenon (an account of memory).

**Count condition:** A teller, a subject matter, and stated claims are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An account is not the event. Two honest accounts can differ. To account for something (explain it) is not to justify it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A102 | account | sense s2: ledger

**Common meaning:** A record of money received and paid; an arrangement with a bank or business to hold funds or extend credit.

**Example:** He opened a savings account.

**RRGM bridge:** A running record between named parties in which every change is written and the balance is the net of what has been written.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A running record of value owed or held between identified parties.

**Active domains:** [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A ledger unit with debits and credits; a customer relationship carrying a balance.

**Count condition:** The parties, the unit of value, and an ordered record of entries exist.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The balance shows the net and hides the path. An account is the record of value and is distinct from the value itself.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A102 | account | sense s3: user identity record

**Common meaning:** An arrangement giving a person or process personalised access to a computer system or online service.

**Example:** Log in to your account to see your orders.

**RRGM bridge:** The system-side anchor of identity: a persistent record to which credentials, permissions, and state are attached.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A persistent record by which a system recognises a principal and attaches permissions and state to it.

**Active domains:** [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] An identifier with credentials, a privilege set, and stored settings or history.

**Count condition:** A unique identifier, a means of authentication, and associated permissions exist in the system.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An account is not the person: one person may hold many, and one account may be used by many. Control of the credentials is not proof of identity.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A103 | accountability

**Common meaning:** The fact or condition of being required to answer for one's actions to someone entitled to ask.

**Example:** The reform increased the accountability of ministers to parliament.

**RRGM bridge:** Needs three links together: a record that ties the act to an actor, a party with standing to ask, and a route by which the answer has consequences.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A standing obligation to answer for conduct to a party with standing, with consequences attached to the answer.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Answerability of office-holders, professionals, and institutions to those they serve or report to.
- [DIG] The property that actions in a system can be traced to an identity through logs or signatures and so answered for.

**Count condition:** The actor, the party owed the answer, the matter, and a consequence route are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not responsibility (the duty to do the thing), not transparency (visibility with no consequence), not blame (an attitude), not liability (a legal exposure).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A409 | accountability-failure

**Common meaning:** A breakdown in which conduct that should be answered for is not, because a needed link is missing.

**Example:** The inquiry found an accountability-failure: no one had authority to review the contractor.

**RRGM bridge:** Located at a missing link, so the report should say which one: no record, no party with standing, or no consequence.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Conduct owed an answer goes unanswered because the record, the standing party, or the consequence route is absent or broken.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Oversight bodies without powers, diffused responsibility, or reports that no one must act on.
- [DIG] Actions that cannot be attributed (shared credentials, missing logs) or attributed actions that no process reviews.

**Count condition:** An act that was owed an answer is identified, together with the specific link that failed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Distinct from the original harm. Distinct from an open refusal to answer, which is visible and can itself be answered for. The failure can be structural with no bad intent anywhere in it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A294 | accountability-loop

**Common meaning:** A closed cycle in which actions are recorded, reviewed by a party with standing, and corrected, with the correction reaching future action.

**Example:** Incident reviews close the accountability-loop only when their findings change the procedure.

**RRGM bridge:** A return structure: the actor comes back to the same function carrying a changed record. If nothing comes back, it is a report line and not a loop.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A cycle of act, record, review, and consequence in which the consequence reaches the actor's later conduct.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Audit and review arrangements whose findings bind the reviewed party.
- [DIG] Logging, evaluation, and correction pipelines in which evaluation results alter subsequent system behaviour.

**Count condition:** All four links are present and at least one traceable case shows a correction arriving back at the actor.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Reporting that no one acts on is open, not a loop. A loop is not punishment; correction is the consequence that closes it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A104 | accountable

**Common meaning:** Required or expected to justify actions or decisions; answerable.

**Example:** The board is accountable to shareholders.

**RRGM bridge:** A three-place predicate: who answers, to whom, for what. With any place empty the word does no work.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A named party is answerable to a named other for a named matter.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Holding a role in which one must explain and bear consequences for outcomes.
- [DIG] Of an entity whose actions are bound to its identity by record so that it can be asked about them. For a DI the binding runs through the record that carries its continuity.

**Count condition:** Who, to whom, and for what are all specified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Accountable is not responsible: one party may do the work while another answers for it. Being accountable does not imply being at fault. The clause about DI in the [DIG] line is [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; DI clause [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A257 | accounting

**Common meaning:** The systematic recording, reporting, and analysis of financial transactions; a detailed report rendered.

**Example:** She studied accounting before joining the firm.

**RRGM bridge:** Record-keeping under a reconciliation rule: every flow is written twice or balanced against a total, so that an unrecorded change shows up as a mismatch.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Systematic recording of flows such that totals reconcile under a stated rule.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [DIG], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Bookkeeping and financial reporting under standards that fix recognition and measurement.
- [DIG] Tracking of resource use per identity, as in authentication, authorisation, and accounting.
- [FOR] An identity that must balance by definition, used to check that nothing was created or lost in the books.
- [REL] To give an accounting: to render a full explanation of conduct or of things held in trust.

**Count condition:** A defined unit, a set of recorded entries, and a rule under which they must balance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Books that balance are consistent, which is weaker than true. Accounting profit is defined by convention and is not cash.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0001 | accretion

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** Gradual growth by the addition of matter or layers from outside.

**Example:** The planet grew by accretion of dust and ice.

**RRGM bridge:** Growth supplied from outside across a boundary; the receiver, the source, and the capture process each need naming.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Increase of a body by external material being added to it and retained.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Infall of gas or solids onto a star, planet, or compact object under gravity; build-up of sediment, ice, or crustal blocks onto a margin or surface.
- [ECO] Gradual increase recognised as belonging to an owner: land added by a river's deposit, or the scheduled rise of a discounted bond toward face value.

**Count condition:** A receiver with a boundary and a net gain attributable to material or value arriving from outside.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Infall is not accretion unless it is retained. Legal accretion is slow; a sudden shift of land is treated under a different rule. The discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: Luna illustrative draft of accretion was read in Build Manual v1.0 section 8 before this entry was written · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0002 | accretion disk

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A rotating disk of gas and dust around a massive body, from which material spirals inward onto that body.

**Example:** X-rays from the binary come from the hot inner accretion disk.

**RRGM bridge:** The structure that mediates the growth: matter must shed angular momentum to cross from orbit to capture, and the disk is where that happens.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A flattened orbiting flow that delivers matter inward to a central object while transporting angular momentum outward.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Found around young stars, white dwarfs, neutron stars, and black holes; internal stresses heat the gas, which radiates.

**Count condition:** An orbiting disk with evidence of net inward mass flow onto the central object.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every disk accretes: a debris disk around a mature star does not. The disk is the structure; accretion is the process.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A082 | accumulate

**Common meaning:** To gather or build up gradually over time.

**Example:** Dust accumulated on the shelves.

**RRGM bridge:** Requires a store that persists between additions. Where nothing is retained across a boundary, nothing accumulates across it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A quantity increases through successive additions that are retained.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Build-up of matter, charge, or heat where inflow exceeds outflow.
- [BIO] Retention of a substance in tissue faster than it is cleared.
- [ECO] Build-up of capital, interest, or debt across periods.
- [DIG] A running value held in state and updated at each step, as an accumulator does.

**Count condition:** A persisting store and at least two retained additions are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not accrete (addition onto a body from outside), not aggregate (grouping of units with no time implied). Many additions with matching losses do not accumulate.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A228 | accumulated

**Common meaning:** Built up to a total over time.

**Example:** The accumulated snowfall reached two metres.

**RRGM bridge:** A net record at a reference time: it holds the sum and drops the order and timing of what made it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The total retained so far from earlier additions, taken at a reference time.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A balance carried forward, such as accumulated depreciation or accumulated losses.
- [PHY] A dose, deposit, or strain summed over an exposure period.

**Count condition:** A reference time and a total attributable to earlier retained additions.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The total hides the path: equal accumulated values can come from different histories. Accumulated describes the result; accumulate describes the doing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A227 | accumulation

**Common meaning:** The process of gradually gathering or building up; a mass that has built up.

**Example:** An accumulation of leaves blocked the drain.

**RRGM bridge:** Names either the building-up or the heap. Which one is meant has to be clear before a rate or an amount can be attached.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The process of build-up through retained additions, or the mass that results.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO], [BIO], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Net gain of snow, sediment, or charge in a region.
- [ECO] Reinvestment of surplus to enlarge a stock of capital.
- [BIO] Rise in the concentration of a substance up a food chain or in an organ.
- [FOR] An accumulation point of a set: a point every neighbourhood of which contains other points of the set.

**Count condition:** A store, an inflow, and net retention are evidenced; for the formal sense, the neighbourhood condition is met.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The formal sense involves no growth over time. Accumulation is general; accretion is growth of a body by outside material.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A153 | accuracy

**Common meaning:** The degree to which a measurement, calculation, or statement is close to the true or correct value.

**Example:** The clock keeps time to an accuracy of one second a year.

**RRGM bridge:** A comparison against a reference. Without a reference value there is nothing for accuracy to be measured from, and the honest status is unknown.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The degree of closeness between a result and the true or reference value.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Agreement of a measurement with the accepted value, separate from the spread of repeated readings.
- [DIG] The share of outputs that match labelled answers; the closeness of a computed number to the exact one.
- [COG] The match between a memory or judgement and what occurred.
- [LIN] Fidelity of a quotation or translation to its source.

**Count condition:** A result, a reference value, and a measure of their distance are available.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not precision, which is agreement among repeated results. A high share of correct outputs can mislead when classes are unequal. Consistency does not show accuracy.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10
