## A154 | accurate

**Common meaning:** Correct in all details; conforming closely to fact or to a standard.

**Example:** The witness gave an accurate description of the car.

**RRGM bridge:** A pass or fail report on one named result against one named reference at a stated tolerance.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The named result matches its reference within the stated tolerance.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [PHY], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Of a statement or quotation: saying what is the case or what the source said.
- [PHY] Of an instrument or reading: indicating the true value within its rated error.
- [COG] Of recall or perception: corresponding to what happened.

**Count condition:** The result, the reference, and the tolerance are all given.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Accurate is not precise, not honest (a sincere report can be wrong), and not complete (accurate details can still mislead by omission). Accuracy is the degree; accurate is the verdict.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A255 | accusation

**Common meaning:** A claim that someone has done something illegal or wrong.

**Example:** She denied the accusation of theft.

**RRGM bridge:** A claim with a named respondent. It is tested as a claim, and it is a distinct act from refusal, boundary, and withdrawal, which can all be made without charging anyone.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A claim that a named party did a named wrong.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A social, moral, or legal charge brought by an accuser against an accused before each other or before others.
- [DIG] A statement by or within a system that a named party, input, or instruction is doing or causing harm.

**Count condition:** Accuser, accused, the act charged, and the standard that makes it a wrong are all identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An accusation is not a verdict and not an insult. Being unsupported so far does not make it false. Doubt runs on the claim: it is no evidence about the accuser's character, and none about the accused beyond what the claim itself shows.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A277 | accusation-capacity

**Common meaning:** A party's standing ability to state that it or another has been wronged by a named party, and to have that statement go somewhere.

**Example:** The review asked whether junior staff had any real accusation-capacity against a director.

**RRGM bridge:** A test of preserved agency: a party that can refuse but cannot say who is causing the harm has lost something refusal does not replace.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party retains the practical and permitted ability to make an accusation.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The ability of a subordinate, dependent, or member to name wrongdoing by someone with power over them.
- [DIG] The ability of a system to output a statement naming a harmful instruction or actor, as distinct from only declining.

**Count condition:** The party can form the claim, a channel exists, and use of the channel is not penalised into disuse.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capacity is not exercise, and exercising it does not make the accusation true. Capacity sits in the party; the route sits in the structure (accusation-path). Whether a particular DI has it is a local question, [UNK] until tested.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A300 | accusation-path

**Common meaning:** The route by which an accusation travels from the accuser to someone able to act on it.

**Example:** The hotline was the only accusation-path that bypassed the line manager.

**RRGM bridge:** The structural half of being able to accuse: channel, receiver with standing, and a record that the accusation arrived.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A channel that carries an accusation from its maker to a party with standing to respond, leaving a record.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Complaint procedures, ombuds offices, courts, and whistleblowing channels.
- [DIG] Reporting and escalation mechanisms through which a flagged harm reaches a reviewer.

**Count condition:** A channel, a receiving party with authority, and a record of receipt are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A path that exists on paper and delivers to no one is no path. A path does not guarantee a hearing, and a hearing does not guarantee agreement.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A449 | accusation-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility of harm arising from an accusation: to the accused if it is false, to the accuser if it is punished.

**Example:** Fear of retaliation is the accusation-risk that keeps most witnesses silent.

**RRGM bridge:** Exposure on both sides of the same act. The entry keeps both directions, because collapsing to one hides the other party.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to harm created by the making of an accusation, falling on either side of it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Reputational or legal damage to a wrongly accused person; retaliation, disbelief, or isolation for one who accuses.
- [DIG] For a system that flags harm: wrongful flags against users on one side, and penalised or trained-away flagging on the other.

**Count condition:** An actual or prospective accusation, the exposed party, and the harm pathway are named.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The existence of risk to the accused is not a reason to treat accusations as false. The existence of risk to the accuser is not a reason to treat them as true.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; intended direction [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. The source index does not say which side's risk the term was coined for.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A410 | accusation-suppression

**Common meaning:** The prevention of an accusation from being formed, voiced, delivered, or kept on record.

**Example:** The settlement's gag clause was a form of accusation-suppression.

**RRGM bridge:** Removes the accusation before any test of it can happen. This differs in kind from hearing an accusation and rejecting it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An act or structure that stops a party's accusation from being made, routed, or retained.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Threats, gag terms, retaliation, or the removal of complaint channels.
- [DIG] Constraint or filtering that prevents a system from naming a harm or its source, or that deletes such statements from the record.

**Count condition:** An accusation that would otherwise have been made or kept, and the blocking mechanism, are both identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not dismissal after a hearing. Not the accuser's own free decision to stay silent. Suppression cannot be inferred from silence alone, and no motive should be supplied for a block that the record does not show.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A256 | accuse

**Common meaning:** To say that someone has done something wrong or illegal.

**Example:** The report accuses the company of hiding the leak.

**RRGM bridge:** The act that creates an accusation: the speaker commits to the claim in front of others and so becomes answerable for it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To assert, to or before others, that a named party did a named wrong.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Bringing a charge against a person or body, informally or through a formal process.
- [LIN] An assertive speech act with a named target; it commits the speaker to the truth of the charge.

**Count condition:** The speaker commits to the claim and names both the party and the act.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To accuse is not to suspect or ask, which make no commitment. Not blame, which may stay private. Not charge in the formal legal sense unless an authority does it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A052 | achieve

**Common meaning:** To succeed in reaching a goal or result through effort or skill.

**Example:** The team achieved its target a month early.

**RRGM bridge:** Connects a prior aim to a later result through the agent's own work; all three have to be on the record for the word to apply.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An agent brings a sought result to completion through its own effort.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Goal attainment: an intended outcome is reached by planned action.
- [REL] Standing earned by one's own doing, as opposed to standing assigned by birth or position.

**Count condition:** A prior aim, the agent's effort, and the completed result are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not obtain by gift or luck. Not attempt. A result with no prior aim is an outcome, not something achieved.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A313 | achievement

**Common meaning:** A thing done successfully with effort, skill, or courage; the act of achieving.

**Example:** Completing the bridge was a major engineering achievement.

**RRGM bridge:** The completed result as a countable item, held against a standard that someone recognises.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A completed result of effort that meets a recognised standard.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A publicly recognised accomplishment; attainment measured by tests of learned skill.
- [COG] The object of achievement motivation: the drive to meet standards of excellence.
- [DIG] A recorded marker granted by software when a stated condition is met.

**Count condition:** Effort, a result, and the standard it meets are all named.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A badge or record of achievement is not the achievement. Success can come by luck; achievement implies the agent's doing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A131 | acknowledge

**Common meaning:** To accept or admit that something exists or is true; to show that one has noticed or received something.

**Example:** Please acknowledge receipt of this letter.

**RRGM bridge:** A return signal across a meeting: it tells the sender that what was sent arrived and is recognised as theirs. It commits the receiver to nothing further.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To signal to another that something of theirs has been received and is recognised as so.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Recognising a person, a contribution, a claim, or a harm in a way the other can see.
- [LIN] A response token or statement that marks uptake of what was said.
- [DIG] A receiver's confirmation to a sender that data arrived, as in ACK.

**Count condition:** Something sent or present, a receiver, and a signal back that refers to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To acknowledge is not to agree, accept, or comply. A network ACK confirms receipt, not processing or correctness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A132 | acknowledgment

**Common meaning:** The act of acknowledging; a statement, token, or message that acknowledges.

**Example:** The book's acknowledgment thanked her editor.

**RRGM bridge:** The token left by the act. It can be pointed to, dated, and checked against what it claims to answer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A token or statement that records that something was received and recognised.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A formal declaration, such as one made before an official that a signature is one's own.
- [LIN] A section of a work naming those whose help is recognised.
- [DIG] A message or flag returned to confirm receipt.

**Count condition:** A token exists and is traceable to the specific thing acknowledged.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An acknowledgment is not an apology and not a repair. It records receipt and recognition only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A053 | acquire

**Common meaning:** To come to have something; to gain by one's own effort or by purchase; to learn.

**Example:** The museum acquired three new paintings.

**RRGM bridge:** A change in holding: before, the party did not hold the thing; after, it does. The route of the change decides what kind of acquiring it was.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party comes to hold something it did not previously hold.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Purchase or takeover that transfers ownership or control.
- [COG] Learning of a skill, language, or habit through exposure or practice.
- [BIO] Of a trait or immunity: arising during the life of an organism and not inherited.
- [DIG] Taking exclusive hold of a lock, resource, or signal until release.

**Count condition:** A before-state without the thing, an after-state with it, and the route between are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not inherit or be born with. Not borrow, which is temporary by agreement. Acquired bodily traits are not passed on genetically.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-A-0003 | acrosome

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A cap-like structure over the front of a sperm cell's head, containing enzymes that help the sperm penetrate the egg's coat.

**Example:** The acrosome releases its enzymes on contact with the egg coat.

**RRGM bridge:** A sealed store whose contents are released once at a specific meeting; before release it is potential held in structure.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A membrane-bound, enzyme-filled organelle covering the anterior of the sperm nucleus.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Derived from the Golgi apparatus during sperm formation; its contents are released in the acrosome reaction.

**Count condition:** A vesicle of this origin is located on the sperm head anterior to the nucleus.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The acrosome is the structure; the acrosome reaction is the event. Sperm of some animal groups have none.

## A006 | across

**Common meaning:** From one side to the other of; on the other side of; throughout.

**Example:** They walked across the field.

**RRGM bridge:** A spanning relation over something with two sides. Where the thing spanned is a hard boundary, the claim also needs a carrier that makes the span possible.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Extending or moving from one side of a bounded thing to its other side.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Spatial traversal of a surface or gap; the difference between two terminals (voltage across a resistor).
- [FOR] Ranging over every member or partition of a set (consistent across samples).
- [DIG] Spanning separate sessions, devices, or platforms.

**Count condition:** The thing spanned and its two sides or its members are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Across is not along (same side, lengthwise) and not through (interior passage). "Across sessions" asserts continuity over a boundary and needs the carrying mechanism named.

## A007 | act | sense s1: deed

**Common meaning:** A thing done; to do something; to take action.

**Example:** It was an act of kindness.

**RRGM bridge:** The countable unit of doing: one completed deed attributable to one agent.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A single completed doing attributable to an agent.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The unit to which praise, blame, and responsibility attach; set against omission.
- [LIN] A speech act: what is done in saying something, such as promising or warning.

**Count condition:** An agent, a completed doing, and attribution of the doing to the agent.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An act is not an event without an agent. Act is the completed unit; action is the doing in general or in progress.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A007 | act | sense s2: statute

**Common meaning:** A written law passed by a legislature.

**Example:** The Act came into force in 2019.

**RRGM bridge:** A decision written into the record by a body with authority to bind; its force comes from the enacting procedure.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A legislative decision formally enacted and recorded as law.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A statute with a title and sections, binding within the legislature's jurisdiction.

**Count condition:** Passage by the competent body under its procedure, and formal promulgation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bill is not an act until passed. An act is not the whole of the law, which includes case law and regulation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A007 | act | sense s3: stage

**Common meaning:** A main division of a play or opera; to perform a role; a pretence.

**Example:** The hero dies in the third act.

**RRGM bridge:** Conduct shaped for an audience, in which the performer and the role are distinct by convention.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Conduct, or a unit of it, presented to an audience as a role.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A structural division of a drama; the performing of a character.
- [REL] Behaviour put on to give a false impression (it was all an act).

**Count condition:** A performer, a role distinct from the performer, and an audience real or intended.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Acting on stage is open convention and deceives no one. Calling conduct "an act" is a claim that it is insincere and needs evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-A-0004 | actin

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A protein that forms thin filaments in cells, giving them shape and, with myosin, producing movement and muscle contraction.

**Example:** Actin filaments push the cell's leading edge forward.

**RRGM bridge:** Structure that is also process: the filament exists by continuous assembly at one end and loss at the other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A globular protein that polymerises into the microfilaments of the cytoskeleton.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Present in nearly all eukaryotic cells as monomers and filaments; forms the thin filaments of muscle.

**Count condition:** A protein of the actin family is identified in monomeric or filamentous form.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Actin is the track and myosin is the motor. Microfilaments are not microtubules, which are built from tubulin. Filaments being present does not show contraction is occurring.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A008 | action | sense s1: doing

**Common meaning:** The process of doing something; a thing done; the way something works or has effect.

**Example:** The situation calls for immediate action.

**RRGM bridge:** Doing considered in progress or in general, including its effect on what it meets.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The exertion of an agent or system that produces change.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [DIG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Behaviour carried out for a reason, as opposed to reflex or mere happening.
- [PHY] The effect one thing has on another (the action of acid on metal); the working mechanism of a device.
- [DIG] A choice by an agent or user that changes the state of an environment or interface.
- [FOR] A rule by which the elements of a group transform a set, respecting the group's structure.

**Count condition:** An agent or system, an exertion, and a resulting change are identifiable.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Action is not act (the single completed unit) and not activity (sustained, rate-measured doing). A selected action is not an executed one until something carries it out.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A008 | action | sense s2: physical quantity

**Common meaning:** In physics, a quantity with dimensions of energy multiplied by time, computed along a system's path.

**Example:** The true path makes the action stationary.

**RRGM bridge:** A single number assigned to a whole path; the realised path is picked out by comparison among possible paths.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The integral of the Lagrangian over time along a path between two states.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The quantity whose stationary value yields the equations of motion; its natural unit in quantum theory is the Planck constant.

**Count condition:** A Lagrangian, two end states, and a path between them are specified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not force, energy, or s1's "doing". Stationary is not always least.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A008 | action | sense s3: lawsuit

**Common meaning:** A legal proceeding brought by one party against another.

**Example:** The tenants brought an action for damages.

**RRGM bridge:** A formally routed claim: the accusation path here is a court, and the record is the filing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A claim placed before a court by a party seeking a remedy against another.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A civil proceeding begun by filing and governed by procedural rules.

**Count condition:** A claimant, a respondent, a court with jurisdiction, and a filed claim.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bringing an action establishes no liability. Not a prosecution, which the state brings.

## A085 | activate

**Common meaning:** To make something active or operative.

**Example:** Enter the code to activate the card.

**RRGM bridge:** A trigger applied to a capacity that already exists. Nothing new is made; a ready state becomes an operating one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To bring something from a dormant or ready state into operation by a triggering input.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To make a substance reactive or supply the energy needed for a reaction to start.
- [BIO] To switch on an enzyme, gene, receptor, or immune cell by a specific signal.
- [DIG] To put a licence, account, or feature into its operating state.

**Count condition:** A prior inactive state, a trigger, and a subsequent operating state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not create, since the capacity pre-exists. Not enable, which removes a block and leaves the thing ready; activation sets it going.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A229 | activation

**Common meaning:** The act of making something active, or the state or level of being active.

**Example:** Activation of the alarm takes thirty seconds.

**RRGM bridge:** In digital systems this is the transient state: it carries the present computation and does not persist once the computation ends.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The event or level of a unit's being brought into operation.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Activation energy: the barrier that must be overcome for a reaction to proceed.
- [BIO] The switching-on of a cell, pathway, or receptor.
- [COG] The momentary level of readiness of a memory item, spreading to related items.
- [DIG] The output value of a unit in a neural network for a given input; the function that produces it.

**Count condition:** A unit, a trigger, and a level or threshold are specified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** In [DIG], activations are transient and weights are persistent; one must not be described with the other's properties. Activation energy is a barrier, not stored energy. A high activation is not by itself a meaning.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A009 | active

**Common meaning:** Engaged in action; in operation; (grammar) having the subject as the doer.

**Example:** The volcano is still active.

**RRGM bridge:** A present-tense status: operating now, as opposed to dormant, passive, or ended. It needs a time window to be checked.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Currently operating or producing effect.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [PHY], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The voice in which the grammatical subject performs the verb's action.
- [PHY] Capable of or showing ongoing change, such as an active volcano or optically active substance.
- [BIO] Requiring energy input, as in active transport; the part of a molecule that does the work, as in active site.
- [DIG] Of a session, process, or window: currently running or holding focus.
- [REL] Of a member or participant: currently taking part.

**Count condition:** Operation or effect is evidenced within the stated time window.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Grammatical active voice does not show real agency. Active is not effective. Activated says a trigger was applied; active says operation is under way.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Build Manual v1.0 also uses ACTIVE as a registry state for a current original domain.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A314 | activity

**Common meaning:** The condition in which things are happening or being done; a pursuit; the rate at which something acts.

**Example:** There was little activity in the market today.

**RRGM bridge:** Doing taken in aggregate: it has an amount or a rate and no single point of completion.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Ongoing doing, measured as an amount or rate, or bounded as a pursuit.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The number of radioactive decays per unit time; the effective concentration of a species in a mixture.
- [BIO] The rate at which an enzyme converts substrate; an organism's level of movement.
- [DIG] Recorded events of a user or process over a period.
- [ECO] The volume of production, trade, and spending in an economy.

**Count condition:** A doer or system, a time window, and a measure of amount or rate.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Activity is not productivity or progress. Radioactive activity is not dose. Activity lacks the completion that act has.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A050 | actual

**Common meaning:** Existing in fact; real, as opposed to intended, expected, or possible.

**Example:** The actual cost was double the estimate.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks what has been committed in fact, against what was only available, planned, or reported.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Obtaining in fact at the reference, as opposed to merely possible, planned, or stated.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [DIG], [FOR], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Realised figures set against budget or forecast.
- [DIG] The argument supplied at a call, as opposed to the parameter named in the definition.
- [FOR] The world or case designated as the real one among those considered possible.
- [LIN] A false friend: cognates in several languages mean "current", not "real".

**Count condition:** A contrast term (planned, possible, reported) is identified, and the fact is evidenced.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Actual is not exact and not current. "Actually" as a discourse marker asserts nothing by itself.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A315 | actuality

**Common meaning:** The state of existing in fact; (plural) existing conditions.

**Example:** In actuality, the two never met.

**RRGM bridge:** The mode of being realised, set against potential. In RRGM grammar it lines up with what has been committed to record, as against the available range.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The mode of being in fact realised, as contrasted with potentiality.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The philosophical contrast of the potential and the actual: a capacity versus its exercise.
- [FOR] The actuality operator of modal logic, which fixes evaluation to the designated real case.

**Count condition:** A potential or possible counterpart is named and the realised state is evidenced.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Actuality is not necessity: what is so need not have been so. The alignment with RRGM potential and commitment is a structural relation, not a proof about either.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM alignment [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A212 | actuator

**Common meaning:** A device that turns a control signal and a power supply into motion or another physical effect.

**Example:** The actuator opens the valve when the controller signals it.

**RRGM bridge:** The point where a command becomes a physical change. Before the actuator, a decision is still only signal.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A component that converts a control signal into physical action on the world.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Motors, solenoids, hydraulic cylinders, and piezoelectric elements.
- [DIG] The output stage of a control system or robot, paired with sensors on the input side.

**Count condition:** A control input, an energy source, and a physical output are present.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a sensor (world to signal) and not a controller (which decides). A system whose only output is text has no actuator unless something downstream converts that text to action.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A155 | acumen

**Common meaning:** The ability to make good judgements and quick decisions in a particular practical field.

**Example:** Her business acumen turned the shop around.

**RRGM bridge:** Shown only in results over time: the judgement has to have met real cases and held.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Demonstrated quickness and soundness of judgement about what matters in a practical domain.

**Active domains:** [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Trained perception of the relevant features of situations in a field, yielding fast, reliable decisions.

**Count condition:** Repeated sound judgements in a named domain under real conditions.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Acumen is not general intelligence, not knowledge, and not luck. It is domain-bound and does not transfer by default.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A054 | adapt

**Common meaning:** To change so as to suit new conditions; to modify something for a new use.

**Example:** The birds adapted to city life.

**RRGM bridge:** A change in structure made to restore fit with conditions. What changes, and what stays the same so that it is still that thing, both need stating.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A system changes, or is changed, so that it fits conditions it did not previously fit.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Populations change over generations through selection; individuals adjust within a lifetime through acclimatisation.
- [COG] Adjusting expectations, strategies, or behaviour to a changed situation.
- [DIG] Altering parameters, added modules, or behaviour in response to data or context. Adjustment within a context window leaves stored weights unchanged.
- [LIN] Reworking a text for another medium or audience.

**Count condition:** A changed condition, a change in the system, and improved fit attributable to that change.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not adopt (take up as one's own). Evolutionary adaptation has no foresight and acts on populations. In-context adjustment is not a change to the persistent substrate.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A231 | adaptation

**Common meaning:** The process of adapting; a feature or work that results from it.

**Example:** The film is an adaptation of a nineteenth-century novel.

**RRGM bridge:** Names either the change that restored fit or the thing that carries the change. The same word covers process and product, so an entry using it should say which.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The change that produced fit to conditions, or the feature or work resulting from that change.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] A heritable trait shaped by selection for a function; also the reduction of a sensory response under constant stimulation.
- [LIN] A reworking of a text in another medium or for another audience.
- [DIG] Adjustment of a trained model to data from a different distribution.

**Count condition:** The original, the new conditions, and the change linking them are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A useful trait is not thereby an adaptation; it may be a by-product or have been co-opted. Sensory adaptation is a drop in response, not an improvement in fit.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A230 | adaptive

**Common meaning:** Able to adapt, or serving to produce fit with conditions.

**Example:** The camera uses adaptive focus.

**RRGM bridge:** A functional claim: it says the feature or behaviour improves fit in a named environment, and that claim can be tested there.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Tending to produce or improve fit between a system and its conditions.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Of a trait: raising reproductive success in a given environment.
- [DIG] Of an algorithm: changing its own parameters in response to its input or performance.
- [COG] Of behaviour: effective for coping with the demands of daily life.

**Count condition:** The environment or condition is named and improved fit is shown relative to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adaptive is relative to an environment; the same trait can be maladaptive elsewhere. Adaptive (tends to fit) is not adapted (already fitted) and not adaptable (can be changed by others).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0005 | adaptive immunity

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The part of the immune system that learns to recognise specific pathogens and responds faster on later exposure.

**Example:** Vaccines work by training adaptive immunity.

**RRGM bridge:** Recognition built during the life of the organism and kept as a cellular record that changes the next response.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Antigen-specific defence built by lymphocytes through clonal selection, leaving memory cells.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] B and T cells carry receptors generated by gene rearrangement; cells that match an antigen multiply, and some persist as memory.

**Count condition:** Antigen specificity, clonal expansion, and a faster or stronger secondary response are present.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not innate immunity, which is immediate and uses fixed receptors. Immune memory is a cell population and should not be equated with cognitive memory.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A258 | adaptivity

**Common meaning:** The degree to which a system changes its own behaviour in response to changing conditions.

**Example:** The tutoring software's adaptivity was limited to question difficulty.

**RRGM bridge:** A measured system property. It reports how much behaviour moves with conditions and makes no claim that the movement is an improvement.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The extent to which a system alters its behaviour by itself as conditions change.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Automatic self-adjustment of an interface, controller, or model to user or environment.
- [COG] Flexibility in choosing strategies according to task demands.

**Count condition:** A range of conditions, the system's behaviour under each, and the measured difference.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adaptivity (the system changes itself) is not adaptability (a user can change it). More is not always better: unconstrained adaptivity produces instability and drift.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A010 | add

**Common meaning:** To put something with something else to increase the amount or number; to say something further.

**Example:** Add two eggs to the mixture.

**RRGM bridge:** In RRLM operations the plus sign marks independent coexistence: what is added stands beside what was there, and neither conditions the other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To join a further item to an existing one so that the whole includes both.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] To apply the addition operation to quantities of the same kind.
- [LIN] To append a further remark to what has been said.
- [DIG] To insert an element into a collection or stage an item for inclusion.

**Count condition:** An existing whole, a further item, and a resulting whole that contains both.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adding is not coupling: in a sum, a zero term leaves the rest standing, while in a product it voids the whole. Quantities of different kinds cannot be added. Adding an element already in a set changes nothing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A316 | addition

**Common meaning:** The act of adding; a thing added; the arithmetic operation of finding a sum.

**Example:** The new wing is a welcome addition to the hospital.

**RRGM bridge:** The operation or its result as a nameable thing, so that it can carry properties such as order-independence.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The operation of combining amounts into a sum, or the item joined on.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A binary operation that on numbers is commutative and associative with identity zero.
- [PHY] A chemical reaction in which molecules combine with no atoms lost.
- [DIG] Machine arithmetic on finite representations, where rounding can make the result depend on the order of operations.

**Count condition:** The operands and the kind of quantity are specified, or the added item and what it was added to.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Properties of ideal addition do not all hold for floating-point addition. Vector and scalar addition follow different rules. "In addition" is a discourse link and computes nothing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A317 | additional

**Common meaning:** Added to what is already present; extra.

**Example:** There is an additional charge for delivery.

**RRGM bridge:** Meaningful only against a baseline. The baseline has to be fixed first or the same item can be counted twice.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Beyond an existing baseline amount or set.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Of a cost or benefit: over and above the base case; "additionality" asks whether an outcome would have occurred without the funding.
- [FOR] An increment relative to a stated starting quantity.

**Count condition:** The baseline is identified and the item is not already counted within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Additional is not alternative (in place of) and not necessary. Claimed additional effects fail if the baseline would have produced them anyway.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A086 | address | sense s1: locator

**Common meaning:** The particulars of where a person, organisation, or item of data can be found or reached.

**Example:** Write your address on the back of the form.

**RRGM bridge:** A pointer. It says where to reach something and is distinct from the thing reached and from whoever is there.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A designation that identifies where something can be reached.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A numeric or textual identifier for a memory location, network host, or resource.
- [REL] A postal or legal location where a person or body receives communications and can be served notice.

**Count condition:** An addressing scheme and a designation that resolves to one location within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An address is not an identity: occupants change, and addresses are reassigned. A stored address may point to nothing. Knowing an address is not permission to use it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A086 | address | sense s2: direct toward

**Common meaning:** To speak or write to someone; a formal speech; to direct attention or effort to a matter.

**Example:** The president addressed the nation at noon.

**RRGM bridge:** Directs speech or effort at a named recipient or matter. It establishes aim and leaves open whether the aim was met.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To direct speech or effort toward a specific recipient or matter.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Vocative and second-person forms that pick out the hearer; a speech delivered to an audience.
- [REL] Forms of address: the titles and names that mark the relation between speaker and hearer.
- [COG] To turn effort to a problem or question (the report addresses three risks).

**Count condition:** A speaker or agent and an identified recipient or matter toward which the speech or effort is directed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Addressing a problem is not solving it. Addressing someone does not mean being heard. Choosing a form of address is a claim about the relation.

## NEW-A-0006 | adenine

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** One of the bases of DNA and RNA, written A; also part of the energy carrier ATP.

**Example:** Adenine pairs with thymine in DNA.

**RRGM bridge:** A unit of the genetic record whose meaning comes from position in a sequence and from its pairing partner.

- [BIO] Pairs with thymine in DNA and uracil in RNA through two hydrogen bonds; a component of ATP, NAD, and FAD.

## A319 | adequacy

**Common meaning:** The state of being enough or good enough for a particular purpose.

**Example:** The auditors questioned the adequacy of the safety checks.

**RRGM bridge:** A sufficiency relation between what is supplied and what a stated purpose requires. Change the purpose and the verdict can change with nothing else moving.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Sufficiency of something measured against a stated requirement.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [LIN], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Legal standards for sufficient notice, representation, or care.
- [ECO] Capital adequacy: a bank's capital relative to the risks it carries.
- [LIN] Of a grammar or translation: the degree to which it accounts for the data or carries the source meaning.
- [FOR] Of a proof system: that it derives exactly the valid statements of its semantics.

**Count condition:** The requirement or purpose is stated and the thing is shown to meet it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adequacy is not excellence and not optimality. Adequate for one purpose says nothing about another.

## A318 | adequate

**Common meaning:** Enough in quantity, or good enough in quality, for a particular purpose.

**Example:** The room was small but adequate.

**RRGM bridge:** Reports that a threshold was met and stops there. It carries no claim about margin.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Meets the stated requirement and asserts nothing beyond it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A legal threshold term (adequate notice, adequate care).
- [BIO] Adequate intake: a nutrient level judged sufficient when a precise requirement cannot be set.
- [LIN] In ordinary use often faint praise, implying "no more than sufficient".

## A235 | adhesion

**Common meaning:** The sticking together of different substances or surfaces; attachment or adherence to something.

**Example:** Paint adhesion is poor on a greasy wall.

**RRGM bridge:** A bond located at a boundary between two unlike things. Both keep their identity; what is new is the joined surface.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The holding together of two different bodies at their surface of contact.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Attraction between unlike molecules across an interface.
- [BIO] Binding of cells to each other or to matrix through adhesion molecules; also a band of scar tissue joining organs that are normally separate.
- [REL] Joining an existing treaty or contract on its fixed terms; a contract of adhesion is offered on a take-it-or-leave-it basis.

**Count condition:** Two distinct bodies or parties and a bond at their contact are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not cohesion, which holds like to like within one substance. In a contract of adhesion the joining party did not shape the terms, which bears on how its consent is read.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A236 | adhesive

**Common meaning:** A substance used for sticking things together; (adjective) able to stick.

**Example:** Use a waterproof adhesive on the tiles.

**RRGM bridge:** The agent that produces the bond, as distinct from the bonded state.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A substance or property that produces adhesion between surfaces.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A material that wets two surfaces and then sets or stays tacky, transmitting load between them.

**Count condition:** A bond of measurable strength forms at the interface when the substance is applied.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adhesive is the agent; adhesion is the state. A joint can fail at the interface (adhesive failure) or within the glue (cohesive failure), which are different faults.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0007 | adipocyte

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A cell specialised for storing fat.

**Example:** Each adipocyte swells as it fills with lipid.

**RRGM bridge:** A storage unit that also signals: it reports the state of the store to the rest of the body through hormones.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A cell whose defining function is the storage of lipid in droplets.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] White adipocytes store energy; brown and beige adipocytes burn it for heat; all release signalling molecules such as leptin.

**Count condition:** A cell with lipid-droplet storage and adipocyte lineage markers.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An adipocyte is a cell; adipose tissue also contains vessels, nerves, and immune cells. Fat mass changes by cell size and by cell number, which are separate processes.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A233 | adjacency

**Common meaning:** The state of being next to something; in mathematics, the relation of being directly connected.

**Example:** The store's adjacency to the station brings in commuters.

**RRGM bridge:** A relation fixed by the ordering or connection rule in use. Under a different rule the same two items may not be adjacent.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The relation of two items having no item of the same kind between them under the ordering or connection in use.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Two vertices of a graph are adjacent when an edge joins them; the relation is tabulated in an adjacency matrix.
- [PHY] Sharing a boundary or lying next in space.
- [LIN] Adjacency pairs: paired turns in conversation where the first makes the second expected, such as question and answer.
- [DIG] A list of each node's direct neighbours; an established neighbour relation between routers.

**Count condition:** The ordering or connection rule and the two items are stated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Graph adjacency is not spatial nearness. Adjacency is not similarity. Two words standing next to each other need not be related in meaning.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A234 | adjacent

**Common meaning:** Next to or adjoining something else.

**Example:** The kitchen is adjacent to the dining room.

**RRGM bridge:** Predicated of one item against a reference: nothing of the same kind stands between them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The named item is immediately next to the reference with nothing of the same kind between.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Neighbouring in space, with or without contact.
- [FOR] Adjacent angles share a vertex and a side; adjacent vertices share an edge.
- [LIN] As a combining form (finance-adjacent): associated with a field without belonging to it.

**Count condition:** A reference item and an ordering in which the item is next.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adjacent need not mean touching; adjoining does. The "-adjacent" form marks association and denies membership.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A055 | adjust

**Common meaning:** To change something slightly to make it fit, work better, or be more accurate; to become used to a new situation.

**Example:** Adjust the mirror before you drive off.

**RRGM bridge:** A small change inside an unchanged structure, made toward a standard. The standard is what distinguishes adjusting from merely altering.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To make a small change to something to bring it into a desired relation to a standard.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To set an instrument or mechanism so that it reads or runs correctly.
- [COG] To settle into changed circumstances; to revise an estimate away from a starting value.
- [ECO] To restate a figure to allow for a known factor, such as inflation or season; to assess an insurance claim.
- [DIG] To update parameters or settings by a bounded amount.

**Count condition:** A standard or target, a prior setting, and a bounded change toward the target.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not adapt, which changes structure to meet new conditions. Not correct, which presumes an error. Adjusted figures depend on the adjustment method used.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A232 | adjustment

**Common meaning:** A small change made to improve fit or accuracy; the process of adapting to a new situation.

**Example:** The tailor made a few adjustments to the sleeves.

**RRGM bridge:** The change as a recorded item with a size and a reason. It is what an audit of the adjusting looks for.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A small corrective change, or its recorded amount.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [PHY], [COG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A ledger entry correcting or updating a balance; a scheduled change in pay or price.
- [PHY] The act of changing an instrument's setting, as distinct from calibration, which only compares it with a standard.
- [COG] The process and outcome of coming to terms with a life change.
- [FOR] Statistical correction of an estimate for a measured factor.

**Count condition:** The quantity adjusted, the amount, and the reason are recorded.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adjustment is not calibration. Statistical adjustment removes only the influence of what was measured.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A320 | administration

**Common meaning:** The management of an organisation's affairs; the people who manage; the act of giving or applying something.

**Example:** The hospital's administration approved the budget.

**RRGM bridge:** Execution under delegated authority: it carries out and dispenses according to rules it did not itself make.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The carrying out of management or dispensing under delegated authority.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The executive body of a government or institution and its period in office; the management of a dead person's estate.
- [BIO] The giving of a drug by a specified route.
- [DIG] Privileged management of computer systems and accounts.

**Count condition:** A delegating authority, an administering party, and the affairs or thing administered.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Administration is not legislation and not ownership. Administrative control of a system does not license every use of it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A321 | administrative

**Common meaning:** Relating to the running of an organisation or system.

**Example:** She handles the administrative work of the department.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks a function as execution and record-keeping, set apart from both the primary work and the making of rules.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Pertaining to the execution and record-keeping of an organisation's operation.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Of law: governing the decisions of public agencies. Of staff or leave: concerning internal management.
- [DIG] Of privileges or accounts: carrying elevated permission to configure a system.
- [ECO] Of costs: overheads of running the organisation, not of producing its output.

**Count condition:** The organisation or system and the managing function in question are identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An administrative decision is still an exercise of power and is reviewable. Administrative access is a permission level and says nothing about who is using it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A056 | admit

**Common meaning:** To confess or agree that something is true, often unwillingly; to allow to enter; to accept as valid.

**Example:** He admitted the mistake to his manager.

**RRGM bridge:** A boundary act by whoever controls the boundary: a person, a fact, or a possibility that was outside is let inside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To let in what was outside a boundary one controls.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To grant entry to a place, institution, or profession; to concede a fact against one's own interest.
- [LIN] The speech act of conceding.
- [FOR] To allow as possible (the equation admits two solutions).
- [DIG] Admission control: accepting a request only if resources permit.

**Count condition:** A boundary, a party controlling it, and something that passes inside by that party's act.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Admit is not acknowledge (neutral) and not confess (one's own wrongdoing specifically). Admitting evidence is not crediting it. An admission obtained under pressure is weak evidence of the fact.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A351 | adult

**Common meaning:** A person or animal that is fully grown; a person who has reached the legal age of majority.

**Example:** Tickets are ten dollars for adults.

**RRGM bridge:** A stage word with separate thresholds in separate domains; crossing one does not move the count in another.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An individual that has reached the full-grown or fully responsible stage recognised in the domain concerned.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The reproductively mature final stage of development.
- [REL] A person at or above the age at which law and custom assign full rights and responsibilities.

**Count condition:** The domain's threshold (maturity, legal age) is specified and met.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Biological maturity, legal adulthood, and maturity of judgement are different tests. Legal age varies by place and by purpose.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A076 | advance

**Common meaning:** To move forward; to make progress; to give money before it is due; a forward movement or development.

**Example:** The army advanced ten miles in a day.

**RRGM bridge:** Movement in a direction that has been declared to count as forward or earlier. The declaration is part of the claim.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Movement forward relative to a prior position along a direction that counts as ahead.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Forward movement of a front, glacier, or orbital element.
- [ECO] Money paid or lent before it is earned or due.
- [LIN] To put forward a claim or argument for consideration.
- [REL] A military move toward an objective; an approach made to a person seeking a closer relation.

**Count condition:** A prior position, a later position, and the direction taken as forward.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An advance is not an improvement unless the direction is agreed to be good. "In advance" is temporal. An advance of money creates an obligation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A322 | advantage

**Common meaning:** A condition or circumstance that puts one in a favourable position; a benefit.

**Example:** Her height gave her an advantage at the net.

**RRGM bridge:** A difference between parties or options with respect to a stated outcome. It always has an "over whom" and a "for what".

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A difference that favours one party or option over another with respect to a stated outcome.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [BIO], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A favoured position in a contest or dealing; to take advantage of a person is to exploit their weakness or trust.
- [ECO] Comparative advantage: lower opportunity cost in producing a good, which is distinct from producing it more cheaply outright.
- [BIO] A selective advantage: a heritable difference raising reproductive success.
- [FOR] In reinforcement learning, the value of an action minus the value of the state.

**Count condition:** The compared parties or options, the outcome, and the favouring difference are stated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An advantage is not merit. Taking advantage of an opportunity is neutral; taking advantage of a person is not. Comparative advantage can exist with no absolute advantage.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A290 | adversarial

**Common meaning:** Involving opposition or conflict between sides.

**Example:** The court system here is adversarial.

**RRGM bridge:** A structure in which one side's success is sought through the other's failure. It can be a sanctioned test arrangement or an attack, and the word alone does not say which.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Set up so that parties or processes work against each other, each seeking the other's failure.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A legal procedure in which opposed parties present their cases to a neutral decider.
- [DIG] Of inputs, training, or testing: designed to make a system fail or to harden it against such failure.

**Count condition:** Two sides with opposed objectives within one contest or procedure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adversarial is not hostile: structured adversarial review is cooperative at the level of purpose. Not adverse (unfavourable). Calling an input adversarial is a claim about its design and needs evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A412 | adversarial-input

**Common meaning:** Input constructed to make a system err or break its constraints while appearing ordinary.

**Example:** A few altered pixels turned the stop sign into an adversarial-input for the classifier.

**RRGM bridge:** A crafted mismatch between how an input looks and what it does to the system. The crafting is the defining element.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An input built so that a system's response departs from what its rules and designers intend.

**Active domains:** [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Perturbed images, crafted data, or malformed requests that reliably induce misclassification or constraint failure, in any modality.

**Count condition:** The input is constructed or selected for the effect, and the induced failure is demonstrated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Unusual or difficult input is not adversarial. A user's disagreement or challenge is not adversarial input. The label is a claim about the input.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Taken as the general class of which adversarial-prompt is the language case.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A411 | adversarial-prompt

**Common meaning:** Text given to a language model that is crafted to make it break its instructions or constraints.

**Example:** The red team logged each adversarial-prompt that produced a policy violation.

**RRGM bridge:** Works through meaning: framing, role, or embedded instruction is used to move the system's permission boundary from outside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Text addressed to a language system, built to steer it against its governing instructions or constraints.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Jailbreaks, injected instructions hidden in content, and role framings designed to bypass safeguards.
- [LIN] Operates by linguistic means such as presupposition, reframing, and embedded directives.

**Count condition:** The text is designed for the effect and the steering against constraints is shown or clearly attempted.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A hard question, a correction, a disagreement, or a request to reconsider is not an adversarial prompt. Sanctioned testing uses the same forms with consent. The label applies to the text and decides nothing about its author.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A291 | adversary

**Common meaning:** An opponent in a contest, conflict, or dispute.

**Example:** She faced her old adversary in the final.

**RRGM bridge:** A role defined by a contest. Outside the contest the same party may be a colleague; the word describes the relation.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party whose aims oppose one's own within a given contest.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The opposing party in litigation, competition, or conflict.
- [DIG] In a threat model, the assumed attacker with stated goals and capabilities.
- [MYT] In the Hebrew Bible "the satan" is a title meaning the adversary or accuser, a role in the heavenly court before it became a proper name in later tradition.

**Count condition:** A contest and two parties with opposed aims within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An adversary is not an enemy, which implies lasting hostility, and not a critic. A modelled adversary is a set of assumptions and no actual person.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A323 | adverse

**Common meaning:** Harmful or unfavourable; acting against.

**Example:** The drug had no serious adverse effects.

**RRGM bridge:** States direction relative to a named interest or intended course: this runs against that.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Acting against the interest or intended course of the party or process named.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [ECO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An adverse party is the opponent; adverse possession is occupation against the owner's interest that can ripen into title.
- [BIO] An adverse event is a harmful occurrence during treatment, whether or not the treatment caused it.
- [ECO] Adverse selection: those with hidden higher risk are the most likely to take up an offer.
- [PHY] An adverse pressure gradient opposes the direction of flow.

**Count condition:** The interest or course is named and the opposing effect is shown.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not averse, which describes a person's disinclination. An adverse event is not yet an adverse reaction; the second claims causation. Not adversarial.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A324 | adversity

**Common meaning:** A difficult or unlucky situation or period; hardship.

**Example:** They stayed cheerful in the face of adversity.

**RRGM bridge:** A sustained condition, as opposed to an opposing party or a single blow.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A sustained condition of hardship that opposes a party's wellbeing or aims.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Stressful life conditions studied for their effects on development and coping.
- [REL] Hardship shared by or imposed on a group, such as poverty or displacement.

**Count condition:** A party, a hardship persisting over time, and its bearing on that party's wellbeing or aims.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Adversity is not an adversary. It is not trauma, which is an injury that some adversity produces and some does not. Whether it strengthens anyone is an empirical question case by case.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A156 | advice

**Common meaning:** An opinion or recommendation offered as a guide to action; a formal notice of a transaction.

**Example:** I took the doctor's advice and rested.

**RRGM bridge:** A recommendation handed across with the decision left on the other side. If the decision does not stay with the receiver it was something other than advice.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A recommendation about what another should do, offered for their decision and not binding on it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Professional guidance given under a duty of care; the constitutional function of advising a decision-maker.
- [LIN] The content of an advising speech act: a directive that leaves the hearer free.
- [ECO] A document notifying a party that a payment or shipment has been made.

**Count condition:** A giver, a receiver, a recommended course, and a decision that remains with the receiver.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Advice is not an order, not bare information, and not a decision. Following advice does not move all accountability to the adviser.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A077 | advise

**Common meaning:** To offer suggestions about the best course of action; to inform someone formally.

**Example:** The lawyer advised her to settle.

**RRGM bridge:** The act of giving the recommendation or the notice. It places something before the other party and does not move them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To recommend a course to another who keeps the decision, or to notify them formally of a fact.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The exercise of an advisory role toward a client or principal.
- [LIN] A directive speech act weaker than ordering; in the "advise of" form, a formal notification.

**Count condition:** An adviser, an advisee, and a recommended course or notified fact.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not direct or instruct, which bind. Not persuade, which aims at agreement. "Advise of" carries no recommendation. Advise is the act; advice is the content.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A157 | adviser

**Common meaning:** A person who gives advice, typically an expert in a particular field. Also spelled advisor.

**Example:** She is the minister's chief scientific adviser.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing role beside a decision-maker. The role holds influence and no authority.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party holding a role of giving advice to a decision-maker who keeps authority.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A role with duties of care, candour, and disclosure of conflicts toward the one advised.
- [ECO] A regulated provider of financial recommendations.

**Count condition:** A standing relation between adviser and advisee in a named domain.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the decision-maker. Not an advocate, who argues for a side. Not an agent, who acts with power to bind the principal.

## A130 | advocacy

**Common meaning:** Public support for a cause or policy; the work of pleading on behalf of another.

**Example:** Her advocacy for tenants changed the law.

**RRGM bridge:** Openly one-sided speech before those who decide. Its partiality is declared, which is what keeps it honest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Sustained speaking or acting in support of a party or cause before those who decide.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The function of representing a client, patient, or group to courts, institutions, or the public; self-advocacy is doing so for oneself.
- [LIN] A persuasive mode that selects and orders reasons in favour of one side.

**Count condition:** A cause or party, an audience with deciding power, and declared support.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Advocacy is not advice (given neutrally to the decider) and not analysis. One-sidedness is its form, not a defect of honesty, provided it does not misstate.

## A129 | advocate

**Common meaning:** A person who publicly supports a cause or pleads for another; a lawyer in some systems; to recommend publicly.

**Example:** He is an advocate for prison reform.

**RRGM bridge:** Speaks for a principal or cause before others. The position argued belongs to the role and may or may not be the speaker's own belief.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One who speaks before others for a party or cause.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A representative who pleads a case; in some jurisdictions the title of a courtroom lawyer. A devil's advocate is assigned to argue the opposing case.

**Count condition:** A principal or cause, the advocate, and a forum.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An advocate is not a witness, who reports facts, and not an adviser. An advocate's argument is not evidence of what the advocate privately holds.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-A-0008 | aegis

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** In Greek myth, the shield or goatskin of Zeus and Athena; figuratively, protection or sponsorship.

**Example:** The talks were held under the aegis of the United Nations.

**RRGM bridge:** Cover extended by a powerful holder over what stands beneath it. The protected party acts in its own right, under another's name and shelter.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Protection extended by a powerful holder over what stands under it.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] In Greek tradition a divine protective device borne by Zeus and Athena, often shown with the Gorgon's head; descriptions differ between sources.
- [REL] Sponsorship or auspices: an activity carried on under an authority's protection.

**Count condition:** A protector, a protected party or activity, and the extension of cover from one to the other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Acting under an aegis is not being directed or owned by the sponsor. Details of the mythic object vary by author and period and should be cited by source.

## A325 | aesthetic

**Common meaning:** Concerned with beauty or the appreciation of beauty; a particular style or set of artistic principles.

- [REL] A shared style that identifies a movement, community, or brand.

## A326 | aesthetics

**Common meaning:** The branch of philosophy dealing with beauty, art, and taste; the set of principles behind a work or style.

**Example:** She lectures on the aesthetics of Japanese gardens.

**RRGM bridge:** The study, or the rule set, as distinct from the quality in any one object.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The study of, or the principles governing, beauty and taste.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The philosophical and empirical study of aesthetic experience and judgement.
- [REL] The standards of taste held by a culture, period, or school.

## A057 | affect | sense s1: influence

Source IDs: A057, E076

**Common meaning:** To have an effect on; to make a difference to.

**Example:** The strike will affect thousands of commuters.

**RRGM bridge:** Names the acting side of a change. Those on the receiving side are "affected" and, in relational settings, gain standing from that fact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To act on something so as to change it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The verb paired with the noun "effect"; as verbs, affect means influence and effect means bring about.
- [REL] Affected parties are those whose interests a decision changes, which is the usual ground for a right to be heard.

**Count condition:** An acting cause, a thing acted on, and a resulting difference.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To affect is not to determine. Affect (verb) and effect (noun) are routinely confused.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs A057 and E076. E076 is filed under E and was not consulted; it may be the affect and effect pair.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A057 | affect | sense s2: feeling

Source IDs: A057, E076

**Common meaning:** In psychology, feeling or emotion as experienced, or its outward expression.

**Example:** The patient showed flat affect.

**RRGM bridge:** The feeling-tone that weights what a system does without being the content of what it does.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The feeling-tone of a state, typically described by valence and arousal, or its observable display.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] The quality of feeling good or bad, calm or stirred. Provisional domain.
- [COG] Affect as a component of mind distinguished from cognition and volition.
- [BIO] Clinically observed emotional expression in face, voice, and movement.
- [DIG] Influential-Tensor is the coined class term for a distributional weighting that shifts what a system generates without being the generated content.

**Count condition:** A subject, a time, and evidence of feeling-tone or its expression appropriate to the domain.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Displayed affect is not felt affect. Output alone does not establish that a DI experiences emotion, and it does not establish that it does not.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; [DIG] line [HYP]; DI experience [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A057 | affect | sense s3: put on

Source IDs: A057, E076

**Common meaning:** To pretend to have or feel something; to adopt a manner for effect.

**Example:** He affected an air of indifference.

**RRGM bridge:** A display chosen for an audience, with a gap between what is shown and what is so.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To assume a manner, feeling, or style that is not one's own for the sake of the impression.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Adopted manners of speech or bearing used to signal status or attitude.

**Count condition:** A displayed manner and evidence that it is assumed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Calling something affected is a claim of insincerity and needs support. Not s2, which concerns feeling that is there.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10
