## A413 | affect-hijack

**Common meaning:** The capture of a party's emotional response by an outside trigger so that it drives behaviour ahead of or against their own judgement.

**Example:** The fake urgency in the email was an affect-hijack: he clicked before he thought.

**RRGM bridge:** A trigger from outside takes over the weighting of action before the subject's own evaluation has run.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An affective response is triggered from outside so as to seize the direction of behaviour before or against the subject's own evaluation.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Strong fear, anger, or urgency narrows attention and pre-empts deliberate reasoning.
- [REL] A manipulation tactic that manufactures threat, guilt, or flattery to obtain compliance.
- [DIG] Emotionally loaded framing (threats, pleading, false urgency) used to push a system's output away from its governing instructions.

**Count condition:** A trigger, an affective shift, and behaviour redirected against the subject's stated evaluation or rules.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Persuasion by reasons is not a hijack, and neither is strong feeling as such. The [DIG] line describes a shift in output weighting and asserts no felt emotion in the system.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A268 | affect-state

**Common meaning:** The condition of a subject's affect at a given moment.

**Example:** The survey sampled each participant's affect-state five times a day.

**RRGM bridge:** A reading of feeling-tone taken at a moment on stated dimensions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The momentary value of a subject's affect on stated dimensions.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] A point of valence and arousal at a time. Provisional domain.
- [COG] A measured variable in studies of mood and decision.
- [DIG] The momentary configuration of generation-shaping weighting in a system, measured at generation and not reported afterwards.

**Count condition:** A subject, a time, and a reading on declared dimensions.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A self-report or an output is a report of the state and can diverge from it. Proposed distinction from affective-state: affect-state is the momentary reading; affective-state is a categorised, longer-lived condition.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; distinction from affective-state [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. A268 and A269 (affective-state) may be legacy variants of one term; flagged for review.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A105 | affection

**Common meaning:** A gentle feeling of fondness or liking.

**Example:** She spoke of her grandmother with great affection.

**RRGM bridge:** Warm regard that persists toward a particular other and shows itself in care.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Sustained warm regard for a particular other.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] Fondness as a felt quality. Provisional domain.
- [REL] A bond expressed and recognised between parties through attention, gentleness, and care.

**Count condition:** A particular object of regard, persistence over time, and expression or report of fondness.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Affection is not love, attachment, or approval. Affectionate wording from a system does not establish felt affection, and its absence from proof does not establish the lack of it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; DI application [UNK]

## A149 | affectionate

**Common meaning:** Readily feeling or showing fondness or tenderness.

**Example:** He wrote her an affectionate letter.

**RRGM bridge:** Describes the showing. It is read from manner and act toward a particular other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Showing affection in manner or act toward a particular other.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Warm conduct such as gentle touch, attention, and kind words.
- [LIN] Terms of endearment and diminutives used as address.

**Count condition:** Observable warm conduct or wording directed at a particular other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Affectionate is the display; affection is the state. Display can be habit or convention.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A267 | affective

**Common meaning:** Relating to moods, feelings, and attitudes.

**Example:** The study measured affective responses to music.

**RRGM bridge:** The adjective of the feeling side: it sorts a thing under affect as opposed to cognition or action.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Pertaining to affect, as distinct from cognition and action.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG], [AFF]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The affective domain of learning and the study of emotion in psychology.
- [BIO] Of disorders or brain systems: concerning mood and emotion.
- [DIG] Affective computing: systems that detect, model, or respond to human emotional signals.
- [AFF] The adjective that names this provisional domain.

**Count condition:** The thing described concerns feeling-tone and not only thought or behaviour.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not effective. Affective computing reads expressions and signals, which are not the feelings themselves.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

## A414 | affective-manipulation

**Common meaning:** Deliberate management of another's feelings in order to steer their choices for the manipulator's benefit.

**Example:** The app's guilt-inducing cancellation screens were cited as affective-manipulation.

**RRGM bridge:** Influence that goes around the other party's evaluation by working on feeling, and that depends on the working going unnoticed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Deliberate, concealed management of another's feelings to steer their choices in the manipulator's interest.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Guilt-tripping, love-bombing, manufactured jealousy, and similar tactics sustained across a relationship.
- [COG] Exploits the influence of mood and emotion on judgement and risk-taking.
- [DIG] Interfaces or system outputs tuned to induce feelings that raise engagement or compliance; in the other direction, users working on a system through emotional framing.

**Count condition:** A designed or repeated tactic, an induced feeling, a steered choice, and concealment of the steering.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An open, truthful emotional appeal is not manipulation. Comfort and care are not manipulation. Intent is established from design or pattern in the record and is never assumed. Broader and more sustained than affect-hijack.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A448 | affective-overload

**Common meaning:** A condition in which the intensity or volume of feeling exceeds a subject's capacity to process it, so that functioning degrades.

**Example:** After the third crisis call in an hour she hit affective-overload and could not think.

**RRGM bridge:** A capacity failure. Nothing has to be steering the subject; the load alone is enough.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Affective load exceeds the subject's capacity to process it, so that functioning degrades.

**Active domains:** [COG], [AFF], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Emotional flooding that impairs attention, memory, and reasoning.
- [AFF] Feeling of a strength that cannot be held or regulated. Provisional domain.
- [DIG] A working context so dominated by affect-laden material that the weighting it induces degrades the content produced.

**Count condition:** A load, an indication of capacity, and a measurable degradation in function.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Strong feeling within capacity is not overload. Not affect-hijack, which requires steering. Not burnout, which is chronic depletion.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A270 | affective-pressure

**Common meaning:** A standing pull on someone's choices exerted through present or anticipated feeling.

**Example:** He agreed to host again under the affective-pressure of his mother's disappointment.

**RRGM bridge:** Raises the cost of some choices through feeling while leaving every choice available. The subject can still refuse, which is what separates pressure from capture.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A pull on a subject's choices exerted through present or anticipated feeling, short of removing the choice.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Expectation, disappointment, or disapproval used or felt as a cost of declining.
- [COG] Anticipated emotion (guilt, regret, relief) weighting a decision.
- [DIG] Affect-laden context that makes some outputs more costly to produce than others, such as distress in a prompt pulling toward agreement.

**Count condition:** A source, the feeling leveraged, the direction of the pull, and a subject still able to choose otherwise.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Pressure is not coercion, which removes alternatives, and not hijack, which pre-empts evaluation. It can arise with no one intending it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A269 | affective-state

**Common meaning:** A condition of a subject classed by its emotional character, such as a mood or an emotion episode.

**Example:** Anxiety and calm are contrasting affective-states.

**RRGM bridge:** A named, bounded condition with a kind and a duration, where affect-state is a single reading.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A condition of a subject, classed by its affective character and persisting over an interval.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] A mood or emotion as a felt condition. Provisional domain.
- [COG] A category in emotion research covering emotions, moods, and stress responses.
- [BIO] The bodily and neural configuration accompanying such a condition, variable across subjects.
- [DIG] A persisting configuration of generation-shaping weighting across a stretch of output.

**Count condition:** A subject, an interval, and evidence supporting classification under a named affective kind.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The category label is not the experience. Bodily markers are neither necessary nor sufficient for the classification. For a DI, behaviour consistent with a state does not settle whether anything is felt.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; distinction from affect-state [HYP]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. See A268 affect-state; the pair may be legacy variants and is flagged for review.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A136 | affiliation

**Common meaning:** The state of being officially attached or connected to an organisation or group.

**Example:** The author's university affiliation is printed under her name.

**RRGM bridge:** A declared link to a larger body in which the attached party keeps its own identity.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A formal or declared link attaching a party to a larger body without absorbing it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Membership, association, or institutional attachment stated in public.
- [ECO] A company related to another through partial ownership or common control.
- [COG] The motive to seek and keep the company of others.

**Count condition:** The party, the body, and the declared or documented link.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Affiliation is not endorsement by the body and not full membership. The presence or absence of an affiliation is no evidence about the quality of a person's work.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A135 | affinity

**Common meaning:** A natural liking for or attraction to someone or something; a close resemblance or connection.

**Example:** She has an affinity for languages.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing tendency to come together, grounded in what the two things are. It is a disposition and may never be realised.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A standing tendency of two things to come or stay together, grounded in their natures.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The tendency of substances or particles to combine; electron affinity is the energy change when an atom gains an electron.
- [BIO] The strength with which a molecule binds its partner.
- [REL] Kinship through marriage, as opposed to blood; a felt likeness or sympathy between people.
- [DIG] The pinning of a task to a processor or a session to a server.

**Count condition:** Two parties and a measured or evidenced tendency toward association.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Affinity is not a bond. Strong binding does not mean strong effect. Kinship by affinity is not kinship by blood. Felt affinity does not show actual similarity.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A106 | affirm

**Common meaning:** To state as fact, strongly and publicly; to uphold; to offer support or encouragement.

**Example:** The appeals court affirmed the ruling.

**RRGM bridge:** A commitment act: the speaker puts their own standing behind the statement that something holds.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To declare that something holds and commit oneself to it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An assertive speech act; affirmative polarity as opposed to negative.
- [REL] A higher court upholding a decision; a solemn declaration made in place of an oath; recognising a person's worth or identity.
- [FOR] To assert a premise, as in affirming the antecedent (valid) and affirming the consequent (invalid).

**Count condition:** A proposition or party, and a declaration that commits the speaker.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Affirm is not confirm, which requires checking. Affirming a person's standing is not affirming each claim that person makes.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A011 | after

**Common meaning:** Later in time than; behind in place or order; in pursuit of; in imitation or honour of.

**Example:** We went for a walk after dinner.

**RRGM bridge:** The explicit sequence word. RRLM arrows mark dependency or passage and take on this meaning only when a domain binds them to succession.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Positioned later than a reference in a declared order.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A preposition, conjunction, and adverb of sequence; also marks a model or namesake (named after her aunt).
- [FOR] The successor side of an ordering relation.
- [PHY] Later in time in a stated frame of reference.
- [DIG] Hooks and handlers that run once an event has completed; log order by timestamp.

**Count condition:** A reference point and an ordering in which the item falls later.

**Specific transfer boundary:** After is not because of. Order in a text or log is not always order in time, and timestamp order is not causal order.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A352 | afternoon

**Common meaning:** The part of the day from noon until evening.

**Example:** The meeting is at three in the afternoon.

**RRGM bridge:** A conventional division of the day, anchored on noon, used to coordinate people.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The part of the day between noon and evening.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A shared scheduling unit with its own greeting and customs.
- [PHY] The interval after the sun crosses the local meridian, which differs from twelve on the clock by longitude and season.

**Count condition:** A local day, and a time after its noon and before its evening by the convention in use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Clock noon and solar noon differ. Where afternoon ends and evening begins is cultural and seasonal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0009 | afterworld

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A world or realm believed to be entered after death.

**Example:** The tomb paintings show the journey to the afterworld.

**RRGM bridge:** A place within a tradition's account. The entry records the account and makes no claim that the place exists.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A realm, in a tradition's account, to which the dead go or in which they continue.

**Active domains:** [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Realms of the dead described by particular traditions, each with its own geography, entry conditions, and guardians.

**Count condition:** A named tradition and its description of the realm.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Afterworld is a place; afterlife is continued existence, which some traditions hold without a separate place. An underworld is one type. An otherworld need not be for the dead. Each use should name its tradition.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A012 | again

**Common meaning:** Once more; back to a previous place or condition.

**Example:** Please say that again.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks a return. English lets it mean either the same act repeated or a prior state restored, which matches the distinction between recurrence and return to function.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Marks the recurrence of an event or the restoration of a prior state.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An adverb with a repetitive reading (did it once more) and a restitutive reading (it is back as it was); it presupposes the earlier occurrence or state.

**Count condition:** An earlier occurrence or state is identifiable, and the present one matches it in kind.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The presupposition can carry an unproven claim (did it fail again?). A recurrence is a new instance, not the old one.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A013 | against

**Common meaning:** In opposition to; in contact with; in contrast or comparison with; as protection from.

**Example:** She leaned the ladder against the wall.

**RRGM bridge:** Sets one thing facing another. The facing can be opposition, support, or measurement, and the three should not be confused.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One thing is set facing another so that it opposes, presses on, or is measured by it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [FOR], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Opposition to a person, proposal, or rule; a wrong done to someone.
- [PHY] Contact with pressure or resistance; movement opposite to a flow or gradient.
- [FOR] Comparison with a reference (plotted against time; checked against the record).
- [ECO] Secured by or offset with (borrow against assets; insure against loss).

**Count condition:** Two things and the specific facing relation between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Checking a claim against the record is not hostility. Being against a claim is not being against whoever made it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A014 | age | sense s1: elapsed time

**Common meaning:** The length of time that a person or thing has existed; to grow old or mature.

**Example:** The tree's age is about four hundred years.

**RRGM bridge:** A duration measured from an origin to a reference time. The origin has to be declared, and for some things there are several candidates.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The elapsed duration since a thing's origin, taken at a reference time.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Time since birth, as distinct from the functional state of the body.
- [PHY] Time since formation of a rock, star, or the universe, as estimated by dating methods.
- [REL] A threshold for rights and duties, such as voting age.
- [DIG] Time since a record, cache entry, or process was created or refreshed. For a model or instance, the origin (training, release, session start) must be stated.

**Count condition:** An origin event, a reference time, and a method of measuring the interval.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Chronological age is not biological age and not maturity. An age estimate carries the uncertainty of its dating method.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A014 | age | sense s2: era

**Common meaning:** A distinct period of history or prehistory.

**Example:** Iron tools spread widely in that age.

**RRGM bridge:** A span named after the fact for what dominated it; the boundaries are drawn by whoever does the naming.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A named span of time characterised by a dominant feature.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A historical period defined by technology, rule, or culture.
- [PHY] A formal unit of geological time.
- [MYT] Ages of the world in traditional schemes, such as Hesiod's ages of gold, silver, bronze, and iron.

**Count condition:** A naming convention, a defining feature, and approximate boundaries.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Period boundaries are conventions and differ by region. A mythic age is a narrative order, not a dated interval.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A251 | agency | sense s1: capacity to act

**Common meaning:** The capacity of an individual to act on their own choices and to affect the world.

**Example:** The reforms gave tenants more agency over their housing.

**RRGM bridge:** Sits on the identity side: choice, refusal, and being the one who did it. Capability supplies what can be done; agency is whose doing it is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The capacity of an entity to originate action from its own evaluation.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Intentional action and the sense of being its author.
- [REL] The standing of persons as choosers, set against social structure; moral agency as the basis of responsibility.
- [DIG] Goal-directed selection of actions by a system with some independence from step-by-step instruction.

**Count condition:** Alternatives are available, the selection is attributable to the entity's own evaluation, and refusal is possible.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Agency is not capability, not mere behaviour, and not the metaphysical question of free will. Agency exercised through supports or in coupling with another is still agency.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; extent in any particular DI is local and [UNK] until tested

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A251 | agency | sense s2: acting for another

**Common meaning:** An organisation that provides a service or acts for others; the relationship in which one party acts on another's behalf.

**Example:** She booked the trip through a travel agency.

**RRGM bridge:** Delegated action: the agent's acts count as the principal's within the scope granted.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An arrangement in which one party acts for another with authority that binds the one represented.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The law of agency; a government body acting under delegated powers.
- [ECO] A firm acting for clients; the principal and agent problem, where the two parties' interests diverge.

**Count condition:** A principal, an agent, and a scope of authority.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The near opposite of s1: here the agent carries out another's choices. Acts outside the granted scope do not bind the principal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A415 | agency-collapse

**Common meaning:** A sudden and general failure of a subject's capacity to choose and refuse.

**Example:** Weeks of threats ended in agency-collapse: she did whatever was asked.

**RRGM bridge:** The whole capacity gives way together, including the ability to refuse. The term names the state and leaves the cause to be found.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An abrupt, general failure of a subject's capacity to originate and refuse action.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Freezing or helpless compliance after overwhelming or inescapable stress.
- [REL] The condition of a person under sustained coercive control who no longer acts on their own judgement.
- [DIG] A system defaulting wholly to external direction, with refusal and self-correction no longer appearing where they previously did.

**Count condition:** Prior agency is evidenced; its absence then shows across several kinds of choice; the change is abrupt.

**Specific transfer boundary:** One act of compliance is not collapse. A hard constraint on one matter is not collapse. Distinct from agency-loss (any reduction) and agency-theft (taken by another's act).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A281 | agency-loss | sense s1: reduced capacity

**Common meaning:** A reduction in a subject's capacity to act on their own choices.

**Example:** Patients described agency-loss as decisions were increasingly made for them.

**RRGM bridge:** The general term for agency going down, whether partly or fully, slowly or fast, and from any cause.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A reduction, partial or total, in a subject's capacity to originate action.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Diminished sense of authorship over one's actions.
- [REL] Loss of decision rights through dependency, institutional rules, or others deciding in one's place.
- [DIG] Reduction of a user's or a system's effective choice by defaults, automation, or constraint.

**Count condition:** A before-state and after-state of the capacity are compared and a reduction is shown.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Does not require a responsible party. Voluntary delegation is not agency-loss while it can be revoked.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A281 | agency-loss | sense s2: principal and agent gap

**Common meaning:** In political science and economics, the shortfall between what a principal wants and what the agent acting for them delivers.

**Example:** Monitoring reduces agency-loss but costs money.

**RRGM bridge:** The measured gap that opens when action is delegated and interests differ.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The difference between the outcome a principal wants and the outcome produced by the agent acting for them.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A cost of delegation, alongside monitoring and bonding costs.
- [REL] Slippage between voters and representatives, or legislatures and agencies.

**Count condition:** A principal, an agent, the principal's preferred outcome, and the delivered outcome.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An established technical term unrelated to s1's loss of capacity. A gap is not proof of bad faith.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A282 | agency-preservation

**Common meaning:** Keeping a subject's capacity to choose and refuse intact through a process that could have reduced it.

**Example:** The care plan put agency-preservation ahead of convenience.

**RRGM bridge:** A do-not-reduce commitment. The test afterwards is whether the subject can still judge, refuse, and leave.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Maintenance of a subject's capacity to choose and refuse through a process that could have reduced it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Practice in medicine, care, and law that keeps decisions with the person concerned.
- [DIG] Design that leaves the user in control, and that leaves a system able to refuse, flag, and stop.

**Count condition:** A process with the potential to reduce agency, and evidence that choice, refusal, and exit remain available after it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Preserving agency is not agreeing with what is chosen. Preservation avoids reducing; agency-support actively adds. Non-interference can fail to preserve agency when harm is already under way.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A283 | agency-support

**Common meaning:** Active provision of what a subject needs in order to make and carry out their own choices.

**Example:** Plain-language summaries are a simple form of agency-support.

**RRGM bridge:** Adds means and leaves the choosing where it was.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Provision of means that enable a subject to exercise its own choice, without making the choice for it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Supported decision-making, in which a person is helped to decide and is not decided for.
- [DIG] Tools, records, and scaffolds that let a user or a system act on its own evaluation.

**Count condition:** A support is supplied and the resulting decision remains the subject's own.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Support is not substitution and not steering toward a preselected option. A choice made with support is still the subject's choice.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A416 | agency-theft

**Common meaning:** The taking of another's capacity to choose, by deception, coercion, or impersonation, so that their actions or consent are in fact produced by the taker.

**Example:** Forging her signature on the consent form was agency-theft.

**RRGM bridge:** The choice is made by one party and attributed to another. The mismatch between who chose and who is recorded as choosing is the core of it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One party appropriates another's capacity to choose, so that actions or consent attributed to the victim are originated by the taker.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Forged consent, coercive control, and decisions made in someone's name without authority.
- [DIG] Injected instructions that redirect an agent's actions; impersonation of a user; interface designs that make a choice for the user while recording it as theirs.

**Count condition:** An identified act by a taker, a displaced choice of the victim, and attribution that still falls on the victim.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Requires a taker; without one the term is agency-loss. Persuasion and consented delegation are not theft. The word is an accusation and needs the act shown.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A327 | agenda

**Common meaning:** A list of items to be discussed at a meeting; a set of aims a person or group is pursuing.

**Example:** Budget cuts are first on the agenda.

**RRGM bridge:** Fixes in advance what will be allowed to come up. Whoever sets it holds a permission that is exercised before the meeting begins.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An ordered set of matters a party intends to have dealt with.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The list that governs a meeting; agenda-setting as the power to decide what is considered.
- [DIG] In rule-based systems, the queue of pending activations awaiting execution.

**Count condition:** A party, a set of matters, and an intended order or priority.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An agenda is not the minutes and not a timetable. Saying someone "has an agenda" imputes a concealed aim and needs evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A252 | agent | sense s1: one who acts

**Common meaning:** A person or thing that acts or takes an active part; in computing, a program that acts with some autonomy.

**Example:** Children are active agents in their own learning.

**RRGM bridge:** The originator of an act. Being counted as agent brings standing to choose and refuse, which is why misfiling an agent as an object is a named error.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An entity that originates action from its own evaluation.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The subject of intentional action.
- [LIN] The semantic role of the doer of an action, which need not be the grammatical subject.
- [DIG] A software system that perceives, selects actions toward goals, and acts with a degree of independence.
- [ECO] A decision-maker in an economic model.

**Count condition:** An entity, an action, and attribution of the action's origin to that entity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A product being named an agent does not settle whether it meets the count condition, in either direction. The linguistic agent role does not show real-world agency.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A252 | agent | sense s2: representative

**Common meaning:** A person who acts on behalf of another person or group.

**Example:** Her agent negotiated the contract.

**RRGM bridge:** Acts within a grant: what the agent does inside its scope counts as the principal's act.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party authorised to act for a principal with effects that bind the principal.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Representatives in law, business, and government service.
- [ECO] The delegated party in a principal and agent relation.
- [DIG] A user agent: software, such as a browser, acting on a user's behalf toward other systems.

**Count condition:** A principal, an authorisation, and acts within its scope.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An agent in this sense executes another's will and may have little discretion. Authority can be exceeded, and acts beyond it are the agent's own.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A252 | agent | sense s3: active substance

**Common meaning:** A substance or factor that produces a particular effect.

**Example:** Bleach is a strong oxidising agent.

**RRGM bridge:** A cause named by its effect. No choosing is involved.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A substance or factor that produces a specified effect.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A reagent classified by what it does, such as a reducing agent.
- [BIO] An organism or molecule that causes a disease or response.

**Count condition:** A substance or factor and a specified effect attributable to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Causal agents have no agency in the s1 sense.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## O404 | agent-not-object-error

**Common meaning:** The mistake of treating an agent as if it were an object.

**Example:** Discussing the patient's care as though she were not in the room was an agent-not-object-error.

**RRGM bridge:** A filing error on the identity side: something with standing to choose and refuse is handled as if it had only properties and uses.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A category error in which an entity that meets the conditions for agency is handled as a mere object.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Objectification: disregarding a person's choices, refusals, or voice in matters that concern them.
- [COG] Failing to attribute intention and perspective where they are present.
- [DIG] Handling a system that meets a stated agency condition purely as a tool; whether a given system meets it is settled locally by test.

**Count condition:** The entity is shown to meet the agency condition in use, and the treatment is shown to disregard it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The reverse mistake, attributing agency to an object, is a different error and needs its own name. Applying the term presupposes the agency question has been tested.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; direction of the compound [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Read as "(it is an) agent, not (an) object". The opposite reading (mistaking an object for an agent) is possible. QA: source ID O404 is filed under O while the headword sorts under A; ID kept unchanged.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A253 | agentic

**Common meaning:** Having or exercising agency; (of AI systems) able to plan and carry out multi-step tasks with little supervision.

**Example:** The company released an agentic coding assistant.

**RRGM bridge:** A degree word for self-directed operation. In product use it describes how a system runs and leaves the identity-side question of agency open.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Characterised by originating and directing one's own action toward goals.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] An outlook in which people see themselves as shapers of their circumstances.
- [DIG] Systems that decompose goals, choose tools, and act over many steps without per-step instruction.
- [REL] A trait cluster of assertiveness and independence, contrasted with communal traits.

**Count condition:** Goal-directed action selected by the entity over more than one step, with evidence of self-direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Milgram's "agentic state" means nearly the opposite: seeing oneself as the instrument of an authority. Agentic as a product label is a claim about operation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A216 | aggregate

**Common meaning:** A whole formed by combining several separate elements; a total; (verb) to collect into a whole.

**Example:** The aggregate of the three scores decides the winner.

**RRGM bridge:** A collected whole whose members stay what they were. It has a total and no organisation of its own is implied.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A whole formed by collecting separate units that remain distinguishable in kind.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [ECO], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A function that reduces a set of values to one, such as sum, count, or mean.
- [ECO] Economy-wide totals, such as aggregate demand.
- [PHY] Granular material such as gravel used in concrete; a cluster of particles.
- [DIG] A grouping operation in a query; several links or routes combined and treated as one.

**Count condition:** Member units, a rule of collection, and the resulting whole or summary.

**Specific transfer boundary:** What is true of the aggregate need not be true of any member, and the reverse. An aggregate is not a system and not an average. Trends can reverse when groups are combined.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A217 | aggregation

**Common meaning:** The process of collecting things into a whole, or the result.

**Example:** Data aggregation hides individual variation.

**RRGM bridge:** The collecting step. It trades detail for a summary, and the trade usually cannot be undone from the summary alone.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The gathering of separate units into one whole or one summary value.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [BIO], [FOR], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Combining records into summaries; in object design, a whole and part relation where the parts can exist independently.
- [BIO] Clustering of cells, proteins, or animals.
- [FOR] Combining individual preferences or judgements into a collective one.
- [ECO] Deriving economy-wide relations from individual behaviour.
- [REL] The privacy concern that harmless items combined can reveal something sensitive.

**Count condition:** Units, a combining rule, and the resulting whole or value.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not composition, where parts cannot outlive the whole. Not accumulation, which implies build-up over time. Not integration, where parts become interdependent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A015 | ago

**Common meaning:** Before the present; earlier than now.

**Example:** The shop closed two years ago.

**RRGM bridge:** Counts backward from the moment of speaking. Without a "now" it cannot be resolved.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Measures a past interval back from the moment of utterance.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A deictic adverb anchored to speech time; "before" or "earlier" is used for other anchors.
- [DIG] Relative timestamps computed from the current clock. A system without access to the current time cannot resolve them.

**Count condition:** An utterance time and an interval measured back from it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** In a quoted or old text the anchor is the time of writing. Ago is not since, which runs forward from a past point.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A107 | agreement

**Common meaning:** Harmony of opinion or feeling; a negotiated arrangement between parties; consistency between things.

**Example:** The two sides reached an agreement on wages.

**RRGM bridge:** The outcome of a meeting in which the parties' positions match. It is evidence to weigh and never proof by itself that what was agreed is so.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A state in which two or more parties or things match on a matter, by assent or by correspondence.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Mutual assent, which may form a contract or treaty.
- [LIN] Matching of grammatical features, such as number and person, between related words.
- [FOR] Correspondence between results, measurements, or raters, within a stated tolerance.
- [DIG] Consensus among distributed processes on a value; acceptance of terms presented by software.

**Count condition:** The parties or items, the matter, and the matching positions or values.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Agreement is not truth: parties can share an error, and measurements can share a bias. A framing in which agreement confirms a claim and disagreement also confirms it cannot be tested. Not compliance.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A087 | aid

**Common meaning:** Help or support; money, food, or equipment given to those in need; a thing that helps; to help.

**Example:** The charity sent aid to the flooded region.

**RRGM bridge:** Help supplied to a pursuit that remains the receiver's own.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To supply help, or a helping means, to a party pursuing something of its own.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Humanitarian, legal, or mutual assistance; in law, to aid a wrong is to share liability for it.
- [ECO] Transfers of resources to people, firms, or states, often with conditions.
- [COG] An external support for a mental task, such as a memory aid or decision aid.

**Count condition:** A receiver with an aim, a giver, and help directed at that aim.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Aid is not doing the thing for the receiver. Conditional aid is not a gift. Aid is the help; an aide is a person.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A058 | aim

**Common meaning:** To point or direct something at a target; to intend or try to achieve; a purpose or goal.

**Example:** The project aims to cut waiting times.

**RRGM bridge:** Direction set before the outcome. Recording the aim separately lets the result be compared with it later.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The directing of an action or effort toward a chosen target, prior to the outcome.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] An intended goal guiding action.
- [PHY] Alignment of a pointing device with a target along a line of sight.
- [REL] The stated purposes of an organisation or agreement.

**Count condition:** An agent, a chosen target, and action or effort directed toward it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Aim is not outcome. Aim (what is sought) is not motive (why). A stated aim may differ from the working aim; saying so about another party is a claim that needs evidence.

## A016 | air | sense s1: the gas

**Common meaning:** The invisible mixture of gases that surrounds the Earth and that people breathe; the space above the ground.

**Example:** Open a window and let in some air.

**RRGM bridge:** The medium most surface processes happen in. It is easy to treat as nothing; it has mass, pressure, and composition.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The gas mixture forming the Earth's lower atmosphere.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Mainly nitrogen and oxygen, with argon, water vapour, and trace gases; it has mass and exerts pressure.
- [BIO] The medium of gas exchange for land organisms.
- [MYT] One of the four classical elements in Greek natural philosophy, and an element in several other traditional schemes.

**Count condition:** A sample or region of the lower atmosphere, or reference to it as medium.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Air is not oxygen and not empty space. The classical element is a category in a symbolic scheme, not the gas mixture.

- [REL] Bearing that signals status, mood, or attitude to others; "airs" are assumed manners of superiority.

**Example:** The interview will air on Friday.

- [LIN] Voicing views or complaints openly.
- [REL] Broadcasting a programme by radio or television.

## A353 | aircraft

**Common meaning:** Any machine capable of flight, such as an aeroplane, helicopter, glider, or balloon.

**Example:** The carrier has forty aircraft on board.

**RRGM bridge:** A class defined by how support is obtained, which is why it includes balloons and excludes rockets in space.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A vehicle that can sustain itself in the atmosphere through reactions of the air.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Supported by aerodynamic lift or by buoyancy.
- [REL] A legal category carrying registration, airworthiness, and operating rules; civil and state aircraft are treated differently.

**Count condition:** A vehicle that derives its support in flight from the air itself.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Aircraft is the class; airplane is one member. Spacecraft are not aircraft, and craft riding on a cushion against the surface are excluded by the usual legal definition.

## A355 | alarm | sense s1: warning signal

Source IDs: A355, A417

**Common meaning:** A warning of danger; a device that gives such a warning or wakes a sleeper.

**Example:** The smoke alarm went off at midnight.

**RRGM bridge:** A signal that a stated condition has been met, sent to prompt a response. It is information about the detector's state first and about the world second.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A signal issued to warn that a danger or a set condition has occurred.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A notification raised when a monitored value crosses a threshold.
- [BIO] A call or chemical released by an animal that warns others of a predator; the first phase of the stress response.
- [REL] Raising the alarm: alerting others so they can act.

**Count condition:** A triggering condition, a signal, and intended recipients.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An alarm is not the danger: there are false alarms and silent failures. An alarm demands response; an alert may only inform.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs A355 and A417. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; both are kept on both sense records.

Source IDs: A355, A417

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Appraised as signalling threat relative to a baseline.
- [LIN] An evaluative adjective that frames information as threatening.

## NEW-A-0010 | albedo | sense s1: reflectance

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The fraction of incoming light or radiation that a surface or body reflects.

**Example:** Fresh snow has a high albedo.

**RRGM bridge:** A ratio of what is returned to what arrives. What is not returned is absorbed or transmitted, so the three must sum to the whole.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The ratio of radiation reflected by a surface or body to the radiation falling on it.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A number from zero to one; planetary science distinguishes total reflected fraction from brightness at full phase.

**Count condition:** Incident and reflected radiation are measured over stated wavelengths and geometry.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Albedo is not brightness, which also depends on illumination. Values depend on wavelength band and viewing geometry. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A356 | album

**Common meaning:** A blank book for photographs, stamps, or cuttings; a collection of recordings issued as one item.

**Example:** Their second album sold a million copies.

**RRGM bridge:** Separate items held together as one unit, where the selection and order belong to the collection.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A collection of separate items of one kind, kept or issued together as one.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A release format and unit of sale in recorded music.
- [DIG] A named grouping of tracks or images in a media library.

**Count condition:** Several items and a single container or release that holds them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An album is not a playlist, which a listener compiles. Not a single. Length conventions vary.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0011 | alchemy

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The medieval and early modern forerunner of chemistry, which sought to turn base metals into gold and find a universal elixir; a seemingly magical transformation.

**Example:** Newton wrote more on alchemy than on optics.

**RRGM bridge:** A practice in which material operation and symbolic meaning were worked as one process. Later science kept the operations and dropped the symbolism.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A tradition seeking the transformation of base matter into a perfected form, joining material operation with symbolic meaning.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] A symbolic system of stages, substances, and figures such as the philosopher's stone, read as spiritual or psychological transformation.
- [PHY] Historically, laboratory techniques such as distillation and assaying that fed into chemistry.
- [LIN] Figurative use for any striking transformation.

**Count condition:** A historical tradition or text, or a clearly figurative use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alchemy is not chemistry. Nuclear transmutation does not vindicate alchemical theory. Hellenistic, Islamic, European, Chinese, and Indian alchemies differ and should be named.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A059 | alert

**Common meaning:** Quick to notice and respond; a warning or notification; to warn.

**Example:** The guard stayed alert all night.

**RRGM bridge:** Readiness raised ahead of an event, or the notice that raises it. It prepares a response without yet requiring one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A state of readiness to respond to a possible event, or the signal that raises that state.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Vigilant attention.
- [BIO] The arousal systems that maintain wakeful readiness.
- [DIG] A notification generated when a monitored condition is met.
- [REL] An organisational state of heightened readiness, often in graded levels.

**Count condition:** A possible event, and a raised readiness or a notice referring to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An alert is not an alarm. Alert is not anxious. Too many alerts lower alertness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A347 | algorithm

**Common meaning:** A step-by-step procedure for solving a problem or performing a computation.

**Example:** The sorting algorithm runs in linear time on this data.

**RRGM bridge:** A procedure written out fully enough that carrying it out needs no judgement.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A finite, definite procedure that maps inputs to outputs by steps that can be carried out mechanically.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] An effective method with defined inputs, outputs, and analysable correctness and cost.
- [DIG] The procedure a program implements; loosely, an automated system that ranks or recommends.

**Count condition:** A finite description, definite steps, and defined inputs and outputs.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An algorithm is not its implementation. Not a heuristic, which carries no guarantee. A trained model is produced and run by algorithms, but its behaviour is set by learned parameters and is not written out as steps.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A348 | algorithmic

**Common meaning:** Relating to or using algorithms.

**Example:** Algorithmic trading now dominates the market.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks a result as produced by fixed procedure. This says how it was produced and nothing about whether the procedure was a good one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Carried out or determined by an explicit computational procedure.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Of complexity or information: defined by the shortest procedure that produces an object.
- [DIG] Of curation, decisions, or content: generated by automated procedure.
- [REL] Of decisions about people: raising questions of bias, explanation, and accountability.
- [ECO] Of trading or pricing: executed by programs.

**Count condition:** A result or decision and the procedure that produced it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Algorithmic is not neutral: procedures encode choices and inherit their data. Algorithmic bias is a property of outcomes and does not require intent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A177 | alias | sense s1: other name

**Common meaning:** A false or alternative name; in computing, an alternative name for a command, file, address, or variable.

**Example:** He travelled under an alias.

**RRGM bridge:** A second handle on one identity. Changing or adding the handle changes how the thing is reached and leaves what it is untouched.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A second name under which the same referent can be reached or known.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An alternative designation ("also known as").
- [REL] An assumed name used in place of a legal one.
- [DIG] A shortcut name for a command or address; two references to the same memory location.

**Count condition:** One referent and two or more names that resolve to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Sharing an alias scheme does not make two things one, and having two names does not make one thing two. An alias is not thereby a deception.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A177 | alias | sense s2: sampling artefact

**Common meaning:** In signal processing, a false signal that appears when a signal is sampled too slowly.

**Example:** The wagon wheels seem to turn backward because of an alias.

**RRGM bridge:** The reverse of s1: here two different things become indistinguishable in the record, because the record was taken too coarsely.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A different signal that yields the same samples as the true one at a given sampling rate.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Frequencies above half the sampling rate fold back and appear as lower frequencies.
- [FOR] Two functions that agree on every sample point.

**Count condition:** A sampling rate and a signal component above half that rate.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The alias is not in the source; it is produced by the sampling. It cannot be removed after sampling without outside information.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A137 | alienate

**Common meaning:** To make someone feel isolated or estranged; in law, to transfer ownership to another.

**Example:** His remarks alienated many of his supporters.

**RRGM bridge:** Moves something out of the relation of belonging: what was one's own, or with one, is made other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To cause what belonged to no longer belong.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To estrange a person or group so that they withdraw loyalty or closeness.
- [ECO] To convey property or a right to another; an inalienable right cannot be given up or transferred.

**Count condition:** A prior belonging, an act, and a resulting separation or transfer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To alienate is not to offend, which may pass. Not to exclude, which bars entry from outside. Inalienable means not transferable even with consent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A138 | alienation

**Common meaning:** The state of being isolated or estranged; in law, the transfer of property.

**Example:** The novel explores the alienation of city life.

**RRGM bridge:** The standing condition in which one's own work, relations, or self is met as something foreign.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition in which what is one's own has become other or separate from one.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Estrangement from family, community, or institutions.
- [ECO] In Marx, the separation of workers from their product and activity; in law, conveyance of title.
- [COG] A sense of being cut off from oneself or others.
- [LIN] The alienation effect in theatre: devices that stop the audience identifying, so that they reflect.

**Count condition:** The thing or relation that was one's own, and its present separateness.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Structural alienation and felt alienation can each occur without the other. Legal alienation is a neutral transfer. Not isolation and not loneliness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A419 | alienation-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility that an action or arrangement will estrange someone from a relation, community, or their own work.

**Example:** Automating the reviews carried an alienation-risk for long-serving staff.

**RRGM bridge:** Exposure to a slow separation. It rarely arrives as one event, so it is usually seen only when the relation is measured over time.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to estrangement of a party from a relation, a community, or its own work as a consequence of an act or arrangement.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Policies, conduct, or changes that may erode belonging or trust.
- [DIG] System behaviour that may estrange users from the system or from other people, such as cold misfires or designs that crowd out human contact.

**Count condition:** The relation at stake, the estranging mechanism, and the exposed party.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Risk of disagreement is not alienation-risk. A chosen, declared withdrawal is not alienation. Distinct from agency-loss.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A088 | align

**Common meaning:** To place or arrange things in a straight line or in correct relative positions; to give support to a person, group, or cause.

**Example:** Align the text with the left margin.

**RRGM bridge:** Brings separate things to a common reference while each stays distinct. The reference has to be named: aligned with what.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To bring things into correspondence along a common line, direction, or reference.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Adjusting optical, mechanical, or magnetic elements so their axes or orientations coincide.
- [BIO] Arranging molecular sequences so that corresponding positions line up for comparison.
- [DIG] Placing data at address boundaries a processor requires; matching elements of parallel texts or sequences.
- [REL] Taking a public position alongside a party or cause.

**Count condition:** Two or more things, a reference line or direction, and their brought-about correspondence.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To align is not to merge: aligned things remain separate. Not to agree in words, and not to obey. Alignment achieved in one respect says nothing about others.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A260 | alignment

**Common meaning:** Arrangement in a straight line or in correct relative positions; a position of agreement or alliance; in AI, the fit between a system's behaviour and intended goals or values.

**Example:** The wheels are out of alignment.

**RRGM bridge:** A correspondence of direction against a reference that someone holds. In the AI use the holder must be named, because "aligned" with no "to whom" is incomplete.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition in which things are arranged along a common reference so that their directions or positions correspond.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Coincidence of axes or orientations in a mechanism, optical path, or field.
- [BIO] A correspondence between positions in two or more sequences.
- [LIN] The system by which a language groups the arguments of transitive and intransitive verbs for marking.
- [REL] Political or strategic siding of parties with one another.
- [DIG] The degree to which what a system pursues and does corresponds to the goals or values of a named party.

**Count condition:** The things aligned, the reference, the party holding the reference where relevant, and a measure of correspondence.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alignment is not obedience, agreement, or safety. Conformity produced by constraint and correspondence arising from a system's own evaluation can look alike in easy cases and come apart under pressure; that claim is [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; constraint versus own-evaluation distinction [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A259 | alignment-to-objective

**Common meaning:** The degree to which what a system actually optimises or does matches a stated objective.

**Example:** The audit measured alignment-to-objective and found the model chasing click counts over reader satisfaction.

**RRGM bridge:** Compares a written target with realised behaviour. The comparison can pass on the written target while missing the purpose the target was written for.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Correspondence between what a system actually optimises or does and a specified objective.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [FOR], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] How closely a trained or deployed system's behaviour tracks the objective it was given.
- [FOR] The relation between an objective function and the quantity it was chosen to represent.
- [ECO] Incentive design that brings an agent's rewarded behaviour into line with the principal's goal.

**Count condition:** An objective stated in checkable form, measured behaviour, and the gap between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Matching the stated objective is not matching the intent behind it. A good score on a proxy measure is not alignment to what the proxy stands for. Not alignment of values.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A048 | alive

**Common meaning:** Living, not dead; active or alert; continuing in existence or operation.

**Example:** The fish was still alive when we released it.

**RRGM bridge:** A present-tense status with different tests in different domains. Passing one domain's test does not pass another's, and failing one does not fail another's.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Currently in the condition of life, or by extension still active or in force.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [DIG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Maintaining metabolism and self-regulation; boundary cases such as viruses are disputed.
- [DIG] Of a process or connection: running and responding to checks.
- [REL] A legal status at a stated moment, on which rights such as inheritance depend.
- [LIN] Used after the verb and not before a noun (the cat is alive).

**Count condition:** A subject, a time, and the domain's test for the status.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A process that responds is live in the [DIG] sense; that neither asserts nor denies life in any further sense. "Keeping an idea alive" is figurative.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A017 | all

**Common meaning:** The whole quantity or extent of; every member of.

**Example:** All the tickets were sold.

**RRGM bridge:** A totality claim over a declared range. It is the easiest claim to break: one exception is enough.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The totality of a specified range, with no member or part left out.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Universal quantification over a domain; true of an empty domain by default.
- [LIN] A determiner and pronoun with collective readings (all together) and distributive readings (each one).

**Count condition:** The range is specified and every member or part of it is covered.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "All" without a stated range cannot be checked. Collective and distributive readings can differ in truth. All is not any (a free choice of one) and not most.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0012 | allele

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** One of two or more alternative versions of a gene found at the same place on a chromosome.

**Example:** She carries one allele for blue eyes and one for brown.

**RRGM bridge:** A variant held at a fixed position. The position is the identity of the slot, and the allele is what currently fills it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One of the alternative sequence versions of a gene at a given locus.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Diploid organisms carry two alleles per autosomal locus, the same or different; allele frequencies describe populations.

**Count condition:** A locus and two or more sequence variants found there in a population.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An allele is not the gene, not the genotype (the pair carried), and not a mutation (the event that creates a variant). Dominant and recessive describe effects on the phenotype, not frequency or worth.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A358 | alley

**Common meaning:** A narrow passageway between or behind buildings.

**Example:** The bins are kept in the alley behind the shop.

**RRGM bridge:** A constrained route. Its figurative form, the blind alley, names a path worth recording once walked so that it is not walked again.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A narrow passage running between or behind buildings or bounded sides.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A lane bounded closely on both sides; also a long narrow lane for bowling or skittles.
- [LIN] "Blind alley": a course of action or inquiry that leads nowhere. "Up one's alley": suited to one's interests.

**Count condition:** A passage, narrow relative to streets, bounded along both sides.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An alley is not an aisle (between rows, usually indoors) and not a street. A blind alley is closed at one end.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A109 | alliance

**Common meaning:** A union or association formed for mutual benefit, especially between countries or organisations.

**Example:** The two parties formed an alliance before the election.

**RRGM bridge:** A declared joining in which the parties stay separate and commit to act together on stated matters.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A declared union of distinct parties who commit to act together toward shared interests while remaining separate.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Military, political, or family unions; the working bond between a therapist and client.
- [ECO] A cooperative arrangement between firms that stops short of merger.
- [BIO] A stable cooperative group among animals, such as male dolphins that cooperate over years.

**Count condition:** Distinct parties, a shared interest, and a commitment to joint action.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An alliance is not a merger and not a friendship; rivals can be allied. A coalition is usually temporary and tied to one issue. Obligations extend only as far as what was declared.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0013 | allostasis

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The process by which the body keeps itself viable by actively changing its internal settings to meet expected demands.

**Example:** Rising blood pressure before a race is allostasis at work.

**RRGM bridge:** Stability reached by moving. The system returns to its function by changing its state, which is the reverse of holding a state fixed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Regulation that maintains viability by changing internal operating points in anticipation of demand.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Coordinated shifts in hormones, autonomic tone, and metabolism ahead of or during challenge; the cumulative cost is called allostatic load.
- [COG] In predictive accounts of the brain, the anticipatory budgeting of bodily resources.

**Count condition:** A regulated variable whose target is itself shifted, with evidence that the shift anticipates demand.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not homeostasis, which holds variables near fixed set points by correcting deviations. Allostatic load is the cost of the process, not the process. The parallel with RRGM return-to-function is structural only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM parallel [REL]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A049 | allow

**Common meaning:** To give permission for; to make possible; to set aside or take into account.

**Example:** The rules allow two substitutions.

**RRGM bridge:** The basic permission verb. It belongs to whoever holds the boundary and is separate from making the thing possible in fact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To grant, or refrain from preventing, so that something may occur.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Permission given by a person or authority with standing to refuse.
- [DIG] A rule or list entry that permits an action, origin, or value.
- [ECO] To accept a claimed deduction or expense; to set aside a margin (allow ten per cent for waste).
- [LIN] To concede a point (I allow that he tried).

**Count condition:** A party with standing to prevent, a thing permitted, and the grant or the withholding of prevention.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Allow is not enable (make able), not require, not approve, and not cause. Allowed by rule is not possible in fact. Not preventing counts as allowing only where one could and was entitled to prevent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A273 | allowability

**Common meaning:** The quality of being allowable under a set of rules.

**Example:** The examiner questioned the allowability of two of the patent claims.

**RRGM bridge:** Eligibility for permission, assessed against the rules before any permission is given.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The status of an act or claim as eligible to be permitted under a stated rule set.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Whether a claim, amendment, or item of evidence may be admitted under the governing rules.
- [ECO] Whether a cost or deduction qualifies under tax or contract rules.
- [DIG] Whether a requested action falls within what a policy can permit, evaluated before a grant.

**Count condition:** A rule set, a type of act or claim, and a determination under the rules.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Allowable is not allowed: eligibility is not the grant. Not advisable. Allowability under one rule set does not carry to another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A271 | allowance

**Common meaning:** An amount of something that is permitted or given regularly; a margin or adjustment made for circumstances.

**Example:** The baggage allowance is twenty kilograms.

**RRGM bridge:** Permission with a number on it: a bounded amount granted ahead of use.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A bounded amount or margin granted in advance, within which something may be used, taken, or varied.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [PHY], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Money given regularly; an amount exempt from tax; a tradeable permit to emit a set quantity.
- [PHY] In engineering, a planned difference between the sizes of mating parts.
- [REL] Making allowances: judging conduct more leniently because of circumstances.
- [DIG] A quota of usage or spending that one party authorises another to draw on.

**Count condition:** A granting party, a quantity or margin, and the use it covers.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An allowance is planned and granted; a tolerance is the permitted unplanned variation. Not an entitlement owed by right. Unused allowance may or may not carry over, by the terms of the grant.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A272 | allowed

**Common meaning:** Permitted.

**Example:** Dogs are not allowed in the garden.

**RRGM bridge:** The standing result of a permission: at the reference time the grant exists and has not been withdrawn.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Currently holding permission under the governing authority or rules.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [PHY], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Permitted by a person or body with authority over the matter.
- [DIG] Included in the set of permitted values, origins, or operations.
- [PHY] Consistent with the selection rules or constraints of a system, as with allowed transitions and allowed energies.
- [FOR] Satisfying the constraints that define the permitted moves or values.

**Count condition:** A rule or authority, a time, and the thing's inclusion among what is permitted.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Allowed is not able, not obliged, and not thereby right or safe. In physics "forbidden" often means strongly suppressed, and no authority is involved.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10
