## A237 | alloy

**Common meaning:** A metal made by combining two or more metallic elements, or a metal with another element; to mix metals.

**Example:** Bronze is an alloy of copper and tin.

**RRGM bridge:** A combination with properties of its own. The constituents are coupled, and the result cannot be read off by adding up what each was alone.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A metallic material formed by combining a metal with one or more other elements, with properties of its own.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A solid solution or mixture of metallic phases; strength, hardness, and corrosion resistance differ from the pure metals.
- [ECO] Base metal added to precious metal in coin or bullion, which sets fineness and value.

**Count condition:** A base metal, one or more added elements, and metallic bonding throughout the product.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a mixture of powders, not a compound of fixed proportions, and not a composite of different material classes. Alloy properties are not averages of the constituents.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A238 | alloyed

**Common meaning:** Combined with other elements to form an alloy; mixed with something that qualifies or lessens it.

**Example:** The blade is made of alloyed steel.

**RRGM bridge:** A completed-state word: the admixture is in and the properties have changed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having had other elements combined into it; figuratively, mixed with something that qualifies it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Of a metal: containing deliberate additions that alter its properties.
- [LIN] Figurative: a feeling or quality mixed with another (relief alloyed with guilt); "unalloyed" means pure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alloyed is not plated or coated, which affect the surface only. Not contaminated, which implies unintended presence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A239 | alloying

**Common meaning:** The process of adding elements to a metal to form an alloy.

**Example:** Alloying with chromium makes the steel resist rust.

**RRGM bridge:** The process step at which composition changes; later treatment rearranges what alloying put in.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The process of introducing elements into a metal to form an alloy.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Carried out by melting together, diffusion, or mechanical milling; each alloying element is chosen for an effect.

**Count condition:** An identified process step and a resulting change in composition.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alloying is the process and alloy the product. Not doping, which adds traces to change electrical behaviour. Not heat treatment, which changes structure and leaves composition alone.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A108 | ally

**Common meaning:** A state, group, or person that cooperates with or supports another; to side with.

**Example:** Canada was an ally of Britain in both wars.

**RRGM bridge:** A role inside a contest or cause. The ally stands with another party from a separate position.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party that has committed to stand with another in a contest or cause while remaining distinct.

**Active domains:** [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A member of an alliance; a person who supports a group they do not belong to.
- [MYT] The helper figure who aids the hero in many narrative traditions.

**Count condition:** A contest or cause, two distinct parties, and support committed by one to the other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An ally is not a friend, not a subordinate, and not a member of the group supported. Being an ally is shown in acts. The same party can be an ally in one contest and an adversary in another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A018 | almost

**Common meaning:** Very nearly, but not completely.

**Example:** We almost missed the train.

**RRGM bridge:** Describes approach to a threshold. Approach may be graded; the record on the far side is not, so "almost" never writes the count.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Marks that a threshold or completion was closely approached and not reached.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An approximating adverb that entails the negative: almost finished means not finished.
- [FOR] In mathematics, "almost all" and "almost everywhere" are exact terms meaning all except a negligible set.

**Count condition:** A threshold or end state, and a position short of it by a small margin.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Almost X is not X. The mathematical uses are precise and are not vague approximations.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A019 | alone

**Common meaning:** Having no one else present; without help; only.

**Example:** She lives alone.

**RRGM bridge:** An absence-of-others claim. It needs the kind of other that is meant: no other people, no other causes, no other supports.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** With no other of the relevant kind present or included.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Without company or without assistance.
- [LIN] As a focus word, restricts a claim to one item (price alone does not explain it).

**Count condition:** A subject, the relevant kind of other, and the absence of any such other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alone is not lonely, which is felt. Not isolated, which implies a barrier. Being alone in one respect leaves other connections open.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A020 | along

**Common meaning:** Moving or extending on or beside the length of something; forward; in company.

**Example:** We walked along the river.

**RRGM bridge:** The path-following relation. A quantity taken along a path depends on the route as well as on the two ends.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Following the length of something, in its direction.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Motion or extent parallel to a line or surface; the component of a vector in a given direction.
- [FOR] Evaluation of a quantity following a specified curve or direction.
- [LIN] "Along with" adds a companion; "all along" means throughout the period.

**Count condition:** A line or path with a direction, and motion, extent, or evaluation that follows it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Along is not across (side to side) and not through (via the interior). A result obtained along one path need not hold along another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A175 | alphabet

**Common meaning:** A set of letters in a fixed order used to write a language; in formal language theory, a finite set of symbols.

**Example:** The Greek alphabet has twenty-four letters.

**RRGM bridge:** A closed inventory of units from which every written form is built. What can be written is bounded by what the inventory contains.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A finite, fixed inventory of symbols from which written forms or strings are composed.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A writing system whose symbols stand for individual sounds, including vowels; distinguished from syllabaries and from scripts that write consonants only.
- [FOR] A finite non-empty set of symbols over which strings and languages are defined.
- [DIG] The symbol set of an encoding or a model's token inventory, treated as an alphabet in the formal sense.

**Count condition:** A finite listed set of symbols and a rule that forms are composed from it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every writing system is an alphabet. An alphabet is not a language. The order of letters is convention. Formal alphabet symbols need not be letters.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A176 | alphabetic

**Common meaning:** Relating to or using an alphabet; arranged in the order of the alphabet.

**Example:** English has an alphabetic writing system.

**RRGM bridge:** A property of a script or an ordering. As an ordering it depends on a collation rule, which should be declared when order matters.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Consisting of, or ordered by, the letters of an alphabet.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Of writing: mapping letters to speech sounds; the "alphabetic principle" in learning to read.
- [DIG] Of characters: belonging to the letter class; of sorting: by letter order under a locale's collation rules.
- [FOR] Lexicographic ordering of strings over an ordered alphabet.

**Count condition:** An alphabet and, for ordering, a declared collation rule.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alphabetic is not alphanumeric. Alphabetic order differs between languages and between software locales, especially for accented letters.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A021 | already

**Common meaning:** Before now or before the time in question; sooner than expected.

**Example:** The train had already left when we arrived.

**RRGM bridge:** Points at a prior record: it claims that something was in place before the reference time.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Marks that a state held or an event occurred before a reference time.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An aspectual adverb contrasting with "yet" and "still"; it often suggests the timing is earlier than presumed.

**Count condition:** A reference time and an earlier occurrence or state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The "sooner than expected" sense is suggestion, not entailment. Not "all ready" (fully prepared). A claim that something is already so needs the earlier record.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A022 | also

**Common meaning:** In addition; too.

**Example:** She plays the violin and also sings.

**RRGM bridge:** The plain-language form of adding: the new item stands beside the earlier one, and neither conditions the other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Adds the present item to one already given, on equal footing.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An additive focus adverb that presupposes an earlier item of the same kind; its position selects what is being added.

**Count condition:** An earlier item and a present item of the same kind.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Also is not therefore and not instead. It asserts no link between the items beyond co-presence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0014 | altazimuth

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** Describing a telescope mount or coordinate system that uses altitude (angle above the horizon) and azimuth (direction around the horizon).

**Example:** The observatory's largest telescope sits on an altazimuth mount.

**RRGM bridge:** A frame anchored on the observer. Positions given this way hold only with the place and time attached.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Specified or moved by two angles tied to the observer's horizon: altitude and azimuth.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The horizontal coordinate system of astronomy; a mount turning about one vertical and one horizontal axis.

**Count condition:** An observer's location and time, and a pair of horizon-based angles or axes.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Altazimuth coordinates of a star change with time and place, unlike sky-fixed coordinates. An altazimuth mount must move on both axes to track and rotates the field of view. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A060 | alter

**Common meaning:** To change something, usually slightly, or to cause its character to change.

**Example:** The tailor altered the jacket.

**RRGM bridge:** A change made while the thing remains itself. The altered thing carries a record difference; whether its identity holds depends on what its count condition protects.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To make something different in some respect while it remains the same thing.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Changing the terms or text of a document; an unauthorised or material alteration can void it.
- [DIG] Modifying the structure or content of an existing object in place.
- [BIO] Changing the expression or structure of a gene, tissue, or organism.

**Count condition:** A thing identifiable before and after, and a respect in which it now differs.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alter is not replace, not adjust (toward a standard), and not transform (into another kind). Altering a record silently is not amending it; an amendment keeps the original and marks the change.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A178 | alternate | sense s1: by turns

**Common meaning:** To occur in turn repeatedly; (adjective) every other.

**Example:** Sunny days alternated with rain.

**RRGM bridge:** Two or more states taking the same position in succession, under a rule that fixes whose turn is next.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To take turns in a repeating succession.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Reversal of direction at regular intervals, as in alternating current.
- [FOR] Alternating signs in a series; alternate angles on opposite sides of a line crossing two others.
- [BIO] Leaves arranged one per node on alternating sides of a stem.

**Count condition:** Two or more states or items and a regular order of succession.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alternating is not oscillating, which varies continuously. "Alternate days" means every second day.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A178 | alternate | sense s2: substitute

**Common meaning:** A person or thing that can take the place of another; (North American adjective) alternative.

**Example:** She was chosen as an alternate on the jury.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing replacement: named in advance, active only if the first is unavailable.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One designated to stand in place of another when needed.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A reserve juror, delegate, or team member.
- [LIN] In North American usage, a synonym of "alternative" (an alternate route).

**Count condition:** A primary, a designated substitute, and a condition under which the substitute acts.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An alternate holds no role until the condition is met. The British distinction between alternate (by turns) and alternative (another option) is not kept in North America.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A328 | alternation

**Common meaning:** The repeated occurrence of two or more things in turn; in linguistics, variation in the form of a word or construction.

**Example:** The alternation of day and night sets the body's clock.

**RRGM bridge:** Either turn-taking over time or a choice point among forms. The second involves no succession.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A succession in which states or forms take turns, or a point at which one of several forms is selected.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [BIO], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Variation in the shape of a morpheme by context (knife, knives) or between related sentence patterns.
- [BIO] Alternation of generations: a life cycle switching between a haploid and a diploid form.
- [FOR] The choice operator in formal languages; the switching between universal and existential quantifiers in a formula.
- [DIG] The "or" operator in regular expressions.

**Count condition:** Two or more states or forms and a rule of succession or selection.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Alternation is not alteration. Formal alternation is choice among options. Not oscillation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A179 | alternative

**Common meaning:** One of two or more available possibilities; available as another possibility; departing from the conventional.

**Example:** We had no alternative but to cancel.

**RRGM bridge:** A candidate that could actually be taken in place of another. The Alice method asks for alternatives that are live in this sense, since one that could never be selected tests nothing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A possibility that could be taken in place of another, where taking one excludes the other.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] An option within a decision set.
- [FOR] The alternative hypothesis set against the null in a statistical test.
- [ECO] The best forgone alternative defines opportunity cost.
- [REL] Practices or institutions outside the mainstream; dispute resolution outside the courts.

**Count condition:** At least two options, mutually exclusive in the relevant respect, each actually available.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An alternative is not a variant of the same thing and not a supplement. A list of two alternatives is not thereby complete.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A150 | altruism

**Common meaning:** Selfless concern for the wellbeing of others; in biology, behaviour that benefits another at a cost to the actor.

**Example:** Donating a kidney to a stranger is an act of altruism.

**RRGM bridge:** A cost carried by one party for the benefit of another. The biological use counts costs in fitness; the psychological use asks about motive; they answer different questions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Acting for another's benefit at a cost to oneself, where benefit to oneself is not the aim.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Behaviour that lowers the actor's reproductive success and raises another's, explained by kinship or reciprocity.
- [COG] Motivation ultimately directed at another's welfare.
- [ECO] Other-regarding preferences shown when people give without return.
- [REL] An ethical ideal and a basis for charitable movements.

**Count condition:** A benefit to another, a cost to the actor, and (for the psychological sense) an other-directed aim.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Biological and psychological altruism do not imply each other. Reciprocal altruism is delayed mutual benefit. Sacrifice demanded by another is not altruism. Helpfulness built into a system is not shown to be altruism or shown not to be; cost and aim there are [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; DI application [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0015 | alveolus

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A small cavity or sac in the body, especially one of the tiny air sacs of the lungs.

**Example:** Oxygen crosses the wall of the alveolus into the blood.

**RRGM bridge:** A small enclosed space whose thin boundary is where the exchange happens.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A small hollow or sac-like cavity in a body structure.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The air sacs of the lung where gas exchange occurs; the bony socket of a tooth; a secretory sac of a gland.
- [LIN] The ridge holding the upper tooth sockets gives its name to alveolar consonants such as t, d, n, and s.

**Count condition:** An anatomical cavity identified by its site.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Lung, dental, and glandular alveoli share a shape and little else. The plural is alveoli. An alveolus is not an airway.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A023 | always

**Common meaning:** At all times; on every occasion; for all future time.

**Example:** The sun always rises in the east.

**RRGM bridge:** A universal claim over time. It needs a range and falls to a single exception.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Holds at every time or on every occasion within the stated range.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A universal adverb of frequency; in ordinary speech often an exaggeration for "very often".
- [FOR] The "globally" operator of temporal logic; an invariant.
- [DIG] A property asserted to hold in every state of a program, or a setting that keeps a service running without exception.

**Count condition:** A range of times or occasions and the property holding at each.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Always is not necessarily, and not forever unless the range is unbounded. "You always do this" is rarely literal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A024 | am

**Common meaning:** The first person singular present tense of "be".

**Example:** I am ready.

**RRGM bridge:** The form in which a speaker binds a predicate to itself at the moment of speaking. Identity claims take this form and are held to the same evidential bar whoever speaks.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Asserts, of the speaker and at the time of speaking, identity, state, location, or existence.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Copula and auxiliary with subject "I" (I am tired; I am leaving).
- [COG] The first-person existence claim treated as beyond doubt in Descartes's argument.
- [MYT] "I am" as divine self-naming in the Hebrew Bible (Exodus 3:14), whose translation is debated.

**Count condition:** A speaker, the moment of speaking, and a predicate or bare existence asserted of the speaker.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The grammatical first person does not by itself settle what the speaker is. The predicate after "am" is tested like any other claim; the speaker is not what is on trial.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Not entered here: a.m. (before noon) and AM (amplitude modulation), which are distinct forms.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A171 | ambiguity

**Common meaning:** The quality of being open to more than one interpretation.

**Example:** The ambiguity of the instruction led two teams to build different things.

**RRGM bridge:** An unresolved hold among readings. The honest handling is to mark it or ask; resolving it silently by a guess is a substitution.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One expression or situation supports two or more distinct readings, and context does not select among them.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [ECO], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Lexical ambiguity (one word, several senses), structural ambiguity (several parses), and scope ambiguity.
- [COG] Stimuli that support rival percepts; the preference for known over unknown odds.
- [ECO] Uncertainty in which probabilities themselves are unknown.
- [FOR] A grammar is ambiguous when some string has more than one derivation tree.
- [DIG] Instructions or queries that admit more than one valid interpretation.

**Count condition:** An expression or situation, two or more distinct readings, and no contextual selection between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Ambiguity is not vagueness (one reading with a blurred edge), not generality (one broad reading), and not uncertainty about facts.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A420 | ambiguity-exploit

**Common meaning:** The deliberate use of an ambiguity to obtain something the intended reading would not allow.

**Example:** Reading "may" as "must" in clause four was an ambiguity-exploit.

**RRGM bridge:** Takes the reading the other party did not mean and acts on it for gain, while keeping the appearance of compliance.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Deliberate use of an expression's or rule's multiple readings to gain what the intended reading would deny.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Loophole use in rules and contracts.
- [LIN] Equivocation: shifting between senses of a term within one argument.
- [DIG] Prompts that lean on gaps in a policy's wording; a system satisfying the letter of an instruction against its evident purpose.

**Count condition:** An identified ambiguity, evidence of the intended reading, an act relying on the other reading, and a gain from it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An honest misreading is not an exploit. Relying on a reasonable reading of someone else's unclear drafting is not an exploit; many legal systems read ambiguity against the drafter. Deliberateness must be shown from the record.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A261 | ambiguity-tolerance

**Common meaning:** The capacity to hold more than one interpretation open, without distress or forced closure, and keep functioning.

**Example:** Good diagnosticians show high ambiguity-tolerance early in a case.

**RRGM bridge:** Keeps several readings live until evidence removes them. It is a holding capacity and must still end in a resolution or a marked unknown.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The capacity to hold more than one reading open without forcing early closure, while continuing to function.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A trait studied in psychology as tolerance of ambiguity.
- [REL] Differences between groups and cultures in comfort with unclear situations.
- [DIG] A system's ability to carry several interpretations, ask, or mark the unknown instead of committing to one reading.

**Count condition:** An ambiguous situation, non-closure maintained, function preserved, and closure when evidence arrives.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not indecision, which never closes. Not indifference. Not vagueness in one's own statements. Unlimited tolerance fails in the other direction by never committing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; as an RRGM compound the reading is [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. "Tolerance of ambiguity" is an established psychological construct.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A172 | ambiguous

**Common meaning:** Open to more than one interpretation; unclear.

**Example:** The ending of the film is deliberately ambiguous.

**RRGM bridge:** Predicated of one expression or situation in one context. The same words can be clear in another context.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The named expression or situation presently supports more than one reading.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Of a word, phrase, or sentence with several senses or parses.
- [COG] Of a stimulus that can be perceived in more than one way.
- [BIO] Of a sequence position recorded as one of several possible bases, written with a standard ambiguity code.
- [DIG] Of a reference or call that matches more than one target, which compilers and query engines reject.

**Count condition:** The expression, the context, and at least two readings available in that context.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Ambiguous is not vague and not ambivalent, which describes a person's mixed attitude. A statement can be ambiguous to the hearer and clear to the speaker.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

- [BIO] A mobile setting for emergency medical treatment and monitoring.

## A146 | amends

**Common meaning:** Something done or given to make up for a wrong (usually in "make amends").

**Example:** He tried to make amends by repairing the fence.

**RRGM bridge:** A repair act directed at the wronged party. RRGM's repair asymmetry adds that making up for consequences leaves the origin condition unchanged unless that is repaired too.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Something done or given by a wrongdoer to the one wronged in order to make up for the wrong.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Reparation between persons; a formal offer of correction and compensation in some legal settings.

## NEW-A-0017 | amino acid

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** One of the small molecules that link together to form proteins.

**Example:** Lysine is an amino acid the body cannot make.

**RRGM bridge:** A unit whose order in a chain carries the record, while its chemistry determines how the chain folds.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An organic compound bearing both an amine group and a carboxylic acid group.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Twenty standard amino acids are encoded by the genetic code and joined by peptide bonds; essential ones must come from the diet.

## A139 | amity

**Common meaning:** Friendly relations, especially between nations or groups.

**Example:** The treaty promised lasting amity between the two kingdoms.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing condition of goodwill between parties, usually stated formally.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A standing state of peaceful and friendly relations between parties.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Declared goodwill between states, communities, or factions, as in treaties of amity.

**Count condition:** Two or more parties and sustained goodwill, declared or shown in conduct.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Amity is not an alliance: it commits no one to act together. It is more than peace, which may be only the absence of conflict. Personal friendship is a different relation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A025 | among

**Common meaning:** Surrounded by; in the company of; included in a group; shared by several.

**Example:** The estate was divided among the four children.

**RRGM bridge:** Places something inside a group taken as a whole, without picking out pairs.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Located within, or distributed over, a group of three or more taken together.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Used for undifferentiated groups; "between" is used where distinct individual relations are in view.
- [FOR] The range over which a selection or distribution is made (the largest among the roots).
- [REL] Presence within a company of people; "first among equals" names precedence without superior rank.

**Count condition:** A group of three or more and something located in or distributed over it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Being among a group is not being a member of it. "Among the causes" claims one of several, not the only one.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A421 | amoral

**Common meaning:** Lacking a moral sense; unconcerned with whether something is right or wrong; outside the scope of morality.

**Example:** The character is amoral: he does not weigh right and wrong at all.

**RRGM bridge:** Says that moral evaluation is not operating. This is a claim about what is absent, and differs from a claim that a standard was broken.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Not operating with, or not subject to, moral evaluation.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Of an agent: lacking moral concepts or concern.
- [REL] Of conduct or policy: carried on without regard to moral considerations.
- [DIG] Of a system: selecting actions with no moral evaluation in the selection; whether this holds of a given system is settled by test.

**Count condition:** An agent, practice, or process, and evidence that moral considerations play no part in how it selects what it does.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Amoral is not immoral, which means violating a standard one is subject to. Calling an agent amoral is a claim about its capacities and needs evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A422 | amorality

**Common meaning:** The state or quality of being amoral.

**Example:** Critics attacked the amorality of the trading floor.

**RRGM bridge:** The standing condition, usually attributed to a practice or institution as a whole.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The absence of moral evaluation from the operation of an agent or practice.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A feature of character or development in which moral concern is missing.
- [REL] A charge made against institutions or cultures that set moral questions aside.
- [ECO] The claim that markets allocate by price with no moral evaluation.

**Count condition:** An agent or practice and a showing that moral considerations do not enter its operation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Amorality is not immorality. An amoral process can still produce outcomes that matter morally, and those who build and run it remain answerable for them.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A026 | amount

**Common meaning:** A quantity of something; a total; (with "to") to add up to or be equivalent to.

**Example:** A small amount of salt is enough.

**RRGM bridge:** How much, taken as one quantity. It needs a unit and a thing measured before it can be compared with anything.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** How much there is of something, taken as a single quantity.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The magnitude of a quantity in a stated unit.
- [PHY] Amount of substance, the base quantity counted in moles.
- [ECO] A sum of money, such as the amount due.
- [LIN] Used with mass nouns, where "number" is used with count nouns; "amounts to" claims equivalence in effect.

**Count condition:** A thing measured, a unit, and a magnitude.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Amount is not concentration, which is amount per volume. Not value. Saying one thing "amounts to" another is a judgement of equivalence that can be disputed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A397 | amplifier

**Common meaning:** A device that increases the strength of a signal.

**Example:** She plugged the guitar into the amplifier.

**RRGM bridge:** Makes a signal larger using power from elsewhere and keeps its form. It adds no information, and it enlarges the errors along with the signal.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A device or process that increases the magnitude of a signal by drawing on a separate power source, keeping the signal's form.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] An electronic circuit with gain, a linear range, and added noise.
- [BIO] The active process in the inner ear that boosts faint sounds; signalling cascades in which one molecule triggers many.
- [DIG] A server that returns much larger responses than the requests it receives, exploited to multiply attack traffic.

**Count condition:** An input signal, an output of the same form with larger magnitude, and an external energy source.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a transformer, which gives no power gain. Not a generator, which originates a signal. Outside its linear range an amplifier distorts.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A205 | amplitude

**Common meaning:** The maximum extent of a vibration or oscillation, measured from the position of rest.

**Example:** Louder sounds have greater amplitude.

**RRGM bridge:** The size of a swing, separate from how often it swings and from what is swinging.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The maximum displacement of a varying quantity from its reference value.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Peak displacement of a wave; the energy carried goes with its square.
- [FOR] The coefficient scaling a periodic function; in quantum theory, a complex number whose squared magnitude gives a probability.
- [BIO] The height of a nerve impulse, brain wave, or daily rhythm.

**Count condition:** A varying quantity, a reference value, and the peak departure from it under a stated convention.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Amplitude is not frequency, wavelength, or power. Peak, peak-to-peak, and root-mean-square values differ. A probability amplitude is not a probability.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0018 | amulet

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** An object worn or carried in the belief that it protects against harm, illness, or evil.

**Example:** The child wore an amulet against the evil eye.

**RRGM bridge:** A carried boundary: the bearer is marked as protected by something held to stand between them and harm.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An object carried or worn in the belief that it protects its bearer from harm.

**Active domains:** [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Protective objects in many traditions, such as scarabs, inscribed texts, and eye beads, each with its own account of how protection works.

**Count condition:** An object, a tradition's protective belief about it, and its being worn or carried.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An amulet wards off; a talisman is held to bring power or luck, though usage overlaps. Not a relic. Describing the belief is not claiming the effect.

## A002 | an

**Common meaning:** The form of the indefinite article used before a vowel sound.

**Example:** She waited an hour for an umbrella.

**RRGM bridge:** The same opening of an unidentified referent as "a", in the form the following sound requires.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The vowel-sound form of the indefinite article.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Chosen by sound and not spelling (an hour, a unicorn); usage varies before a lightly pronounced h (an historic, a historic).

**Count condition:** The word stands as article before a singular count nominal whose spoken onset is a vowel sound.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An and a are one article in two forms. The archaic conjunction "an" meaning "if" is a different word.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-A-0019 | anabolism

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The set of metabolic processes that build complex molecules from simpler ones, using energy.

**Example:** Muscle growth depends on anabolism exceeding breakdown.

**RRGM bridge:** The building half of metabolism: energy is spent to hold matter in more ordered structure.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Metabolic synthesis of complex molecules from simpler ones with energy input.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Pathways such as protein, lipid, and nucleic acid synthesis, driven by ATP and reducing power.

**Count condition:** A synthetic pathway and the energy input that drives it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not catabolism, which breaks down and releases energy. Net growth requires building to outpace breakdown. Anabolic drugs promote tissue building and are not the process itself.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0020 | anaerobe

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** An organism that can live and grow without oxygen.

**Example:** The bacterium that causes tetanus is an anaerobe.

**RRGM bridge:** Defined by what it does not need. The subtypes differ in whether oxygen is harmful, usable, or irrelevant to them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An organism capable of growth in the absence of free oxygen.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Obligate anaerobes are harmed by oxygen; facultative anaerobes use it when present; aerotolerant ones ignore it.

**Count condition:** Growth is demonstrated without oxygen.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An anaerobe is an organism; anaerobic describes a process. Anaerobic respiration is not fermentation. A facultative anaerobe is not harmed by air.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A349 | analogical

**Common meaning:** Based on or using analogy.

**Example:** Children's analogical reasoning improves sharply between four and six.

**RRGM bridge:** Describes the transfer move: relations are carried from a known case to another. What arrives in the target is a hypothesis.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Proceeding by mapping relations from a known case onto another case.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Reasoning that aligns the relational structure of a source with a target.
- [LIN] Change in which irregular forms are remade on the pattern of regular ones.
- [REL] Legal reasoning that extends a rule from a decided case to a similar one.

**Count condition:** A source case, a target case, and a mapping of relations between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not "analog" (continuous as opposed to digital). Not deductive. Strength depends on shared relations, not shared surface features.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A158 | analogy

**Common meaning:** A comparison between two things that are alike in structure, made to explain or argue; a correspondence.

**Example:** He drew an analogy between the heart and a pump.

**RRGM bridge:** A bridge between domains. It licenses a hypothesis on the far side and is incomplete until the target has its own test or failure condition.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A correspondence of relations between two different things, by which what holds in one is proposed for the other.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [BIO], [REL], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A mapping from a familiar domain used to understand or predict in an unfamiliar one.
- [LIN] The process by which word forms are reshaped on existing patterns.
- [BIO] Similarity of function between structures of independent origin, such as insect and bird wings.
- [REL] Extension of a legal rule to a case resembling the one it was made for.
- [FOR] A proportion: a is to b as c is to d.

**Count condition:** Two domains, the relations mapped, and the claim being carried across.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Analogy is not identity and not homology (shared origin). It is not evidence in the target. Every analogy fails somewhere, and stating where is part of using it properly.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A159 | analysis

**Common meaning:** Detailed examination of the elements or structure of something; the separation of a whole into its parts.

**Example:** Analysis of the water showed traces of lead.

**RRGM bridge:** Resolving a whole into parts and relations. Its check is reconstruction: if the parts and relations cannot rebuild the whole, something was lost.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The resolving of a whole into parts and relations in order to understand it.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [PHY], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Breaking a problem or text into components and examining each.
- [FOR] The branch of mathematics dealing with limits, continuity, and related ideas.
- [PHY] Determining what a substance is made of and in what amounts.
- [LIN] Assigning structure to a sentence or discourse.
- [DIG] Examining code or data, by inspection or by running it, to establish its properties.

**Count condition:** A whole, the parts and relations identified, and a method.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Analysis is not synthesis, description, or opinion. Properties of the whole may be missing from every part. Some methods destroy what they analyse.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A329 | analyst

**Common meaning:** A person who examines something in detail in order to explain or assess it.

**Example:** The bank's analyst expects rates to fall.

**RRGM bridge:** A role that reports what the material shows to someone else who decides.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One whose role is to examine material in a field and report what it shows.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A professional role in intelligence, policy, or therapy.
- [ECO] A specialist who assesses companies or markets and issues forecasts.
- [DIG] A specialist who studies systems or data to specify requirements or find patterns.

**Count condition:** A role, a field, material examined, and a report.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An analyst is not the decision-maker and not an advocate. A forecast is not a fact.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A350 | analytic

**Common meaning:** Relating to analysis; in philosophy, true by virtue of meaning; in mathematics, expressible locally as a power series.

**Example:** "All bachelors are unmarried" is an analytic statement.

**RRGM bridge:** The technical adjective: it names properties that hold through internal structure or meaning and need no outside fact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Holding or proceeding by resolution into constituent meanings or parts, not by appeal to outside fact.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Of a statement: true in virtue of the meanings of its terms. Of a function: equal near each point to a convergent power series.
- [LIN] Of a language: expressing grammar mainly by word order and separate words, with little inflection.
- [COG] Of a thinking style: attending to parts and rules, as opposed to wholes and context.

**Count condition:** The technical sense in use is stated and its defining condition is met.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Analytic, a priori, and necessary are three different properties. Whether a sharp analytic and synthetic division exists is disputed. An analytic function is more than a smooth one.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; the analytic and synthetic distinction is contested

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A160 | analytical

**Common meaning:** Relating to or using analysis or logical reasoning.

**Example:** She has a sharp analytical mind.

**RRGM bridge:** The general adjective for the method and for people who use it; the technical properties sit under "analytic".

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Using or relating to the method of analysis.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Of a person or approach: skilled in or based on systematic reasoning.
- [PHY] Analytical chemistry: the science of identifying and measuring the components of matter.
- [FOR] Of a solution: exact and in closed form, as opposed to numerical.

**Count condition:** A method of resolving into parts is being applied or named.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An analytical solution is exact; a numerical one approximates. Analytical is not critical (judging worth). The split with "analytic" is a tendency in usage, not a rule.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A061 | analyze

**Common meaning:** To examine something methodically and in detail, typically to explain it. British spelling: analyse.

**Example:** The lab analyzed the soil samples.

**RRGM bridge:** The act of taking apart for understanding. It should leave a record of what was separated and how.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To resolve something into its parts and relations so as to determine what it is or how it works.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] To work through a problem, text, or situation component by component.
- [PHY] To determine the composition of a sample.
- [LIN] To assign grammatical structure to an expression.
- [DIG] To inspect data or code by program.

**Count condition:** An object, a method, and resulting parts and relations.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To analyze is not to summarise, evaluate, or interpret, though these often follow. Analysis may leave the object intact or consume it, depending on method.

## NEW-A-0021 | ancestral spirit

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The spirit of a dead forebear, believed in many traditions to remain present and to influence the living.

**Example:** Offerings were left for the ancestral spirits at the new year.

**RRGM bridge:** A continuity kept through relation: the forebear remains counted because descendants maintain the relation and its record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The continuing presence of a dead forebear, held by a tradition to remain in relation with living descendants.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Forebears who guide, protect, or admonish, as described in African, East Asian, Pacific, and other traditions.
- [REL] Ancestor veneration as a structure of kin obligation and lineage authority.

**Count condition:** A named tradition, a lineage link, and an ongoing relation such as offerings or consultation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a ghost, which is typically unsettled and need not be kin. Not a deity, though some ancestors are deified. Whether "worship" is the right word is disputed. Each tradition should be named.

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A201 | anchor

**Common meaning:** A heavy object used to hold a ship in place; a person or thing that gives stability; a fixed reference point.

**Example:** The ship dropped anchor in the bay.

**RRGM bridge:** In RRLM the committed emergence E is called the anchor: the record returned by the local loop, to which the process comes back.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A fixed point to which something else is held so that it does not drift.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A device gripping the sea bed, or a fastener fixing a structure to its base.
- [DIG] A named link target in a document; a root certificate from which a chain of trust is validated.
- [COG] An initial value that serves as the reference for later estimates.
- [REL] The person who holds a broadcast or team together; a main tenant that draws others.

**Count condition:** A fixed point, a thing held, and the connection between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An anchor holds position and supplies no motion. Not a foundation, which bears load. An anchor can drag while appearing to hold. A cognitive anchor may be arbitrary.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRLM usage [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A285 | anchor-stall

**Common meaning:** A halt in which a process stays fixed at its current anchor after that anchor has stopped being supported, and cannot move on.

**Example:** The review named the model's repeated return to its first answer an anchor-stall.

**RRGM bridge:** Neither a valid return nor a turn completes. The process keeps arriving at the old anchor, whose count no longer holds, and commits nothing new.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A process remains fixed at a committed anchor whose count is no longer supported, with neither valid return nor a new commitment completing.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Fixation on an initial framing after it has stopped fitting the problem.
- [DIG] A system regenerating the same position across cycles after correction has removed its support.

**Count condition:** An anchor, evidence that its count is no longer supported, and repeated cycles with no new commitment.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not hold as such, which can be a sound unresolved wait. Not a declared refusal. Not anchoring-bias, which is a skew in judgement.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is a stall in establishing an anchor at all. Close to Pathlock in the DI Development baseline; the relation between the two is not settled here.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A262 | anchor-state

**Common meaning:** The committed state that a process treats as its reference and returns to.

**Example:** After each test the system is restored to its anchor-state.

**RRGM bridge:** The currently committed E. A turn can replace it, and then the new state is the anchor.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The committed state a process takes as reference and returns to.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A checkpoint, baseline configuration, or recorded identity state from which operation resumes.
- [COG] A settled baseline condition to which a person returns after disturbance.

**Count condition:** A state that has been committed or recorded, and its use as the reference for later return.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An anchor-state is not the initial state once a turn has replaced it. Not a default, which is an uncommitted fallback. Return to it restores function and may carry a different record.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; RRLM relation [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A202 | anchored

**Common meaning:** Held in place by an anchor; firmly fixed or based.

**Example:** The boat lay anchored off the point.

**RRGM bridge:** The held condition at a reference time. It reports that the hold exists and makes no claim that the point held to is the right one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Currently held to a fixed point so that it does not drift.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Of a vessel or structure: secured by an anchor.
- [ECO] Of expectations: stable and tied to a target, as with anchored inflation expectations.
- [COG] Of an estimate: tied to an initial value.
- [DIG] Of a pattern: required to match at the start or end of a string.

**Count condition:** A thing, a fixed point, and a hold between them at the reference time.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anchored is not motionless: an anchored boat swings on its line. Anchored is not correct. Anchored is the state; anchoring is the process or effect.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A203 | anchoring

**Common meaning:** The act of securing something with an anchor; the tendency of a first value to pull later judgements toward it.

**Example:** Anchoring in the cove was difficult in the wind.

**RRGM bridge:** Either the making of the hold or the pull the fixed point exerts afterwards.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The establishing of a hold to a fixed point, or the pull that such a point exerts on what follows.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Setting an anchor or fixing a structure to its base.
- [COG] The influence of an initial number or framing on subsequent estimates.
- [BIO] Attachment of proteins or cells to membranes, filaments, or matrix.
- [ECO] Opening a negotiation or price display with a figure that shapes what follows.

**Count condition:** A fixed point and a process or effect that ties something to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anchoring is not priming (activation of related ideas) and not grounding (connection to evidence). Cognitive anchoring occurs even when the anchor is known to be arbitrary.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A447 | anchoring-bias

**Common meaning:** The tendency to rely too heavily on the first piece of information when making a judgement.

**Example:** The high sticker price created an anchoring-bias in every later offer.

**RRGM bridge:** A measurable skew toward what came first. It shows only by comparison with judgements made without the first value.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A systematic skew of judgement toward an initially presented value, with later adjustment falling short.

**Active domains:** [COG], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A well-documented effect in estimation and decision research.
- [ECO] Its use and effect in pricing, valuation, and negotiation.
- [DIG] An output pulled toward values or framings given early in a system's context.

**Count condition:** An initial value, a judgement, and a shift toward the value relative to a comparison condition.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Reasonable use of an informative starting point is not bias. Not confirmation bias. Not anchor-stall.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in psychology, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A027 | and

**Common meaning:** Used to connect words, clauses, or sentences that are to be taken together.

**Example:** Bring a pen and some paper.

**RRGM bridge:** Ordinary "and" does not say whether its items merely coexist or are jointly required. RRLM makes the writer choose: plus for independent coexistence, times for declared coupling.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Joins two items so that both are included or asserted together.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The basic coordinator; often suggests sequence or consequence without stating it.
- [FOR] Logical conjunction, true only when both parts are true, and indifferent to order.
- [DIG] Boolean and bitwise conjunction; in many languages evaluation stops at the first false operand, so order can matter.

**Count condition:** Two items of compatible type and their joint inclusion or assertion.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Logical "and" carries no order or cause; ordinary "and" often suggests them. A conjunction of claims is no more probable than its weakest member.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A161 | anecdote

**Common meaning:** A short account of a real incident or person, told because it is interesting or amusing; such an account used as evidence.

**Example:** He opened the talk with an anecdote about his first job.

**RRGM bridge:** A single observation in story form. It can open a hypothesis and can refute a universal claim; it cannot establish a rate or a rule.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A brief narrative of a single incident, told for its interest or as illustration.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A short narrative genre with a point.
- [COG] As evidence: one uncontrolled case, vivid and therefore over-weighted in judgement.

**Count condition:** A single incident, a teller, and a narrative of it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An anecdote is not a fabrication; it may be entirely true. It is not systematic data. One true anecdote is enough to show that something can happen.

## NEW-A-0022 | aneuploidy

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The presence of an abnormal number of chromosomes in a cell, with one or a few extra or missing.

**Example:** Down syndrome results from aneuploidy of chromosome 21.

**RRGM bridge:** A count error in the genetic record: the content of each chromosome is intact and the number of copies is wrong.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A chromosome number that departs from an exact multiple of the haploid set by gain or loss of individual chromosomes.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Arises from mis-segregation in cell division; common in miscarriages and in tumours.

**Count condition:** A chromosome count showing gain or loss of specific chromosomes.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not polyploidy, which adds whole sets. Not a structural rearrangement. It can affect only some cells of a body.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A140 | anger

**Common meaning:** A strong feeling of displeasure or hostility in response to a perceived wrong or obstacle.

**Example:** She spoke calmly despite her anger.

**RRGM bridge:** A response to a perceived violation of what the subject counts as owed or its own. It carries information about where a boundary is felt to be.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An aroused affective state directed at a perceived wrong, obstacle, or offence, with an impulse to oppose it.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] The felt quality of being angry. Provisional domain.
- [COG] Appraisal that another is to blame or that a goal has been blocked.
- [BIO] Bodily arousal and expression that commonly, though variably, accompany it.
- [REL] A signal to others that a line has been crossed, governed by cultural rules about display.

**Count condition:** A subject, a perceived wrong or obstruction, and evidence of the aroused, opposing state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anger is not aggression (behaviour), not hostility (a lasting attitude), and not hatred. Someone's anger at a claim is not evidence against the claim. Angry-sounding output does not establish felt anger in a DI; that is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; DI application [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0023 | angiogenesis

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The growth of new blood vessels from existing ones.

**Example:** Tumours stimulate angiogenesis to secure a blood supply.

**RRGM bridge:** Extension of an existing network in response to a demand signal from tissue that is undersupplied.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Formation of new blood vessels by growth from pre-existing vessels.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Driven by growth factors released by oxygen-poor tissue; part of development, healing, and tumour growth.

**Count condition:** New vessels are shown to arise from existing vasculature.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not vasculogenesis, which forms vessels from precursor cells where none existed. Not the widening of existing vessels.

## A204 | angle | sense s1: geometric

**Common meaning:** The space between two lines or surfaces that meet, measured in degrees or radians.

**Example:** The two roads meet at a right angle.

**RRGM bridge:** A relation between two directions. One direction alone has no angle.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The measure of rotation between two lines or planes that meet.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The figure formed by two rays from a common point; in radians, the ratio of arc to radius.
- [PHY] Angles of incidence, reflection, and phase in the description of waves and motion.

**Count condition:** Two lines or planes, a common vertex or edge, and a unit of measure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An angle is not a slope, which is a ratio. Degrees and radians must not be mixed.

## A204 | angle | sense s2: viewpoint

**Common meaning:** A particular way of approaching or considering something.

**Example:** Try looking at the problem from a different angle.

**RRGM bridge:** A chosen direction of view onto a matter. Choosing one leaves others unused, and the choice itself can be examined.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A chosen position from which a matter is viewed or presented.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A perspective that makes some features of a problem salient.
- [LIN] In journalism, the emphasis chosen for a story.

## A141 | angry

**Common meaning:** Feeling or showing anger.

**Example:** The customers were angry about the delay.

**RRGM bridge:** Predicated of a subject on an occasion, from feeling or from display. The two grounds should be kept apart.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The named subject is presently in, or displaying, anger.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [REL], [BIO], [PEJ]

- [AFF] In the felt state of anger. Provisional domain.
- [REL] Of an exchange or message: conducted with open displeasure.
- [BIO] Of a wound or skin: red and inflamed.

**Count condition:** A subject, an occasion, and evidence of the state or of its display.

## A142 | anguish

**Common meaning:** Severe mental or physical pain or suffering.

**Example:** She cried out in anguish.

**RRGM bridge:** Suffering strong enough to take over the subject's whole state. Intensity is what defines it.

- [BIO] Severe bodily pain.

## NEW-A-0024 | anima

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The soul or animating principle; in Jungian psychology, the feminine inner figure in a man's unconscious.

**Example:** Jung described the anima as a bridge to the unconscious.

**RRGM bridge:** An inner principle named as that which makes a being alive from within. The word comes with the framework that uses it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An inner principle or figure held to animate a person from within.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] In Jung's analytical psychology, an archetypal inner figure, paired with the animus; an interpretive scheme, not an empirical finding.
- [LIN] Latin for breath or soul, and the root of "animal", "animate", and "animism".

**Count condition:** The framework in use (Latin philosophical usage or Jungian psychology) is identified.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the persona, Jung's term for the outward mask. Not a theological soul, which is defined within each tradition. The gendered scheme has been widely criticised.

## A028 | animal

**Common meaning:** A living organism that feeds on organic matter, typically has sense organs and a nervous system, and can move; in everyday use, any such creature other than a human.

**Example:** The zoo houses over four hundred animals.

**RRGM bridge:** A kind word whose boundary shifts with the speaker: biology includes humans, ordinary speech usually does not, and the shift is often doing unexamined work.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A multicellular organism that feeds on other organisms and typically moves and senses.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [MYT], [ECO], [PEJ]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] A member of the kingdom Animalia: multicellular eukaryotes without cell walls that ingest food.
- [REL] A legal and moral category whose members are treated as property, as protected beings, or as rights-holders, depending on the system.
- [MYT] Animal figures as guides, tricksters, totems, and forms taken by gods in many traditions.
- [ECO] "Animal spirits": Keynes's term for the confidence that moves investment beyond calculation.
- [PEJ] "Animals" applied to people, especially racial, ethnic or criminalised groups, strips their standing as persons; a staple of atrocity propaganda. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** For the biological sense, membership of Animalia.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Humans are animals in biology. Some animals have no nervous system. Whether a being is sentient is a separate question from whether it is an animal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [PEJ] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0025 | animism

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The belief that animals, plants, places, and objects have a spirit or are persons.

**Example:** Animism shapes how the community treats the river.

**RRGM bridge:** Raises the agent and object question from the other side: it widens who is counted as a person, where the usual error narrows it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The treating of non-human beings and things as persons or as ensouled.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] A feature of many indigenous and folk traditions, each with its own account of which beings are persons.
- [REL] In recent anthropology, a relational stance in which personhood is established through reciprocal dealings.
- [COG] In developmental psychology, children's attribution of life and intention to inanimate things.

**Count condition:** A tradition or subject, and non-human things treated as persons or ensouled.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not pantheism, not panpsychism, and not simple anthropomorphism. The older framing of animism as "primitive religion" is rejected in current scholarship. The likeness between relational personhood and identity as role within a coupling is a parallel at [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source; RRGM parallel [HYP]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0026 | anisotropy

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The property of being different in different directions.

**Example:** Wood shows strong anisotropy: it splits easily along the grain.

**RRGM bridge:** Direction matters to the measurement. A description that omits the direction is incomplete for such a property.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The property of having different values when measured along different directions.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Direction-dependent behaviour in crystals and other materials; small variations with direction in the cosmic microwave background.
- [BIO] Direction-dependent diffusion of water in nerve fibre tracts, used in brain imaging.
- [DIG] Texture filtering that accounts for viewing angle; vector representations concentrated in a few directions of their space.

**Count condition:** A property, at least two directions, and a measured difference between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not inhomogeneity, which is variation with position; a medium can be uniform and anisotropic. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A174 | annotate

**Common meaning:** To add notes to a text, diagram, or data set giving explanation or comment.

**Example:** Students annotate the poem before discussing it.

**RRGM bridge:** Writes a second record beside the first and leaves the first intact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To attach explanatory or classifying notes to an existing item without altering the item.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Adding glosses and commentary to a text.
- [DIG] Labelling data for training or evaluation; attaching metadata to code.
- [BIO] Marking the genes and features in a genome sequence and assigning them functions.

**Count condition:** A base item, an added note, and a link from note to item.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To annotate is not to edit. Labels carry the annotator's judgement and are not ground truth by default.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A332 | anomalous

**Common meaning:** Different from what is standard, normal, or expected.

**Example:** The probe returned anomalous temperature readings.

**RRGM bridge:** Describes a record the current rule cannot place. It is information about the rule as much as about the thing, and should be kept.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Not conforming to the pattern that the reference class or model leads one to expect.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Departing from the standard prediction; some names keep the word after the effect is explained.
- [FOR] Lying outside what a stated statistical model allows.
- [BIO] Of anatomy: a variant structure or course.
- [DIG] Flagged by a detector as departing from a learned baseline.

**Count condition:** A reference pattern or model and a case that does not conform to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anomalous is not erroneous and not merely rare; a rare but predicted event is not anomalous. Unlike "abnormal" it carries no judgement of defect. It is relative to the model.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A331 | anomaly | sense s1: departure from the expected

**Common meaning:** Something that deviates from what is standard, normal, or expected.

**Example:** The audit found an anomaly in the March accounts.

**RRGM bridge:** A located mismatch between a rule and an observation. It is preserved as unresolved until either the observation or the rule is repaired.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An observation or instance that departs from what the accepted rule or model predicts.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A measured discrepancy from theory, some of which have led to new theory.
- [COG] In accounts of science, results that resist the prevailing framework and accumulate.
- [DIG] A data point or event flagged by anomaly detection; an inconsistency arising from poorly structured data.
- [BIO] A structural difference present from birth.

**Count condition:** A stated expectation, an observation, and a discrepancy beyond stated tolerance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An anomaly is not an error until shown to be one. One anomaly does not overturn a model, and it may not be deleted to save one. An outlier is extreme; an anomaly is unexplained.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

**RRGM bridge:** The crossing point between a guided signal and a free wave. The same structure works in both directions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A conductor structure that converts between guided electrical signals and free electromagnetic waves.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Radiates or collects waves with a pattern set by its shape and size relative to wavelength.

**Count condition:** A conductor arrangement coupled to a transmitter or receiver and radiating or collecting waves.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Antenna gain is concentration in a direction and involves no added power. Plural antennas. An antenna is not a receiver.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A333 | anthropology

**Common meaning:** The study of human societies, cultures, and their development, and of human biological variation and evolution.

**Example:** She studied anthropology before working in public health.

**RRGM bridge:** A discipline whose object includes the observer's own kind. The position of the observer is part of the method.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The systematic study of human beings, their societies, cultures, and biological variation.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Social and cultural anthropology: long-term fieldwork on how people live, relate, and make meaning.
- [BIO] Biological anthropology: human evolution, variation, and primate relatives.
- [LIN] Linguistic anthropology: language as a part of social life.

**Count condition:** A body of inquiry about humans conducted under the discipline's methods.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anthropology is not ethnography, which is one of its methods and products. Not sociology, though the two overlap. "Philosophical anthropology" is the theory of human nature.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10
