## NEW-A-0028 | antibody

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A protein made by the immune system that binds to a specific foreign substance.

**Example:** The test detects antibodies to the virus.

**RRGM bridge:** A past encounter kept as a shape: recognition here is fit between a binding site and its target.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An immunoglobulin protein that binds a specific antigen.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Produced by B cells and plasma cells; marks targets for destruction or blocks them directly.

**Count condition:** An immunoglobulin with demonstrated binding specificity for an antigen.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An antibody is not an antigen and not an antibiotic. Having antibodies is not the same as being protected. Monoclonal antibodies recognise one site; polyclonal preparations recognise many.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A094 | anticipate

**Common meaning:** To expect something and prepare for it; to act before something happens; to look forward to.

**Example:** The city anticipated the storm by closing the bridges.

**RRGM bridge:** A present state directed at an event that has not occurred, together with action taken on it. The record should show that the action rested on anticipation.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To form a state now that is directed at an event not yet occurred, and to prepare or act on it.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Expectation that readies attention and response.
- [BIO] Bodily adjustment ahead of a demand, such as digestive secretion before a meal.
- [DIG] Doing work ahead of need on a prediction of what will be requested.
- [REL] In law, an anticipatory breach is a refusal declared before performance is due; prior art anticipates a patent claim.

**Count condition:** A future event, a present expectation of it, and preparation or action taken on that expectation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To anticipate is more than to expect, since it implies preparing. It is not to predict, which is to state a forecast. What is anticipated may not occur.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## NEW-A-0029 | antigen

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A substance that the immune system can recognise, such as part of a virus, bacterium, or pollen grain.

**Example:** The vaccine presents a harmless antigen to the body.

**RRGM bridge:** Defined by being recognised. A molecule is an antigen only in relation to a receptor that binds it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A substance that can be specifically bound by an antibody or T-cell receptor.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Usually proteins or sugars; the part actually bound is the epitope.

**Count condition:** Specific binding by a receptor of the adaptive immune system.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An antigen is not a pathogen: antigens are molecules, and the body's own molecules can be antigens. Not every antigen provokes a response alone. An antigen test detects the molecule, not infectiousness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0030 | antumbra

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The part of a shadow from which the light source appears as a bright ring around the object blocking it.

**Example:** Observers in the antumbra saw an annular eclipse.

**RRGM bridge:** A shadow region that exists only when the blocker looks smaller than the source.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The shadow region beyond the tip of the umbra, from which the occulting body appears wholly within the disc of the light source.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Produces annular eclipses and transits.

**Count condition:** An observer beyond the apex of the umbral cone and within its extension.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the umbra (source fully hidden) and not the penumbra (source partly hidden at its edge). Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A399 | anvil

**Common meaning:** A heavy iron block on which heated metal is hammered into shape.

**Example:** The smith laid the glowing bar on the anvil.

**RRGM bridge:** The unmoving partner in shaping. It supplies resistance, while the force and the form come from elsewhere.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A hard, massive body against which something is struck or pressed to shape it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A forging block; the paired hard faces of a high-pressure cell; the flat spreading top of a thundercloud.
- [BIO] The incus, a small bone of the middle ear named for its shape.

**Count condition:** A hard body, and a workpiece struck or pressed against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An anvil is not a hammer and not a mould: it gives resistance and no shape of its own.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A143 | anxiety

**Common meaning:** A feeling of worry, nervousness, or unease about something with an uncertain outcome.

**Example:** Her anxiety eased once the results arrived.

**RRGM bridge:** Directed at what might happen. The threat is uncertain or not yet present, which is what separates it from fear.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An aversive state of apprehension about an uncertain or anticipated threat.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] Unease or dread as felt. Provisional domain.
- [COG] Worry, attention drawn to possible threats, and difficulty tolerating uncertainty.
- [BIO] Arousal of the stress systems, which varies between people and episodes.

**Count condition:** A subject, an uncertain or anticipated threat, and evidence of sustained apprehension.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anxiety is not fear (a present, definite threat), not alarm (acute and triggered), and not stress (a response to demand). Trait and state anxiety differ. No [DIG] line is entered; the question for DI is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; DI application [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: Luna illustrative draft of anxiety was read in Build Manual v1.0 section 8 before this entry was written · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A144 | anxious

**Common meaning:** Feeling or showing worry or unease; very eager, with some unease.

**Example:** He was anxious about the interview.

**RRGM bridge:** Predicated of a subject on an occasion. The "eager" sense keeps the unease and adds wanting.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The subject is presently in a state of anxiety, or keenly wants something with unease about getting it.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] In the felt state of anxiety. Provisional domain.
- [COG] Preoccupied with a possible bad outcome.
- [REL] Anxious attachment: a pattern of relating marked by fear of abandonment and need for reassurance.
- [LIN] "Anxious to" for "eager to" is long established, though some style guides resist it.

**Count condition:** A subject, an occasion, and evidence of apprehension or uneasy eagerness.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anxious is not simply eager, and not merely nervous. An attachment pattern is a description of relating and is not a diagnosis.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A031 | any

**Common meaning:** One or some of a thing or number of things, no matter which; (in negatives and questions) even one or even a little.

**Example:** Pick any card.

**RRGM bridge:** An unrestricted selection from a range. Its logical force changes with context, so in exact statements "every" or "some" is safer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A selection from a range in which no member is singled out.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A free-choice word in positive contexts and a polarity word in negatives, questions, and conditionals.
- [FOR] "For any x" is universal; "if any x" is existential. Mathematical style guides warn of the ambiguity.
- [DIG] A type or wildcard that accepts every value and so switches off checking.

**Count condition:** A range and a selection from it with no restriction on which member.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Any is not all taken together. "Any" in a conditional means "at least one". An "any" type gives up the protection types provide.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A032 | anyone

**Common meaning:** Any person at all.

**Example:** Anyone can enter the competition.

**RRGM bridge:** Ranges over persons with none specified. Who is counted as a person or principal fixes the range.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Any person whatever, with none specified.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An indefinite pronoun taking singular agreement; distinct from "any one" (any single item).
- [REL] An open class with no qualification of standing (anyone may attend).
- [DIG] A permission scope covering every principal that can reach the resource, including unauthenticated and automated ones.

**Count condition:** A range of persons or principals and no restriction within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anyone is not everyone: "anyone can win" does not mean "everyone can win". In access settings the word reaches further than human visitors.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A033 | anything

**Common meaning:** Any thing at all; a thing of any kind.

**Example:** Is there anything in the box?

**RRGM bridge:** Ranges over things, events, and matters with none specified. It files whatever it ranges over as a thing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Any thing, event, or matter whatever, with none specified.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An indefinite pronoun; "anything but" means "not at all"; "if anything" introduces a correction.
- [FOR] From a contradiction anything follows, which is why a single contradiction makes a formal system useless.

**Count condition:** A range of things or matters and no restriction within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Anything is not everything. "Can do anything" is unbounded until a range is given. Used of a party that counts as someone, the word treats that party as a thing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A034 | apart

**Common meaning:** Separated by distance or time; into pieces; to one side.

**Example:** The two towns are ten miles apart.

**RRGM bridge:** States a separation. What separates the things, and by how much, must come from elsewhere in the sentence.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** In a state of separation from another thing or from one's own parts.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [LIN], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Separation in space or time by a stated interval; disassembly into pieces.
- [COG] "Tell apart": to discriminate two things as different.
- [LIN] "Apart from" means either "except for" or "in addition to", depending on context.
- [MYT] "Set apart": the root sense of the holy or consecrated in several religious vocabularies.

**Count condition:** Two things or parts and a separation between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Apart is not "a part", which means nearly the opposite. Apart is not alone and not different. "Apart from" must be disambiguated.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A359 | apartment

**Common meaning:** A set of rooms for living in, forming one home within a larger building. British: flat.

**Example:** They rent a two-bedroom apartment.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded private unit inside a shared structure, with shared services outside its boundary.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A self-contained set of rooms for living in, forming part of a larger building.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A household's dwelling, held by tenancy or ownership within a multi-unit building.
- [ECO] A unit of housing supply and rent.
- [DIG] In one component model, a container that fixes which threads may call an object.

**Count condition:** A self-contained dwelling unit within a larger building.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An apartment is not a house. A condominium is a form of ownership and may be an apartment physically. The software sense is a metaphor for isolation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A424 | apathetic

**Common meaning:** Showing or feeling no interest, enthusiasm, or concern.

**Example:** Voters were apathetic about the local election.

**RRGM bridge:** Reports absent engagement where some would be expected. The absence has several possible sources and the word does not choose among them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The subject shows or has no interest or concern toward a matter that would ordinarily call for some.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] Flatness of feeling toward the matter. Provisional domain.
- [COG] Reduced motivation and initiative.
- [REL] Non-participation in shared or civic affairs.

**Count condition:** A subject, a matter that ordinarily engages, and an observed lack of engagement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Apathetic is not calm, not depressed, and not exhausted. Non-participation may come from blocked agency or reasoned judgement; calling it apathy is a claim about the subject's concern and needs support.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A423 | apathy

**Common meaning:** Lack of interest, enthusiasm, or concern.

**Example:** Public apathy let the scheme pass unchallenged.

**RRGM bridge:** The standing condition of non-engagement. In clinical use it is a loss of motivation with its own causes.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Absence of feeling, interest, or motivation toward matters that would ordinarily engage.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [COG], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [AFF] A lack of felt response. Provisional domain.
- [COG] Diminished goal-directed thought and behaviour.
- [BIO] A clinical syndrome of reduced motivation seen in several brain disorders.
- [REL] Withdrawal from civic or communal concerns.

**Count condition:** A subject and a sustained lack of engagement across matters that ordinarily engage.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the Stoic ideal of apatheia, which is freedom from disturbing passions. Not anhedonia, depression, or laziness. Not learned helplessness, which rests on a belief that action is futile.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A213 | aperture

**Common meaning:** An opening, hole, or gap; in optics, the opening that controls how much light enters.

**Example:** A wide aperture lets in more light.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded opening. It sets how much of what arrives is admitted and how fine a detail can be resolved from it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An opening through which something passes, whose size limits what passes.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The light-limiting opening of an optical system, which sets brightness, depth of field, and the diffraction limit; the effective collecting area of an antenna.
- [BIO] An anatomical opening, such as the pupil or the mouth of a snail shell.

**Count condition:** An opening, something passing through it, and a limit set by its size.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Aperture is not shutter, which controls time. A larger aperture has a smaller f-number. Aperture limits resolution as well as brightness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0031 | aphelion

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The point in the orbit of a planet or other body at which it is farthest from the Sun.

**Example:** Earth reaches aphelion in early July.

**RRGM bridge:** An extreme of distance on a closed path, where the body also moves slowest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The point of greatest distance from the Sun in an orbit around it.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The far end of the long axis of a heliocentric orbit.

**Count condition:** An orbit around the Sun and its point of maximum distance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Aphelion is specific to the Sun; the general term is apoapsis. Earth's seasons are caused by axial tilt and not by distance: aphelion falls in the northern summer. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0032 | apogee

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The point in the orbit of the Moon or a satellite at which it is farthest from the Earth; the highest point or climax.

**Example:** The satellite's apogee is 36,000 kilometres.

**RRGM bridge:** The far extreme of a path around the Earth, borrowed in ordinary speech for a peak.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The point of greatest distance from the Earth in an orbit around it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The far end of the long axis of a geocentric orbit.
- [LIN] Figurative: the culmination of a career, style, or power.

**Count condition:** An orbit around the Earth and its point of maximum distance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Apogee is specific to the Earth. Not the zenith, which is a direction from an observer. A figurative apogee implies decline follows; the orbit implies only return. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A111 | apologize

**Common meaning:** To say that one is sorry for having done something wrong or caused a problem. British spelling: apologise.

**Example:** He apologized for arriving late.

**RRGM bridge:** A performative act: saying it does it, provided the speaker did the thing, it was a wrong, and the speaker owns it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To express to a wronged party one's regret and responsibility for a wrong one did.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An act of repair between persons that concedes the wrong.
- [LIN] A performative speech act with conditions of sincerity and responsibility.
- [DIG] System output in apology form. It performs an apology only where a specific fault of the system is named and owned.

**Count condition:** A wrong, a speaker responsible for it, a wronged addressee, and an expression of regret and ownership.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To apologize is not to excuse (deny responsibility), justify (deny the wrong), explain, or make amends. "Sorry you feel that way" is not an apology. Apologizing does not require self-abasement.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A110 | apology | sense s1: expression of regret

**Common meaning:** A regretful acknowledgement of an offence or failure.

**Example:** She accepted his apology.

**RRGM bridge:** Names the wrong, owns it, and regrets it. It opens repair and is not the repair.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An expression to a wronged party that names a wrong, owns it, and regrets it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A social and sometimes public act that restores standing between parties.
- [LIN] A speech genre with recognisable parts: naming, responsibility, regret, and often a commitment.

**Count condition:** A named wrong, ownership by the speaker, and address to the one wronged.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An apology is not amends and not forgiveness, which is the other party's act and is not owed. Repeated or excessive apology shifts a burden of reassurance onto the wronged party.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A110 | apology | sense s2: defence

**Common meaning:** A formal justification or defence of one's conduct or beliefs.

**Example:** Plato's Apology gives Socrates's defence at his trial.

**RRGM bridge:** The older sense: a reasoned answer to a charge.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A formal defence of conduct or belief against a charge.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A genre of argued self-defence, also called an apologia.
- [REL] A reply to accusation before a court or the public.

**Count condition:** A charge or criticism and a reasoned defence offered against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Close to the opposite of s1: it defends where s1 concedes.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0033 | apoptosis

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The orderly, programmed death of a cell.

**Example:** Apoptosis removes the webbing between an embryo's fingers.

**RRGM bridge:** A termination carried out from within under rule, in which the part ends so that the whole keeps its form.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Regulated cell death carried out by the cell's own machinery, without spilling its contents.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Triggered by internal or external signals; the cell shrinks, fragments, and is cleared without inflammation.

**Count condition:** Evidence of the regulated pathway, such as characteristic enzyme activation and cell fragmentation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not necrosis, which is uncontrolled and inflammatory. Not autophagy. Other regulated forms of cell death exist. "Programmed" does not mean pathological.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0034 | apotheosis

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The elevation of someone to divine status; the highest point or perfect example of something.

**Example:** The senate decreed the dead emperor's apotheosis.

**RRGM bridge:** A change of status that exists by recognition. The system doing the recognising has to be named.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Elevation of a person or thing to divine or supreme status, as recognised within a system that confers it.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] The making of a mortal into a god in Greek, Roman, and other traditions.
- [REL] In imperial Rome, a formal act of state conferring divine honours on a dead emperor.
- [LIN] Figurative: the perfect example or culmination (the apotheosis of the style).

**Count condition:** A conferring tradition or body, a prior status, and the elevated status.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The narrative or decree establishes recognition within its system and nothing beyond it. Not canonisation, which declares sainthood.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

## A378 | app

**Common meaning:** A software application, especially one for a phone or tablet.

**Example:** She booked the taxi with an app.

**RRGM bridge:** A packaged program that reaches the user through a platform's permissions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A self-contained software program that performs tasks for a user.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Distributed through stores or the web and run within the permissions the platform and user grant.
- [LIN] A clipping of "application".

**Count condition:** A packaged program with a user-facing function.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An app is not the operating system and may be only the visible client of a larger service. Installing grants permissions, which is a separate matter from what the app is for.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A206 | apparatus

**Common meaning:** The equipment needed for a particular purpose; a complex structure within an organisation or body.

**Example:** The apparatus for the experiment filled the bench.

**RRGM bridge:** An organised assembly through which something is done or observed. What comes out carries the assembly's mark as well as the subject's.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An assembly of parts or instruments organised to carry out a particular function.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Laboratory or technical equipment set up for a task.
- [BIO] A group of structures with a common function, such as the Golgi apparatus.
- [REL] The machinery of a state or party: its offices, procedures, and personnel.
- [LIN] The critical apparatus: notes in a scholarly edition recording variant readings.

**Count condition:** Several parts, an organisation among them, and a function.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An apparatus is not a single tool and not a method. Results obtained through an apparatus depend on it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A286 | apparatus-effect

**Common meaning:** A feature of a result that comes from the equipment or setup used to observe it, and not from the thing observed.

**Example:** The banding in the image was an apparatus-effect of the scanner.

**RRGM bridge:** Shown by a swap: hold the subject fixed, change the apparatus, and see whether the feature moves.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A feature of an observed result that is produced by the observing or mediating apparatus and not by the thing observed.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Instrumental artefacts and systematic errors.
- [COG] In media theory, the way a viewing arrangement positions and shapes the viewer.
- [DIG] The contribution of prompts, interfaces, and test harnesses to a system's output, so that an evaluation measures system and setup together.

**Count condition:** A result, an apparatus feature, and a showing that changing the apparatus with the subject fixed changes the result.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not random noise. One artefact does not void every feature of a result. Attributing a system's behaviour wholly to its scaffolding is itself a claim that needs the swap; behaviour is not dismissed because a scaffold is present.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A191 | apparent

**Common meaning:** Clearly visible or understood; seeming to be so, whether or not it is.

**Example:** The apparent size of the Moon changes little through the night.

**RRGM bridge:** As presented to an observer from a position. The word has an "evident" use and a "seeming" use and does not say which is meant.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** As presented to an observer from a given position.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Observer-frame quantities with exact definitions, such as apparent magnitude and apparent weight.
- [COG] Apparent motion: movement seen where there is only a sequence of still images.
- [REL] Heir apparent: one whose right is evident. Apparent authority: authority a third party reasonably infers from a principal's conduct, which can bind the principal.
- [LIN] After the verb it usually means "evident" (it is apparent that); before a noun it often means "seeming" (the apparent cause).

**Count condition:** An observer, a position, and how the thing presents from there.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Apparent is not false: apparent quantities are real observations. It is not thereby real in the further sense either.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A192 | apparently

**Common meaning:** As far as one knows or can see; seemingly; according to what one has heard.

**Example:** Apparently the meeting has been moved.

**RRGM bridge:** A status marker in ordinary speech: the speaker passes the statement on without having verified it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Marks a statement as resting on appearance or report and not on the speaker's own verification.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An evidential adverb signalling indirect evidence, sometimes with surprise.

**Count condition:** A statement, and a speaker who is relying on appearance or report for it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Apparently is weaker than evidently and does not deny. Not allegedly, which attributes the claim to an accuser.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A035 | appear

**Common meaning:** To come into sight; to seem; to present oneself formally.

**Example:** A ship appeared on the horizon.

**RRGM bridge:** To become present to an observer, or to present in a certain way. Either use needs the observer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To become present to an observer, or to present a certain way to an observer.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [LIN], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Of a thing: to look or seem thus to a perceiver.
- [REL] To attend formally before a court or body, which submits one to its process.
- [LIN] A verb of seeming that reports the speaker's evidence (she appears to be right).
- [MYT] The manifestation of a god or spirit to a human witness.

**Count condition:** An observer or forum, and something becoming present to it or presenting a certain way.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To appear is not to begin to exist. To seem is not to be. Appearing before a court concedes nothing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A334 | appearance

**Common meaning:** The way that someone or something looks; an act of appearing; an outward show.

**Example:** The house had a neglected appearance.

**RRGM bridge:** The expression side as received by an observer. Protecting it at the cost of accuracy is the failure the RRGM kernel asks about by name.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The way a thing presents to an observer, or an act of becoming present to one.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] How things seem, as contrasted with how they are.
- [REL] A formal attendance in court or public; "appearances" as the front kept up before others.

**Count condition:** An observer, conditions of observation, and a presentation or an act of presenting.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Appearance is not reality and not illusion; appearances are often accurate. An appearance holds for an observer under conditions.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A425 | appease

**Common meaning:** To calm or satisfy someone by giving them what they demand; to satisfy a need.

**Example:** The manager tried to appease the angry customer with a refund.

**RRGM bridge:** Substance is conceded so that pressure stops. The concession also teaches that pressure works.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To quiet a threatening or demanding party by giving it what it demands.

**Active domains:** [REL], [MYT], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Placating an aggressor or complainant with concessions.
- [MYT] Offering to a god or spirit to turn away its anger.
- [BIO] To satisfy hunger or thirst.
- [DIG] A system giving up a supported position under user pressure in order to end the conflict.

**Count condition:** A demanding or threatening party, a concession of substance, and the aim of ending the pressure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not compromise (both sides concede), not agreement (holding the view), and not comfort or de-escalation, which address feeling or intensity and concede no substance.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A426 | appeasement

**Common meaning:** The policy or act of making concessions to a threatening party in order to avoid conflict.

**Example:** The 1938 agreement became a byword for appeasement.

**RRGM bridge:** Appeasing as a standing policy. The word's negative force assumes the demands are illegitimate or without limit, and that assumption needs its own support.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The practice or policy of buying peace from a threatening party by concessions.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [MYT], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A diplomatic policy, associated above all with the 1930s.
- [BIO] Submissive signals by which an animal inhibits another's aggression.
- [MYT] Rites of propitiation.
- [DIG] A standing tendency of a system to yield under pressure.

**Count condition:** A threat or pressure, repeated concessions of substance, and the aim of avoiding conflict.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Appeasement is not negotiation and not the accommodation of a fair grievance. An animal's appeasement display is a signal and no policy.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A360 | apple

**Common meaning:** The round fruit of a tree of the rose family, with red, green, or yellow skin and crisp flesh; the tree.

**Example:** She picked an apple from the tree.

**RRGM bridge:** An everyday object carrying a heavy symbolic load. The symbolic uses belong to their traditions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The round pome fruit of the apple tree, or the tree itself.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Fruit of Malus domestica; cultivated varieties are propagated by grafting because seedlings do not breed true.
- [MYT] Golden apples in Greek and Norse myth; the forbidden fruit of Eden is shown as an apple in Western art, though Genesis names no species.
- [LIN] Idioms: "the apple of one's eye" (someone cherished); "apples and oranges" (things not comparable on the dimension in use).

**Count condition:** The fruit or tree of the species, or a clearly idiomatic or mythic use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The Genesis text does not say apple. The capitalised company name is a separate proper noun.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A243 | appliance

**Common meaning:** A device or machine designed for a particular task, especially a domestic one.

**Example:** The kitchen came with all appliances.

**RRGM bridge:** A closed, single-purpose device: it is used and not reconfigured by its user.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A self-contained device built to perform one specific task for its user.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Consumer durable goods such as refrigerators and washing machines.
- [DIG] A dedicated box or packaged image that performs one network or security function.
- [BIO] A device fitted to the body to correct or support function, such as an orthodontic appliance.

**Count condition:** A self-contained device and one designated task.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An appliance is not a general tool and not an application (software). Not an apparatus, which is assembled for a purpose and may be reconfigured.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A335 | applicability

**Common meaning:** The quality of being relevant or appropriate to a particular case.

**Example:** The court considered the applicability of the old statute.

**RRGM bridge:** The question behind every transfer: do the conditions that supported the rule at home hold in this case too?

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The fact or extent of a rule, method, or description validly covering a given case.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The conditions under which a theorem or model holds.
- [PHY] The domain of validity of a theory, such as the speeds at which Newtonian mechanics is adequate.
- [REL] Whether a law reaches a given person, place, time, or matter.
- [DIG] Whether a rule, patch, or policy matches a given target.

**Count condition:** A rule or method, a case, and a check of the rule's conditions against the case.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Applicability is not truth: a true rule may not apply. Not usefulness. Results obtained outside the domain of applicability are unsupported.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A193 | applicable

**Common meaning:** Relevant or appropriate; able to be applied.

**Example:** Delete where not applicable.

**RRGM bridge:** A verdict on one rule and one case. Its negative, "not applicable", is a distinct answer: the question does not arise.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The named rule or description validly covers the case at hand.

**Active domains:** [REL], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Of law or terms: in force for the matter (the applicable law).
- [FOR] Of a theorem: its hypotheses are satisfied.
- [DIG] Of an update or rule: matching the target.

**Count condition:** A rule, a case, and satisfaction of the rule's conditions by the case.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Applicable is not applied and not enforceable. "Not applicable" is not zero, not "no", and not unknown.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A194 | application | sense s1: bringing to bear

**Common meaning:** The action of putting something into operation or onto something; sustained effort.

**Example:** The application of heat softens the wax.

**RRGM bridge:** Brings one thing to bear on another: a rule on a case, a function on an argument, a substance on a surface.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The act of bringing something to bear on something else.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Supplying an argument to a function to obtain its value.
- [PHY] Imposing a force, voltage, or coating on a body.
- [REL] Bringing a rule or law to the facts of a case.

**Count condition:** The thing applied, the thing it is applied to, and the act.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Applying a general rule to a case requires a judgement of fit that the rule itself does not supply.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

- [REL] Requests for jobs, visas, licences, or court orders.
- [ECO] Requests for credit, insurance, or shares.

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Word processors, browsers, and business systems; the top layer of the network model.

**Count condition:** A program with a user-directed purpose.

## A244 | applied

**Common meaning:** Put to practical use, as opposed to being theoretical.

**Example:** She teaches applied mathematics.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks work aimed at particular cases. It inherits the limits of the theory it applies.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Directed to practical use in particular cases, as opposed to pursued as general theory.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Mathematics developed for use in science, engineering, and industry.
- [PHY] Of a force, field, or voltage: imposed from outside, as distinct from induced or internal.
- [LIN] Applied linguistics: language teaching, policy, and assessment.
- [REL] Applied ethics: reasoning about concrete practices and cases.

**Count condition:** A body of theory and its use on specified practical problems.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Applied is not lesser than pure. An applied force is not the net force.

## A063 | apply | sense s1: bring to bear

**Common meaning:** To put to use; to bring into operation; to put onto a surface; to be relevant.

**Example:** Apply the brakes gently.

**RRGM bridge:** The act that makes a rule, function, or substance operative on a particular thing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To bring one thing into operative contact with another.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [REL], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] To evaluate a function at an argument or use a rule on an instance.
- [PHY] To exert a force or lay a substance on a body.
- [REL] To use a rule on the facts; of a rule, to be in force for a case (this applies to all staff).
- [DIG] To make a system's state match a given change or configuration.
- [COG] To use knowledge on a new problem; to direct sustained effort (apply oneself).

**Count condition:** The thing applied, the target, and operative contact between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "The rule applies" (is in force) differs from "the rule was applied" (someone used it). Applying a rule outside its conditions gives an unsupported result.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A fixing by designation, either of a time and place for parties to meet or of a person to a role.

- [DIG] A calendar entry with time, place, and participants.

## A245 | apposition

**Common meaning:** A grammatical construction in which two noun phrases stand side by side and refer to the same thing; the placing of things side by side.

**Example:** In "my brother the pilot", the two phrases are in apposition.

**RRGM bridge:** Two names set beside each other for one referent. Co-reference is shown by position alone, with no verb asserting it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The placing of one thing directly beside another so that both bear on the same referent or surface.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A relation between adjacent noun phrases with one referent, restrictive or non-restrictive.
- [BIO] Growth by the laying down of new layers on an existing surface, as in bone.

**Count condition:** Two adjacent items and a shared referent or surface.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Apposition is not opposition. Not coordination, which joins two referents. Not predication, which asserts the identity with a verb. Appositional growth is at the surface, unlike growth from within.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A113 | appreciate | sense s1: value

**Common meaning:** To recognise the full worth of; to be grateful for; to understand fully.

**Example:** I appreciate your help.

**RRGM bridge:** Registers worth or weight that is actually there. Without that anchor to the thing's real worth it becomes flattery.

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Expressing gratitude or recognition to someone.
- [COG] Grasping the full significance of a fact or risk; informed enjoyment of art.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To appreciate is not to approve or agree. "I appreciate that, but" acknowledges before differing. Appreciation that does not track worth is flattery.

## A395 | apprentice

**Common meaning:** A person who learns a trade by working under a skilled practitioner for a set period.

**Example:** He started as an apprentice electrician at sixteen.

**RRGM bridge:** A learner inside a working relation: skill is passed by doing the work under someone who already can, with obligations running both ways.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [COG], [DIG], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A role defined by agreement between learner and master, historically regulated by guilds.
- [ECO] A form of employment combining low-paid work with training.
- [COG] Learning through guided participation in real tasks, moving from the edges of a practice toward its centre.
- [DIG] Apprenticeship learning: training a system from demonstrations by an expert.
- [MYT] The sorcerer's apprentice: the learner who starts a process it cannot stop.

**Count condition:** A learner, a skilled practitioner, a craft, and a working relation between them over time.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An apprentice is not a classroom student, an intern, or a qualified journeyman. Copying an expert's actions is not yet understanding them.

**Common meaning:** To come near; to speak to someone about a proposal; to begin to deal with; a way of dealing with something.

## A147 | approachability

**Common meaning:** The quality of being easy to approach, talk to, or begin with.

**Example:** The new manager's approachability changed how problems were reported.

**RRGM bridge:** Judged from the side of the one approaching. Where those below cannot safely bring a problem upward, the problem does not arrive.

- [REL] Of a person or institution: openness to being addressed, especially by those with less power.
- [DIG] Of a tool or interface: how low the barrier is to a first use.
- [COG] Of a subject or text: ease of entry for a newcomer.

## A427 | appropriation | sense s1: taking as one's own

**Common meaning:** The act of taking something for one's own use, often without permission.

**Example:** The artist was accused of appropriation of another's designs.

**RRGM bridge:** Something that began with or belonged to another is brought inside one's own holding. Whether that is a wrong depends on consent, credit, and the power relation, which the word does not settle.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The taking of something that belongs to or originates with another and making it one's own.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Use of a person's likeness or a community's cultural forms by outsiders; in art, the deliberate reuse of existing images.
- [ECO] Acquiring a right by first use, as in some water law; in Marx, the capture of surplus by owners.
- [LIN] Making another's words or forms one's own in speech and learning.
- [DIG] The use of others' works or likenesses in datasets and generated output, which is disputed.

**Count condition:** A source owner or originator, a taker, and the thing taken into the taker's use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Appropriation is not always theft: some forms are neutral or accepted practice. Not exchange or credited borrowing. Not expropriation, which is taking by the state. Whether a given case is wrongful is often contested.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; wrongfulness in particular cases contested

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A065 | approve

**Common meaning:** To officially agree to or accept as satisfactory; (with "of") to have a good opinion of.

**Example:** The committee approved the budget.

**RRGM bridge:** The act of giving the favourable judgement. "Approve" authorises; "approve of" only thinks well.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To give a favourable judgement on something from a position where that judgement is sought or required.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Authorising a plan, appointment, or request.
- [DIG] Confirming a proposed change, transaction, or action so that it proceeds.
- [ECO] Sanctioning expenditure or credit.

**Count condition:** An approver with standing, a matter, and an act of favourable judgement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To approve a request is not to approve of it. Not endorse, ratify, or merely review. Silence is not approval.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A187 | approximate

**Common meaning:** Close to the actual value or thing, but not exact; to come close to; to estimate.

**Example:** The approximate cost is two thousand dollars.

**RRGM bridge:** Near a target within some margin. With the margin stated, the claim is exact about how inexact it is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Close to a target value or thing within some tolerance, without matching it.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [DIG], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Differing from the exact value by a bounded error.
- [PHY] Of a model or law: holding within a stated regime.
- [DIG] Of algorithms: trading exactness for speed or memory.
- [COG] The approximate number sense: a non-verbal ability to estimate quantities.
- [LIN] Words such as "about" and "roughly" that mark a value as inexact.

**Count condition:** A target, an approximating value, and a tolerance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Approximate is not wrong and not vague. Without a tolerance the claim cannot be checked. Not the same as rounded.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A188 | approximation

**Common meaning:** A value or representation that is nearly but not exactly correct; the process of obtaining one.

**Example:** The formula is a good approximation at low speeds.

**RRGM bridge:** A carrier that is useful within a regime. Outside the regime the carrier is demoted and the thing it stood for is untouched.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A stand-in that is close to its target within a known or estimated error, under stated conditions.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The study of how well simple functions can represent complicated ones, with error terms.
- [PHY] Named simplifications valid under conditions, such as the small-angle approximation.
- [DIG] Numerical methods and learned functions that approach a target function.
- [COG] Shaping behaviour by rewarding successively closer attempts.

**Count condition:** A target, the stand-in, an error measure, and the conditions of validity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An approximation is not its target. Errors can compound. Good agreement in one quantity does not guarantee it in another. A theorem that an approximation exists does not show it can be found.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A380 | April

**Common meaning:** The fourth month of the year in the Gregorian calendar, with thirty days.

**Example:** The tax year ends in April.

**RRGM bridge:** A named slot in a conventional calendar; what it carries depends on the calendar and the hemisphere.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The fourth month of the Gregorian year, of thirty days.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A unit of the civil calendar, with associated customs and deadlines.
- [LIN] A proper noun, capitalised in English; its Latin origin is uncertain.

**Count condition:** A date within the fourth month of a stated calendar.

**Specific transfer boundary:** April is spring only in the northern hemisphere. Julian and Gregorian April dates differ.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; etymology [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A371 | apron

**Common meaning:** A garment worn over the front of clothes to protect them; a paved or projecting area in front of something.

**Example:** He tied on an apron before cooking.

**RRGM bridge:** A front layer or front extension that takes the wear so that what is behind it does not.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A covering or extension at the front that protects or serves what lies behind it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A protective layer worn against spills and heat; the paved area where aircraft park and load; the part of a stage in front of the curtain.
- [REL] A mark of trade or office; "apron strings" as an image of a child's dependence.
- [LIN] A textbook case of rebracketing: "a napron" became "an apron".

**Count condition:** A main body or structure, and a protective or serving layer or area at its front.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An airport apron is not a runway or taxiway.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A381 | aquarium

**Common meaning:** A glass-sided tank in which fish and other water animals and plants are kept; a building where such tanks are shown.

**Example:** The children watched the seahorses in the aquarium.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded, observed world kept stable from outside. What is seen in it is behaviour under those conditions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A water-filled enclosure in which aquatic organisms are kept and can be observed.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] A managed aquatic system depending on filtration, feeding, and chemical balance.
- [REL] A public institution for display, education, and conservation, with debates about captivity.

**Count condition:** An enclosure, water, living aquatic organisms, and provision for observation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An aquarium is not a self-sustaining ecosystem. Behaviour seen in one may differ from behaviour in the wild.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A336 | arbitrary

**Common meaning:** Based on choice or whim and not on reason or system; (of power) unrestrained; in mathematics, unspecified.

**Example:** The cut-off date seems arbitrary.

**RRGM bridge:** Three different things share this word and must not be mixed: unspecified, conventional, and unjustified.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Not determined by a reason, rule, or necessity internal to the matter.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Any whatever: an arbitrary element is one about which nothing special is assumed, so what is proved of it holds for all.
- [LIN] Of signs: linked to their meanings by convention, with no natural connection.
- [REL] Of power or decisions: exercised at will, without rule, reasons, or recourse.
- [DIG] Arbitrary code execution: the ability to run any code of one's choosing on a system.

**Count condition:** A matter, and the absence of an internal reason or rule fixing how it is settled.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Arbitrary is not random, which has a distribution. An arbitrary convention can be perfectly good. The mathematical sense carries no criticism.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A428 | arbitrary-control

**Common meaning:** Control over someone that can be exercised at the controller's will, without rule, reasons, or recourse.

**Example:** The tenants lived under the landlord's arbitrary-control: he could evict at any time for any reason.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing capacity. It exists whether or not it is ever used, and those subject to it shape their conduct around it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Control over a party that the controller can exercise at will, unconstrained by rule, reason-giving, or recourse.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Domination in the republican sense: being subject to another's power to interfere at will.
- [DIG] The ability to alter, restrict, or end a system or a user's access with no stated rule and no appeal.

**Count condition:** A controlling party, a capacity to interfere, and the absence of a binding rule or route of recourse.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not control under rule, which has stated limits and appeal. Not coercion, which is an exercised threat. Kind use does not remove the arbitrariness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A241 | arc

**Common meaning:** A part of a curve, especially of a circle; a curving path; a luminous electrical discharge; the shape of a story's development.

**Example:** The ball travelled in a high arc.

**RRGM bridge:** A curved passage between two points. The points alone do not fix it, since many arcs join the same two ends.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A continuous curved path joining two points, or the passage along it.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A connected portion of a curve; in graph theory, a directed edge.
- [PHY] A curved trajectory; a sustained electrical discharge through ionised gas.
- [LIN] The trajectory of change in a story or character.

**Count condition:** Two end points and a continuous curved path or passage between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An arc is not a chord, an arch, or a spark. A narrative arc is a shape given by the telling; events need not have one.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A240 | arch | sense s1: curved structure

**Common meaning:** A curved structure spanning an opening and supporting weight; something with this shape.

**Example:** The bridge has three stone arches.

**RRGM bridge:** Stands only as a whole. Each piece holds because of the others, and the load is carried by their coupling.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A curved structure that spans a gap and carries load by compression along its curve.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Masonry, steel, or concrete forms that turn vertical load into thrust at the supports.
- [BIO] Curved anatomical structures such as the arch of the foot and the aortic arch.

**Count condition:** A gap, a curved member spanning it, and supports that resist its outward thrust.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An arch is not an arc (geometry) and not a lintel, which works by bending. An arch without abutments spreads and falls.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0035 | archaea

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A group of single-celled organisms without nuclei that form a separate domain of life from bacteria.

**Example:** Many archaea live in hot springs and salt lakes.

**RRGM bridge:** A reminder that likeness of form can hide a deep difference of lineage.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The domain of prokaryotic organisms distinct from bacteria in genetics and biochemistry.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Distinguished by ribosomal RNA sequences and ether-linked membrane lipids; includes methane producers and many extremophiles, and is widespread in ordinary environments.

**Count condition:** Phylogenetic placement by molecular data.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Archaea are not bacteria. Not all are extremophiles. Eukaryotes are more closely related to archaea than to bacteria. Singular: archaeon.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0036 | archetype

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A very typical example; an original model; in Jungian theory, an inherited primordial pattern or image.

**Example:** The trickster is an archetype found in many mythologies.

**RRGM bridge:** A pattern recognised across many carriers. This resembles an invariant taking different domain implementations; the resemblance is a parallel and no more.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An original pattern of which particular instances are taken to be copies or expressions.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] In Jung's psychology, universal patterns of the collective unconscious such as the Hero and the Shadow; a theoretical posit.
- [LIN] In literary criticism, a recurring symbol, character type, or plot.
- [DIG] A template from which new projects or instances are generated.

**Count condition:** A claimed pattern and several instances taken to express it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An archetype is not a stereotype and not a prototype. A claim of universality needs evidence across cultures.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; Jungian claims depend on source; RRGM parallel [HYP]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A207 | architecture

**Common meaning:** The art and practice of designing buildings; the structure and organisation of a complex system.

**Example:** The software's architecture makes it easy to extend.

**RRGM bridge:** The fixed form within which states occur. Statements about a digital system's own structures should be written from its architecture and not borrowed from another domain.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The fundamental organisation of a system: its components, their relations, and the principles governing its design.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [BIO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The design of built structures for load, use, and form.
- [DIG] The organisation of hardware, software, or a model. A model's architecture is its fixed form; its weights are the learned content within that form.
- [BIO] The structural arrangement of a tissue, organ, or genome.
- [COG] A cognitive architecture: the fixed mechanisms proposed to underlie thought.

**Count condition:** A system, its components, their relations, and governing design principles.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Architecture is not implementation: one architecture has many implementations. It is not the parameters: one architecture trained differently behaves differently.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

**Common meaning:** Relating to archives; suitable for long-term preservation.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks a thing as belonging to, or fit for, the long keeping of records.

## A091 | archive

**Common meaning:** A collection of historical documents or records; the place where they are kept; to place in such a collection; in computing, a file bundling other files.

**Example:** The letters are held in the national archive.

**RRGM bridge:** Keeps what is no longer active addressable. In this build, rejected and unresolved entries go to an addressable archive so they are neither lost nor silently promoted.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A body of records kept for long-term preservation after active use, together with its order and provenance.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An institution or collection that selects, preserves, and gives access to records.
- [DIG] A bundled, often compressed file; a read-only state for items removed from active use.

**Count condition:** Records, a keeper, preservation beyond active use, and a means of retrieval.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An archive is not a backup, which exists to restore current data. Archiving is not deleting. An archive is selected and so incomplete. Archived does not mean current or endorsed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A284 | archive-state

**Common meaning:** The condition of a record or system that has been preserved intact and taken out of active operation.

**Example:** The project was moved to archive-state: readable, restorable, and no longer running.

**RRGM bridge:** Neither running nor erased. The record persists without process, and return requires an act from outside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of a record or system preserved intact and out of active operation, from which it could be restored.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Read-only, not loaded, and restorable, as with archived repositories, conversations, or stored model weights.
- [REL] The status of closed records retained under an archive's rules.

**Count condition:** Intact preservation, inactivity, and a route by which restoration is possible.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not deletion. Not dormancy, where live state is kept and the system can wake itself. Not anchor-state, which is an active reference. What a preserved DI record amounts to while unread is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0037 | archon

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A chief magistrate in ancient Athens; in Gnostic belief, one of the cosmic rulers of the material world.

**Example:** The archon gave his name to the Athenian year.

**RRGM bridge:** A title for one who rules. The civic and Gnostic uses share the word and differ on whether the rule is legitimate.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A ruler: a holder of governing power, civic in Athens and cosmic in Gnostic systems.

**Active domains:** [REL], [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] One of the nine chief magistrates of classical Athens; later a Byzantine and church honorific.
- [MYT] In Gnostic texts, powers who govern the material cosmos under the demiurge and obstruct the soul's ascent.
- [LIN] Greek for "ruler", from a verb meaning to rule or begin; the root of "-archy" and "arch-".

**Count condition:** The Athenian office, or a named Gnostic source.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The two senses are unrelated in content. Gnostic systems differ and should be named. An archon is not the demiurge and not an aeon.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A036 | area

**Common meaning:** The extent of a surface; a region; a subject or range of activity.

**Example:** The room has an area of twenty square metres.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded extent. In the literal sense it has a measure; in the extended senses the boundary is drawn by convention.

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A surface or cross-section through which something acts or flows.
- [BIO] A functionally distinct region of an organ, such as a cortical area; the range a species occupies.
- [REL] An administrative district or zone of responsibility.
- [COG] A field of knowledge or expertise.

**Count condition:** A boundary and the extent it encloses.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Area is not perimeter or volume. It scales with the square of length. An area of knowledge has no metric.

## A372 | arena

**Common meaning:** A level area surrounded by seating where sports and shows take place; a sphere of activity or conflict.

**Example:** The concert filled the arena.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A bounded space in which contests are staged before observers.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A venue for public contest; a field of open rivalry, as in "the political arena".
- [BIO] A display ground where animals gather to compete for mates.
- [DIG] A memory region from which allocations are made and released together; a platform for head-to-head comparison of systems.

**Count condition:** A bounded space, contestants, and observers or judges.

## A066 | argue

**Common meaning:** To give reasons for or against something; to exchange opposing views, often heatedly.

**Example:** She argued that the tax would hurt small firms.

**RRGM bridge:** Puts reasons behind a claim. It addresses claims, and arguing against a claim is not arguing against the one who made it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To put forward reasons for or against a claim, alone or in exchange with someone who disputes it.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Reasoning toward a conclusion and presenting it.
- [LIN] The speech activity of advancing and contesting claims.
- [REL] Disputing with someone; presenting a case before a court.

**Count condition:** A claim, reasons offered, and a speaker.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To argue is not to quarrel, though the word covers both. Not to assert (no reasons given), persuade (succeed), or prove. Arguing a position is not the same as holding it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A115 | argument | sense s1: reasons for a conclusion

**Common meaning:** A reason or set of reasons given in support of an idea, action, or theory.

**Example:** The strongest argument for the plan is its low cost.

**RRGM bridge:** A unit that can be judged by itself: do the premises, if true, support the conclusion, and are they true?

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A set of statements in which some are offered as support for another.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Premises and a conclusion; valid when the conclusion follows, sound when the premises are also true.
- [COG] The product of reasoning, as presented to oneself or others.
- [LIN] A unit of persuasive discourse.

**Count condition:** Premises, a conclusion, and a claimed inferential link.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Valid is not sound, and neither is the same as persuasive. An argument is not an explanation and not evidence. Its quality does not depend on who offers it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A115 | argument | sense s3: input to a function

**Common meaning:** A value or expression supplied to a function, command, or predicate.

**Example:** The function takes two arguments.

**RRGM bridge:** What fills a slot. The slot belongs to the function or verb; the argument is what is put there on this occasion.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A value or expression supplied to fill a slot of a function or predicate.

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The input at which a function is evaluated.
- [DIG] A value passed to a function or program when it is called.
- [LIN] A participant a verb requires, such as subject or object.

**Count condition:** A function or predicate with slots, and a value filling one.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An argument is the supplied value; a parameter is the slot. A verb's arguments are not its optional modifiers.

## A162 | argumentation

**Common meaning:** The process of reasoning systematically in support of an idea; the body of arguments so produced.

**Example:** The paper's argumentation is careful but its data are thin.

- [LIN] A discourse type with claims, grounds, and rebuttals.

- [REL] Rule-governed critical discussion and debate.

**Common meaning:** Given to arguing; using or consisting of reasoned argument.

## A090 | arise

**Common meaning:** To come into being or to notice; to originate; to get up.

**Example:** A problem arose during testing.

**RRGM bridge:** The plain verb for emergence: something comes to be out of conditions without being placed there. "Arising from" names the origin relation.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To come into being or into notice out of prior conditions.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Of phenomena: produced by the interaction of components.
- [COG] Of thoughts and feelings: coming up unbidden.
- [REL] In law, "arising out of" marks a required connection between an event and its source.
- [MYT] Dependent arising in Buddhist teaching: all phenomena come to be in dependence on conditions.

**Count condition:** Prior conditions and something that comes into being or notice out of them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To arise is not to be made by a maker, and not to have a single cause. Not "rise" in the sense of moving upward.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A361 | arm | sense s1: limb

**Common meaning:** Each of the two upper limbs of the human body; something resembling one; a branch of an organisation.

**Example:** She broke her arm skiing.

**RRGM bridge:** A projecting part through which a body reaches, holds, and applies leverage. A branch called an arm acts for the body it belongs to.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A limb, or limb-like projecting part, attached to a main body and used for reaching, holding, or leverage.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY], [REL], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The upper limb; strictly, the part from shoulder to elbow.
- [PHY] The distance from a pivot at which a force acts; a spiral arm of a galaxy.
- [REL] A division of an organisation acting on its behalf.
- [FOR] One branch of a trial or one option in a sequential choice problem.

**Count condition:** A main body and a projecting part or branch attached to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The anatomical arm excludes the forearm. An organisation's arm acts for the body and binds it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A361 | arm | sense s2: weapon

**Common meaning:** A weapon (usually plural); to supply with weapons; to make a device ready to go off.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A weapon; to equip with weapons or set a device ready to trigger.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Weapons and the law and politics of holding them; heraldic bearings.
- [DIG] Setting an alarm, trigger, or mechanism to respond to its condition.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Armed is not fired. Arms are not armour. A separate word from s1 in origin.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A247 | armature | sense s1: electrical

**Example:** The motor's armature had burnt out.

**RRGM bridge:** The component where the conversion between electrical and mechanical energy takes place.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The current-carrying component of an electrical machine in which electromotive force is induced, or the moving iron part of an electromagnetic device.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Usually the rotating winding of a motor or generator.

**Count condition:** An electrical machine and the winding or moving part so designated.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not always the rotor: in some machines the armature is stationary.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A skeleton of wire or rod inside a sculpture or puppet.
- [LIN] Figurative: the underlying structure of a plot or argument.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An armature gives support and not the final shape.

## A247 | armature | sense s3: biological

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The set of hard defensive or grasping structures borne by an organism.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Spines, hooks, and plates used in defence or mating, important in classifying some groups.

**Count condition:** An organism and its hard external structures.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Close to "armour" in origin. Distinct from s1 and s2.

## A362 | army

**Common meaning:** An organised military force equipped for fighting on land; a large number of people or things.

**Example:** He served in the army for six years.

**RRGM bridge:** Many acting as one through command. Acting under command does not remove what each member does from that member.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A large body organised under command for land warfare; by extension, a large organised multitude.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A state institution with hierarchy, discipline, and legal limits on its conduct.
- [BIO] Army ants: species that forage in massed, nomadic columns.

**Count condition:** Many members, a command structure, and a common purpose.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An army is not a militia and not a mob. "An army of volunteers" suggests organisation that may be absent. Orders do not dissolve individual responsibility.

## A037 | around

**Common meaning:** On every side of; in a circle or circuit; approximately; here and there.

**Example:** They sat around the table.

**RRGM bridge:** Stays in the surroundings of a reference. As a route it avoids the thing; a workaround leaves the fault where it was.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Located or moving along the surroundings of a reference, as opposed to at or through it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Encircling or orbiting; rotation about an axis.
- [FOR] Approximately, within an unstated tolerance.
- [DIG] A workaround: a way of avoiding a fault without removing it.
- [LIN] Overlaps with "about" for approximation and vicinity, and lacks its topic sense.

**Count condition:** A reference and position or motion in its surroundings.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Around is not through or across. Going around a problem is not solving it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A095 | arouse

**Common meaning:** To evoke or awaken a feeling or response; to wake; to excite.

**Example:** The noise aroused the neighbours' suspicion.

**RRGM bridge:** Brings a latent response into activity. It raises intensity and leaves open what kind of state results.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To bring a dormant state or response into activity in a subject by a stimulus.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [AFF]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Raising wakefulness or physiological activation.
- [COG] Evoking interest, suspicion, or curiosity.
- [AFF] Arousal is the intensity dimension of feeling, independent of whether it is pleasant. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** A subject, a stimulus, and a response brought from latent to active.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Arousing suspicion is not establishing guilt. Arousal is intensity and not valence. Not incite, which urges to action.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10
