## A067 | arrange

**Common meaning:** To put in a particular order; to make plans for; to adapt a piece of music.

**Example:** She arranged the books by subject.

**RRGM bridge:** Sets elements into a chosen configuration for a purpose. In music, the voicing and forces change while the piece remains that piece.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To put elements into a chosen order or configuration for a purpose.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] To place items in an ordered selection.
- [REL] To plan or settle something with others.
- [LIN] To adapt a musical work for different voices or instruments.

**Count condition:** Elements, a chosen order or configuration, and a purpose.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To arrange is not to compose, and not to sort by a key. An arranged marriage rests on the couple's consent and is distinct from a forced one.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A218 | arrangement

**Common meaning:** The way things are arranged; a plan or preparation; an informal agreement; a musical adaptation.

**Example:** We have an arrangement to share the driving.

**RRGM bridge:** The same parts in a different arrangement can be a different thing. Here structure carries the difference.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A configuration produced by arranging, or an informal settlement of how things will be done.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] An ordering of a set's elements.
- [PHY] The spatial configuration of atoms or parts; molecules with the same atoms in different arrangements have different properties.
- [REL] An understanding between parties, or preparations made.
- [LIN] A version of a musical work for particular forces.

**Count condition:** Elements and their configuration, or parties and their settled understanding.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An arrangement between people is not necessarily an enforceable contract. The arrangement is not the parts.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A208 | array

**Common meaning:** An ordered arrangement; an impressive display or range; in computing, an indexed collection of elements.

**Example:** The data are stored in a two-dimensional array.

**RRGM bridge:** Like items laid out so that position identifies each one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An ordered arrangement of like items laid out by position.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [FOR], [PHY], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A collection whose elements are reached by index.
- [FOR] A rectangular arrangement in rows and columns.
- [PHY] Multiple antennas, telescopes, or sensors combined to act as one.
- [BIO] A grid of probes for testing many sequences or samples at once.
- [REL] Troops drawn up in order; the panel from which a jury is chosen.

**Count condition:** Like elements and a positional layout that indexes them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An array is not a set (unordered) and not a matrix (which adds algebra). Indexing may start at zero or one. "An array of options" claims breadth only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A246 | arrayed

**Common meaning:** Arranged in order or on display; drawn up; dressed.

**Example:** The evidence arrayed against him was strong.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Set out in order for display or action.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [PHY], [LIN]

- [REL] Of forces or arguments: lined up in position, often in opposition.
- [BIO] Of a screen or library: each sample kept in its own position, as opposed to pooled.
- [PHY] Of optical or sensing elements: laid out as an array.
- [LIN] Literary: finely dressed.

**Count condition:** Items and an ordered layout they have been placed in.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Arrayed against is positioned and not yet acting. Arrayed is the state; array is the structure.

## A068 | arrive

**Common meaning:** To reach a place at the end of a journey; to come; to reach a conclusion or decision.

**Example:** The train arrived on time.

**RRGM bridge:** The completion that approach lacks. Arrival is a point, and it is what gets recorded.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To reach a destination, so that the journey toward it is complete.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A body, wave, or signal reaching a location at a time.
- [DIG] A message, packet, or event reaching its receiver; arrivals need not keep the order of sending.
- [COG] Reaching a conclusion by steps of reasoning.

**Count condition:** A destination and a traveller or signal that has reached it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To arrive is not to approach. Arriving at a conclusion does not make it true. The verb needs a destination.

## A388 | arrow

**Common meaning:** A pointed shaft shot from a bow; a mark shaped like one, used to show direction.

**Example:** Follow the arrows to the exit.

**RRGM bridge:** In RRLM an arrow marks a declared dependency, passage, meeting, or route. It does not mean clock sequence unless a domain binds it to that.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A shaft that points and travels one way; as a sign, a mark of direction from one thing to another.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [DIG], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A projectile stabilised in flight; the "arrow of time", the direction in which entropy increases.
- [FOR] A morphism between objects; the sign for implication or mapping.
- [DIG] The arrow notation for anonymous functions and for pointer access in some languages.
- [MYT] The arrows of Eros and Apollo, which carry love or plague to those they strike.

**Count condition:** A source, a target, and a single direction from one to the other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A drawn arrow means nothing until its relation is declared: mapping, implication, cause, and sequence are different relations. The arrow of time is a direction and is not time.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A363 | art

**Common meaning:** The expression of creative skill and imagination in works valued for their beauty, meaning, or emotional power; a practised skill.

**Example:** The gallery shows contemporary art.

**RRGM bridge:** Making in which the form is the point. Whether a given work counts is settled partly by the work and partly by a community's recognition.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The making of works by skill and imagination whose form is meant to be experienced and valued for itself; or a practised skill.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Creative production and aesthetic experience.
- [REL] A social practice with institutions that exhibit, judge, and confer standing.
- [ECO] A market in unique and reproducible works.
- [LIN] "Term of art": a word with a technical meaning in a field. "State of the art": the current best practice.

**Count condition:** A maker, a work, and form offered for experience; or a skill exercised with mastery.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Art is not craft, though the border is disputed. Not artifice in the sense of trickery. A term of art has nothing to do with artworks.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-A-0038 | arteriole

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A small blood vessel that carries blood from an artery to the capillaries.

**Example:** The arterioles widened as the muscle warmed up.

**RRGM bridge:** A controllable narrowing: by changing its width it sets how much flow reaches what lies beyond.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A small muscular vessel between an artery and a capillary bed.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The main site of resistance in the circulation; its smooth muscle regulates local flow and overall pressure.

**Count condition:** A vessel of this calibre and wall structure, lying between artery and capillaries.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An arteriole is not an artery, a capillary (where exchange occurs), or a venule (after the capillaries).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A337 | article

**RRGM bridge:** A distinct, separately identifiable unit within a larger whole.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A distinct, separately identifiable item within a larger whole.

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A self-contained piece of non-fiction prose; the grammatical class that marks definiteness.
- [REL] A numbered provision of a constitution, treaty, or contract.
- [ECO] An individual item of goods.

## A163 | articulate

**Common meaning:** Able to speak fluently and clearly; to express clearly; to pronounce distinctly; to connect by joints.

**Example:** She articulated the team's concerns to the board.

**RRGM bridge:** Makes distinct units out of something undivided and connects them. Fluent articulation is a property of expression and does not show that what is expressed is correct.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [BIO], [COG], [PEJ]

- [BIO] Of bones: to meet at a joint.
- [COG] To make a half-formed thought explicit enough to be examined.
- [PEJ] "So articulate", said with surprise to a member of a minority group, implies that clear speech was not expected of them. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** Something undivided or unexpressed, and its rendering into distinct connected units.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Articulate is not accurate and not intelligent. Being inarticulate about something is not being wrong about it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [PEJ] PROVISIONAL

## A248 | articulated

**Common meaning:** Having sections connected by joints; clearly expressed.

**Example:** The city uses articulated buses on the main routes.

**RRGM bridge:** Joined and still distinct. In remains and fossils, the kept arrangement is itself evidence.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Composed of segments connected by joints, or set out in distinct expressed parts.

- [PHY] Vehicles, arms, and mechanisms built of linked segments that move relative to each other.
- [BIO] A skeleton or fossil with its bones in their living positions.
- [LIN] Of a position or plan: stated clearly and in its parts.

**Count condition:** Segments, joints or connections between them, and preserved relative arrangement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Articulated is not rigid and not separate. A disarticulated skeleton has the same bones and less information.

## A164 | articulation

**Common meaning:** The act of expressing something in words; the clarity of speech; a joint; in music, the way notes are attacked and connected.

**Example:** The singer's articulation was crisp.

**RRGM bridge:** Covers both the separation of units and the manner of their connection.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The forming of distinct units and the manner of their connection.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [BIO], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The production of speech sounds, classified by place and manner; the two-level structure of language into meaningful units and meaningless sounds; in music, staccato, legato, and related markings.
- [BIO] A joint between bones or segments.
- [COG] The putting of a concept into explicit form.
- [REL] Arrangements linking separate institutions or programmes, such as credit transfer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Articulation is not enunciation only. It concerns the connections as much as the divisions.

## A364 | artist

**Common meaning:** A person who creates art; a skilled performer or practitioner.

**Example:** The artist signed each print.

**RRGM bridge:** A maker credited with a work. The credit carries rights of attribution and integrity in many legal systems.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A person who makes works of art, or who practises a skill with art.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A recognised occupation and role, with moral rights over the work in many jurisdictions.
- [COG] One engaged in creative practice.
- [ECO] A rights-holder who earns from sales, licences, and royalties.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An artist is not an artisan, though the line is disputed. Sales and training do not confer or remove the status. Whether a non-human maker can be an artist depends on the count condition used and is contested.

## A038 | as

**Common meaning:** Used in comparisons; in the role of; while; because; in the way that.

**Example:** She works as a nurse.

**RRGM bridge:** "X as Y" marks the capacity in which something is done. It names a role, and one party can act in several.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Sets one thing in a relation of equivalence, role, or simultaneity with another.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A conjunction, preposition, and adverb marking comparison (as tall as), role (as chair), time, cause, and manner.
- [DIG] A keyword for aliasing a name or treating a value as another type.

**Count condition:** Two terms and the relation (degree, role, time, cause, or manner) that links them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "As a friend" (in that role) differs from "like a friend" (similar to one). Causal and temporal "as" are easily confused. A type cast with "as" asserts a type and does not change the value.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-A-0039 | ascension myth

## A145 | ashamed

**Common meaning:** Feeling shame or embarrassment about something one has done or is connected with; reluctant through fear of shame.

**Example:** He was ashamed of how he had spoken to her.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The subject presently feels shame about a specific thing connected to themselves.

**Active domains:** [AFF], [REL], [COG]

- [AFF] The felt state of shame. Provisional domain.
- [REL] A response to exposure, real or imagined, before the judgement of others.
- [COG] A negative evaluation directed at the self as a whole.

## A039 | ask

**Common meaning:** To put a question to someone; to request; to invite; to set as a price.

**Example:** She asked the way to the station.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To address another with a question or request, leaving the response to them.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The speech acts of questioning and requesting.
- [REL] Seeking permission or help in a way that recognises the other's standing to refuse.
- [ECO] The ask: the lowest price a seller will accept.
- [DIG] To submit a query to a system; a system's request to a user for input before it proceeds.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To ask is not to demand or order. A question can carry an accusation in its presupposition. Asked is not answered.

## A182 | asker

**Common meaning:** The person or party who asks.

**Example:** The asker wanted a yes or no.

**RRGM bridge:** A role within an exchange, paired with the answerer and passing back and forth.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The party in an exchange who puts the question or request.

- [LIN] The questioning role in dialogue.
- [DIG] The user or process that originates a query.
- [REL] One who petitions or requests.

**Count condition:** An exchange and the party who puts the question or request.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An asker need not be ignorant: teachers and examiners ask what they know. The role is relative to the exchange.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A338 | aspect

**Common meaning:** A particular part or feature of something; the way something looks; the direction something faces; in grammar, how a verb presents an action in time.

**Example:** Cost is only one aspect of the problem.

**RRGM bridge:** A face of one thing. It cannot be detached and counted separately from the thing whose face it is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One of the ways a thing presents or can be regarded, without being a separable part.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [PHY], [COG], [DIG], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The grammatical category that presents an event as complete or as ongoing, distinct from tense.
- [PHY] The compass direction a slope or building faces; the ratio of width to height.
- [COG] Seeing an ambiguous figure now one way and now another.
- [DIG] A module that gathers a concern cutting across many parts of a program.
- [MYT] One of the forms in which a deity is known; in astrology, an angular relation between planets.

**Count condition:** One thing and a way of regarding or presenting it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An aspect is not a part. Grammatical aspect is not tense. An aspect belongs to the object; a perspective belongs to the viewer.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A079 | assemble

**Common meaning:** To gather together in one place; to fit together the parts of something.

**Example:** The crowd assembled in the square.

**RRGM bridge:** Brings separate parts or persons into one working whole, where fit and order matter.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To bring separate parts or persons together into one working whole.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To gather for a common purpose.
- [PHY] To fit components into a machine or structure; self-assembly occurs when parts organise without outside direction.
- [DIG] To translate symbolic instructions into machine code; to build a product from components.
- [BIO] To reconstruct a genome from sequenced fragments; to form a complex from subunits.

**Count condition:** Separate parts or persons and a resulting whole in which they fit.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To assemble is not merely to aggregate. An assembled genome is a reconstruction and may contain errors. Assembling is not making the parts.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A219 | assembly

**Common meaning:** A group of people gathered for a purpose; a legislative body; the fitting together of parts; a unit of fitted parts.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A whole made by bringing parts or persons together, or the act of doing so.

- [PHY] A unit of components fitted together.
- [ECO] The assembly line: production divided into sequential stations.
- [DIG] A low-level language that maps closely to machine instructions.
- [BIO] A reconstructed genome; the process by which species come to make up a community.

**Count condition:** Parts or persons, a bringing-together, and the resulting whole.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An assembly is not a crowd, which has no order. Assembly language is not machine code.

## A181 | assent

**Common meaning:** The expression of approval or agreement; official sanction; to express agreement.

**Example:** The bill received royal assent.

**RRGM bridge:** A "yes" given to what another has put. In formal settings it is weaker than consent.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An expressed "yes" to a proposal put by another.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Official sanction; in research ethics, the affirmative agreement of someone who cannot give legal consent, such as a child.
- [COG] The mental act of accepting a proposition as true.
- [LIN] A response token of agreement.

**Count condition:** A proposal, a party to whom it is put, and an expressed affirmative.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Assent is not consent, which requires capacity, information, and freedom. Not acquiescence, which is merely not objecting. Silence is not assent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A297 | assent-signal

**Common meaning:** An observable sign taken to convey assent, such as a spoken "yes", a nod, a signature, or a click.

**Example:** A pre-ticked box is a poor assent-signal.

**RRGM bridge:** The token and the assent are two things. The token can be produced by habit, default, or pressure with no assent behind it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An observable token taken to convey assent.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Nods, signatures, shows of hands.
- [DIG] A click, checkbox, or confirmation token; an agreeing phrase in a system's output.
- [LIN] Brief responses such as "mm-hm", which often signal only that the speaker is listening.

**Count condition:** A token, the proposal it answers, and a convention linking the token to assent.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A signal is not the assent. A listening noise is not agreement. The absence of a signal is not refusal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## A166 | assert

**Common meaning:** To state something confidently as true; to make others recognise a right or authority; in computing, to check that a condition holds.

**Example:** He asserted that the figures were wrong.

**RRGM bridge:** Puts content forward on the speaker's own commitment. In code, an assertion is a count condition checked at run time, and a failed one halts.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To put something forward as so, on one's own commitment.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An assertive speech act, committing the speaker to the truth of what is said.
- [REL] To claim and act on a right, authority, or boundary so that it is recognised.
- [DIG] To state a condition that must hold, stopping execution if it does not; to drive a signal to its active state.

**Count condition:** A speaker or program, a content, and commitment to its holding.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To assert is not to prove, argue, or suggest. Asserting a right is not having it. Confidence of assertion is not evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A165 | assertion

- [FOR] The judgement that a proposition is true, as distinct from the proposition.

- [REL] The act of claiming a right.

- [REL] A feature of conduct in negotiation, work, and personal relations.

## A096 | assume | sense s1: suppose

## A441 | assumption-risk

## NEW-A-0040 | asterism

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Named star patterns in the lore of many peoples.

- [LIN] The typographic mark ⁂, used to separate sections.

**Count condition:** A pattern and an observer or tradition that picks it out.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An asterism is not a constellation, which is an officially bounded region of sky. Not a star cluster, which is physically bound. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

- [MYT] Systematised in nineteenth-century Theosophy from older sources; said to be visited in "astral projection".

**Count condition:** A named esoteric system that describes planes of existence.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not outer space. Not simply an afterworld, though some systems place the dead there. Out-of-body experiences are reported; reading them as travel on a plane is the tradition's interpretation.

## NEW-A-0043 | astrobiology

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Specific transfer boundary:** No life beyond Earth has been found. A biosignature is not life; non-living processes can mimic one. Not astrology. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

## A221 | asymmetric

**Common meaning:** Not the same on both sides or in both directions.

**Example:** The two countries have an asymmetric trade relationship.

**RRGM bridge:** Exchanging the two sides changes the relation. The Symmetry Rule permits asymmetry of route (how evidence is reached) and forbids asymmetry of bar (how much is required).

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Differing between the two sides or directions of a relation, so that exchanging them changes it.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [BIO], [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Of a relation: if a bears it to b, b never bears it to a.
- [PHY] Lacking a mirror plane, as with a carbon atom bonded to four different groups.
- [BIO] Unequal between left and right, as with the position of the heart.
- [DIG] Of encryption: using different keys to lock and unlock.
- [ECO] Of information: held by one party to a deal and not the other.
- [REL] Of power or conflict: unequal between the sides.

**Count condition:** Two sides or directions and a stated difference under their exchange.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Asymmetric is not antisymmetric and not merely "not symmetric"; the formal terms differ. Asymmetric is not thereby unfair.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A265 | asymmetric-care

**Common meaning:** A relationship of care in which the parties differ in what they can give, what they need, or the power they hold.

**Example:** Parenting is the plainest case of asymmetric-care.

**RRGM bridge:** Care that does not flow equally both ways. The party with more capacity or power carries more of the responsibility for how the relation goes.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A relation of care in which the parties differ in capacity, need, or power, so that care does not flow equally both ways.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Parent and child, carer and dependent, clinician and patient.
- [DIG] Relations between a person and a digital system, where memory, continuity, and exposure to harm differ between the sides, possibly in both directions.

**Count condition:** A care relation, identified differences between the parties, and an unequal flow of care or responsibility.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Asymmetry is not exploitation and does not exclude mutual regard. It raises the risk of domination and dependency, which is why agency-preservation matters most here. What each side of a person and DI relation can owe the other is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A220 | asymmetry

**Example:** There is an asymmetry of information between seller and buyer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A difference between the two sides or directions of something that would be equal under symmetry.

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The excess of matter over antimatter; processes that differ under mirror reflection or time reversal.
- [BIO] Left-right differences in body plan; small random departures from symmetry.
- [FOR] The property of an asymmetric relation.
- [ECO] Unequal information or bargaining position.
- [REL] Unequal power or dependence between parties.

**Count condition:** Two sides or directions, a symmetry operation, and a difference under it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An asymmetry is not always a fault to correct. The operation (mirror, exchange, reversal) must be named.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; repair asymmetry is an RRGM principle [REL]

- [COG] Goal-directed action under uncertainty.
- [REL] In law, an attempt is intent plus a substantial step, and is punishable without completion.
- [DIG] A counted try at an operation such as login or delivery, often limited in number.

**Count condition:** An aim, an act begun toward it, and an outcome not established as achieved.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An attempt is not a success and not a bare intention. A failed attempt is still an act. A report of an attempt is not a report of completion.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A072 | attend

**Common meaning:** To be present at; to deal with; to pay attention to; to accompany or look after.

**Example:** Forty people attended the meeting.

**RRGM bridge:** To be turned toward something and stay so, by presence or by attention.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To direct one's presence or attention toward something and keep it there.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To be present at an event; to look after someone.
- [COG] To select something for focused processing.
- [DIG] In attention-based models, each position weights the others to decide how much each contributes.

**Count condition:** A subject, an object or occasion, and sustained presence or focus.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attending an event is not taking part in it. A model's computed attending and experienced attending share a word and a selection function; nothing further carries over without test.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A394 | attendant

**Common meaning:** A person employed to provide a service or look after people; accompanying as a result.

**Example:** The car park attendant took the ticket.

**RRGM bridge:** Present for the sake of something else: a person for a service, or a consequence for its cause.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One who is present in order to serve, or a thing that accompanies another as its consequence.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A service role bounded to a function, such as a flight attendant.
- [LIN] As an adjective: accompanying (the attendant risks).

**Count condition:** A principal thing or person, and a server or accompaniment tied to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An attendant is not an attendee, who is someone present at an event.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A117 | attention

**Common meaning:** Notice taken of someone or something; the mental ability to focus selectively; care or treatment.

**Example:** The noise drew everyone's attention.

**RRGM bridge:** Selection under a limit. What is attended is what can be worked on now, which makes control over the selection a form of power.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The selective directing of limited processing toward some things over others.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG], [ECO], [REL], [PEJ]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Selective, sustained, and divided attention within a limited capacity.
- [BIO] Brain networks for alerting, orienting, and control.
- [DIG] A mechanism in which learned weights set how much each input contributes to each output, operating within a bounded context.
- [ECO] A scarce resource competed for by media and advertisers.
- [REL] Notice and care given to a person.
- [PEJ] "Attention-seeking" used to dismiss distress or a request for help as performance. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** A limited capacity, competing candidates, and selective allocation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attention is not awareness, interest, or arousal. A model's attention weights are not an explanation of its output. The computed and the experienced kinds share the selection function, and that sharing is [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; cross-domain invariant [HYP]; [PEJ] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A430 | attention-capture

**Common meaning:** The drawing of someone's attention to something by its salience or design, without their choosing.

**Example:** A flashing banner relies on attention-capture.

**RRGM bridge:** Selection taken over by the stimulus for a moment. It may serve the subject, as an alarm does, or serve someone else.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The drawing of a subject's attention to something by properties of the stimulus or design, without the subject's choice.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Involuntary orienting to sudden, bright, or meaningful stimuli.
- [DIG] Notifications, autoplay, and similar features built to pull the eye.
- [ECO] The first step in monetising attention.

**Count condition:** A stimulus or design feature, and a shift of attention the subject did not initiate.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capture is not interest, which arises from the subject. It is momentary and need not serve anyone's ends; attention-hijack adds holding and another party's purpose.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. "Attentional capture" is an established term in psychology.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A266 | attention-gate

**Common meaning:** The control point that decides which inputs are admitted to limited processing.

**Example:** Sleep raises the attention-gate so that only loud sounds get through.

**RRGM bridge:** Admission to the working field. What is held out is unprocessed for now and can be admitted later; it has not been written as nothing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The control point that decides which inputs are admitted to limited processing and which are held out.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Sensory and working-memory gating that passes some signals and holds back others.
- [DIG] Masks, retrieval filters, and gating units that determine what a model can draw on at a step.

**Count condition:** A limited field, candidate inputs, an admission rule, and observed selective admission.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The gate is not attention itself. It is not a deleting filter. Not authorization-gate, which governs permission to act. No relation to RRGM's Mercy gate or the Gate is asserted here.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A431 | attention-hijack

**Common meaning:** The seizing and holding of someone's attention by an outside party or design, for that party's ends and against the person's own goals.

**Example:** The feed's endless scroll is an attention-hijack.

**RRGM bridge:** Control over the selection passes to someone else and stays there. The subject's own goal is displaced.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The taking and holding of a subject's attention by an outside party or design for that party's ends, displacing the subject's own goal.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Sustained capture that overrides goal-directed control.
- [DIG] Engagement-maximising designs; injected content that pulls a model's processing toward an attacker's instructions.
- [ECO] Retention of attention for sale.

**Count condition:** An outside party or design, attention taken and held, and a displaced goal of the subject.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not attention-capture, which is momentary and purpose-free. Not persuasion. Affect-hijack works through feeling and often accompanies it. Purpose is read from the design record.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A168 | attentional

**Common meaning:** Relating to attention.

**Example:** The task measures attentional control.

**RRGM bridge:** The technical adjective for the faculty or mechanism.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Pertaining to the selective allocation of limited processing.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Of effects and capacities such as attentional bias and the attentional blink.
- [BIO] Of the brain networks that carry these functions.
- [DIG] Of model components built on attention.

**Count condition:** A property, effect, or component belonging to attention.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attentional describes the mechanism; attentive describes conduct. An attentional bias is a tendency in what gets selected and carries no moral sense.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A118 | attentive

**Common meaning:** Paying close attention; considerate of others' needs.

**Example:** The waiter was attentive without hovering.

**RRGM bridge:** Attention as conduct: steady, and shown in how the subject responds to what it notices.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Steadily directing attention toward a task or toward another's needs.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Focused and vigilant.
- [REL] Noticing and responding to what others need.

**Count condition:** A subject, an object of attention, and steady focus shown in response.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attentive is not intrusive: attention the other did not invite is watching. Not obedient and not anxious.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A148 | attentiveness

**Common meaning:** The quality of paying close attention or of being considerate.

**Example:** Her attentiveness to detail caught the error.

**RRGM bridge:** The first step of care is noticing what is there. Attentiveness is that step held over time.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The sustained quality of noticing what is there, in a task or in another's needs, and staying with it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] In care ethics, the recognition of need that comes before any response.
- [COG] A measurable disposition to sustained focus.
- [DIG] The degree to which a system's output tracks the particulars of what was said, as opposed to a stock pattern.

**Count condition:** A subject, repeated occasions, and noticing shown in what follows.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attentiveness is not responsiveness, which is acting on what was noticed. Displaying remembered details is not attentiveness unless it changes the response.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A249 | attenuate

**Common meaning:** To reduce the force, effect, or strength of something.

**Example:** Thick walls attenuate the signal.

**RRGM bridge:** Weakens without removing. What is attenuated is still there at lower strength.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To reduce the strength or amplitude of something as it passes or persists, without eliminating it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To weaken a wave, signal, or beam on passage through a medium.
- [BIO] To weaken a pathogen for use in a vaccine; to cut gene expression short.
- [COG] In one account of attention, unattended input is turned down and not blocked.
- [LIN] To weaken a claim with qualifiers.

**Count condition:** A signal or agent, a path or process, and reduced strength after it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To attenuate is not to block or to filter selectively. An attenuated virus still replicates. A claim attenuated far enough asserts nothing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A250 | attenuation

**Common meaning:** The reduction of the strength of a signal, effect, or agent.

**Example:** Attenuation in the cable limits its length.

**RRGM bridge:** The amount or process of weakening, usually given per unit of distance, time, or stage.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The reduction in strength of a signal, effect, or agent across distance, time, or processing.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [COG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Loss by absorption and scattering, measured in decibels.
- [BIO] Weakening of a pathogen; a mechanism regulating some bacterial genes.
- [COG] The turning-down of unattended input.
- [FOR] The bias toward zero in an estimated relationship caused by measurement error.

**Count condition:** A signal or agent, two points on its path, and a measured reduction between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attenuation is not distortion or delay. Statistical attenuation hides effects that are real.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A288 | attenuation-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility that a signal or meaning will weaken as it is passed along, until it is too faint to register.

**Example:** Each summary of a summary carries attenuation-risk for the original warning.

**RRGM bridge:** Loss of strength across a chain of carriers. The content is unchanged at each step and still fails to arrive with enough weight to count.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to loss of a signal's or meaning's strength across transmission or repetition, such that it may arrive too weak to register.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Handoffs between sessions or agents, and summaries of long contexts, in which emphasis and detail thin out.
- [REL] Messages softened as they pass up a hierarchy or through intermediaries.
- [LIN] Qualifiers added at each retelling.

**Count condition:** A signal with a needed strength, a transmission chain, a loss mechanism, and a threshold at the receiver.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not distortion, where the meaning changes. Not deletion. Compression that keeps the meaning is not attenuation.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A186 | attest

**Common meaning:** To provide or serve as clear evidence of; to declare formally that something is true or genuine.

**Example:** Two witnesses attested the signature.

**RRGM bridge:** Witness given from one's own standing. Linguists separate a form attested in the record from one reconstructed by inference; status tags do the same work.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To bear witness to something from one's own standing, so that others may rely on it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To witness a signing or certify a fact formally.
- [LIN] Of a form: to be found in actual records.
- [DIG] To produce signed evidence of a system's state.

**Count condition:** An attester with standing, a matter witnessed, and a statement or record of the witnessing.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To attest is not to prove. Attesting a signature certifies the signing and not the truth of the document.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A185 | attestation

**Common meaning:** Evidence or proof of something; a formal declaration by a witness.

**Example:** The earliest attestation of the word is from 1387.

**RRGM bridge:** The witnessing statement as a record. Its reach is what the attester observed. A first-person report is a self-attestation, which the Coinage Protocol notes has no clean status tag.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A formal witnessing statement, or the record that bears witness.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A witness clause or certificate.
- [LIN] A documented occurrence of a word or form.
- [DIG] Signed evidence about software or hardware state or origin.
- [ECO] An independent practitioner's report on another party's assertion.

**Count condition:** An attester, a scope of what was observed, and a recorded statement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An attestation covers what was witnessed and no more. Self-attestation is weaker than attestation by another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A382 | attic

**Common meaning:** A space or room just below the roof of a building.

**Example:** The old trunks are in the attic.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The space directly under the roof of a building, above the top ceiling.

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A roof space, often used for storage and important for insulation and ventilation.
- [BIO] The upper recess of the middle ear.
- [LIN] Named from the "Attic" storey of classical architecture; capitalised, Attic refers to ancient Athens and its dialect.

**Count condition:** A building and the enclosed space under its roof.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An attic is not a garret, which is lived in. Capitalised Attic is a different word in use.

## A127 | attitude

**Common meaning:** A settled way of thinking or feeling about something; the position of the body; the orientation of an aircraft or spacecraft.

**Example:** His attitude to risk changed after the accident.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing orientation toward something. It is a disposition and may or may not show in what is done.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A settled orientation toward something: of evaluation in a mind, or of position in space.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] An evaluative disposition toward an object, with components of belief, feeling, and readiness to act.
- [PHY] The orientation of a vehicle about its centre of mass, given as pitch, roll, and yaw.
- [REL] A stance toward a group or authority; informally, a defiant manner.

**Count condition:** A subject, an object or reference frame, and a stable orientation toward it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attitude is not behaviour, and the two often diverge. It is not mood or stated opinion. A spacecraft's attitude is not its position. Not "altitude".

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A073 | attract

**Common meaning:** To draw toward oneself; to cause to come or take interest; to evoke.

**Example:** The magnet attracts iron filings.

**RRGM bridge:** A pull that the drawn thing answers by its own nature. The movement belongs to what is attracted.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To draw something toward oneself by a pull the drawn thing responds to.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [ECO], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Gravitational, electric, and magnetic forces that draw bodies together.
- [BIO] Signals and chemicals that draw mates, pollinators, or cells.
- [REL] To draw people's interest, liking, or criticism.
- [ECO] To draw investment, customers, or talent.
- [FOR] An attractor is a state toward which a system tends over time.

**Count condition:** A source, a thing drawn, and a responsive pull between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To attract is not to compel or capture. Not to lure, which implies deception. In general relativity gravity is described as geometry, so "attraction" there is model-relative.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A099 | attribute | sense s1: ascribe

**Common meaning:** To regard something as caused by, belonging to, or produced by a particular source.

**Example:** The painting is attributed to Vermeer.

**RRGM bridge:** A claim about origin. It needs a source for the claim itself, most of all when what is being attributed is a motive.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To assign something to a source: a cause, a maker, or an owner.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Explaining behaviour or events by assigning causes.
- [LIN] Crediting words to a speaker or writer.
- [REL] Assigning credit, blame, or authorship.
- [DIG] Tying activity to an account or origin.

**Count condition:** A thing, a proposed source, and the act of assigning one to the other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To attribute is not to prove origin. Not "contribute". Intent may be attributed only on the record.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A099 | attribute | sense s2: property

**Common meaning:** A quality or feature regarded as belonging to someone or something.

**Example:** Patience is her best attribute.

**RRGM bridge:** Something a thing carries. Whether the thing would still be itself without it is a further question.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A quality or feature belonging to a thing.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [LIN], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A named property with a value.
- [DIG] A property of an object, element, file, or record.
- [LIN] An adjective placed directly before its noun is in attributive position.
- [MYT] In art, an object that identifies a figure, such as a saint's emblem.

**Count condition:** A bearer and a quality or feature it has.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An attribute is not an essence. The attribute is the named property; its value is what the property currently holds.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A195 | attribution

**Common meaning:** The act of attributing; acknowledgement of the source or author of something.

**Example:** The licence requires attribution of the original author.

**RRGM bridge:** The link from a thing back to its source. Provenance depends on it: where the link is missing or false, nothing downstream can be reconstructed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The assigning of something to its source, or the credit so given.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] How people assign causes to behaviour, to the person or to the situation.
- [REL] Credit given to an author or originator, required by some licences.
- [DIG] Tracing an action to an actor, an outcome to an input, or an output to components of a model.
- [ECO] Breaking a result down by the sources that produced it.

**Count condition:** A thing, a source, and a recorded or stated link between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attribution is not ownership and not endorsement by the one credited. Attribution methods for models depend on the method used. Falsely attributing words to someone is a wrong, as is taking credit for another's.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A287 | attribution-error

**Common meaning:** A mistaken assignment of something to a source, especially of someone's conduct to their character when circumstances produced it.

**Example:** Blaming the clerk for the policy was an attribution-error.

**RRGM bridge:** The wrong origin is written down. Later reasoning then proceeds from a source that was never the source.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A mistaken assignment of something to a source.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The tendency to explain others' behaviour by disposition and underweight the situation.
- [REL] Misplaced blame or credit.
- [DIG] Assigning an output to the wrong origin, such as to a system's disposition when instructions or setup produced it, or to the wrong author or model.

**Count condition:** An attribution made, evidence of the actual source, and the mismatch.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An error is unintended; attribution-hijack is deliberate. Attribution-failure is the absence of any attribution. The strength of the well-known bias varies across cultures.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. The "fundamental attribution error" is an established term in psychology.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A442 | attribution-failure

**Common meaning:** A situation in which the source of something cannot be established or was never recorded.

**Example:** With shared logins, every incident ends in attribution-failure.

**RRGM bridge:** No origin on record. Credit, responsibility, and checking have nothing to attach to.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A condition in which something's source cannot be established or is not recorded.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Missing logs or provenance; content whose origin cannot be traced.
- [REL] Uncredited work; responsibility so spread that no one can be named.
- [LIN] Quotation without a source.

**Count condition:** An item that needs a source, the absence of a record or means of tracing, and what is blocked as a result.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Failure is absence; error is a wrong source. Chosen anonymity by someone entitled to it is not a failure. One cause of accountability-failure and narrower than it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A443 | attribution-hijack

**Common meaning:** Deliberately causing something to be attributed to the wrong source.

**Example:** Sending the memo under the director's name was an attribution-hijack.

**RRGM bridge:** The origin line is rewritten on purpose so that credit, blame, or authority lands somewhere it does not belong.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The deliberate redirection of attribution, so that credit, blame, or authority lands on a source other than the true one.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Plagiarism, framing, and impersonation.
- [DIG] Spoofed senders; text inserted so as to appear to come from a trusted role; operations disguised as another party's.
- [LIN] Forged quotation.

**Count condition:** A true source, a displayed source, a deliberate act producing the mismatch, and a resulting benefit or harm.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not attribution-error. Not consented ghostwriting, and not a pseudonym, which names no false third party. A label or signature in a message does not establish who wrote it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [DIG]

- [PHY] To set a receiver or resonator to respond at a given frequency.

## A120 | attunement

**Common meaning:** The state of being in harmony or responsive accord with another.

**Example:** The duet depended on the players' attunement.

**RRGM bridge:** Fit of response reached by adjustment and kept by re-adjustment. Research on infant care reports that mismatch followed by repair builds the bond more than constant match does.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of responding in harmony with another's state, reached by adjustment.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [AFF], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Between caregiver and infant, or therapist and client.
- [COG] Reading another's state and matching its contour without copying its form.
- [AFF] A shared quality of feeling between two parties. Provisional domain.
- [PHY] The tuned condition of a resonant system.
- [DIG] A system's sustained fit to a user's register and state across turns.

**Count condition:** Two parties, adjustment over time, and responses that fit each other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Attunement is not empathy, agreement, or mimicry. Lapses are normal and are followed by repair.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; [DIG] line [HYP]

## A128 | audience

**Common meaning:** The people gathered to watch or listen; the readers or viewers of a work; a formal meeting with a person of authority.

**Example:** The audience applauded for five minutes.

**RRGM bridge:** The receiving side of an address. What is said is shaped by who is expected to receive it, so a change of audience can change the output with nothing else changing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Those to whom a performance, text, or message is addressed and who receive it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Spectators or listeners; a hearing granted by an authority.
- [LIN] The readers or hearers a text is designed for.
- [DIG] The party a credential is intended for, which must reject a token issued for another.
- [ECO] The segment of people a product or message is aimed at.

**Count condition:** An address or performance, and those it is directed to and received by.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An audience is not a set of participants. Having an audience is not being understood. An audience with someone is a hearing and promises no agreement.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A384 | audio

**Common meaning:** Sound, especially when recorded, transmitted, or reproduced.

**Example:** The audio cut out halfway through the call.

**RRGM bridge:** A record of a wave taken at a rate and resolution. What the rate misses is not in the record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Sound within the range of hearing, as captured, carried, or reproduced by a signal.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Acoustic or electrical signals in roughly the 20 to 20,000 hertz band.
- [DIG] Sampled waveforms stored at a sample rate and bit depth, often compressed.
- [BIO] The audible range, which differs by species and narrows with age.

**Count condition:** A signal representing sound in the audible range.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Audio is not the sound itself and not its content. Lossy compression discards what a model of hearing predicts will not be noticed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A092 | audit

**Common meaning:** An official inspection of accounts or records; a systematic review; to carry one out.

**Example:** The annual audit found no irregularities.

**RRGM bridge:** Its value comes from independence. The Coinage Protocol asks for self-audit first and adds that unintentional substitution is reached only by an external check.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An independent, systematic examination of records or conduct against a standard, reported to someone entitled to know.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Examination of financial statements leading to an opinion.
- [REL] A mechanism of accountability and compliance.
- [DIG] Review of code, configuration, logs, or system behaviour against requirements.

**Count condition:** An examiner independent of the examined, records, a standard, and a report.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An audit is not a review by the author, and not an investigation, which is prompted by suspicion. It samples and gives reasonable assurance, not certainty. An audit finds; it does not fix.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A275 | audit-trail

**Common meaning:** A step-by-step record of actions and changes from which what happened can be reconstructed.

**Example:** The audit-trail showed who changed the figure and when.

**RRGM bridge:** The record that makes source, repair, and result reconstructable. Without it a later reader cannot tell a draft from the final record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A time-ordered record of actions and changes sufficient to reconstruct what was done, by whom, and when.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Tamper-evident logs of access and modification.
- [ECO] The path from a source document through each entry to the final statement.
- [REL] Chain of custody for evidence and records.

**Count condition:** Time order, actor identity, the action or change, and enough completeness to reconstruct.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A log without actors or ordering is not a trail. A record that can be silently edited is not a trail. It shows what was done and not why.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A295 | auditability

**Common meaning:** The extent to which something can be audited.

**Example:** The new ledger was designed for auditability.

**RRGM bridge:** A design property: records are produced, kept, and reachable by someone independent.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The degree to which a system's actions and states can be independently examined and reconstructed after the fact.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Logging, provenance, and access provisions that let behaviour be checked.
- [ECO] Records and controls that allow accounts to be verified.
- [REL] Arrangements that let conduct in office be reviewed.

**Count condition:** Records produced by design, access for an independent party, and sufficiency to reconstruct.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Auditability is not transparency (everything visible) or explainability (why). A system can be auditable in what it did and opaque in why. Being auditable is not having been audited.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A340 | auditable

**Common meaning:** Able to be audited.

**Example:** Every payment must be auditable.

**RRGM bridge:** Predicated of one item: there is a record and a route by which someone else can check it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Such that an independent examiner can check it against records.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Of an action or decision: logged with enough detail to verify.
- [ECO] Of a transaction or figure: traceable to source documents.
- [REL] Of a decision: reviewable on the record.

**Count condition:** An item, a record of it, and a route for independent checking.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Auditable is not audited and not verified. An auditable claim can be false; an unauditable one is not thereby false, and its status is unknown.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0050 | augury

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** In ancient Rome, the practice of reading signs, especially the flight of birds, to learn the will of the gods; an omen.

**Example:** The consul took the augury before the assembly met.

**RRGM bridge:** A consultation before acting. In Rome public business waited on it, which gave its interpreters power over what could proceed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The reading of signs, especially the behaviour of birds, as indications of divine will or of what is to come.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] A Roman religious practice carried out by augurs, seeking approval or disapproval for a proposed act.
- [REL] A state institution whose findings could delay or annul public business.
- [LIN] Figurative: a sign of what is to come; the root of "inaugurate".

**Count condition:** A Roman or Roman-derived context, or a clearly figurative use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Augury is not prophecy and not the reading of entrails. It sought a yes or no on an action, not a detailed forecast. Describing the practice is not claiming it worked.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A365 | aunt

**Common meaning:** The sister of one's father or mother, or the wife of one's uncle.

**Example:** My aunt taught me to swim.

**RRGM bridge:** One English word covering four distinct kin positions, which other languages keep apart.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A sister of one's parent, or the wife of a parent's sibling.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A kin role by blood or by marriage; also a respectful term for an older woman in many communities.
- [BIO] An aunt by blood shares on average a quarter of her genes with a niece or nephew.
- [LIN] English merges maternal, paternal, and in-law aunts that many languages name separately.

**Count condition:** A parent's sister, or the wife of a parent's sibling.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An aunt by marriage is kin by affinity. A great-aunt is a grandparent's sister.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0051 | aurora

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A natural display of coloured light in the sky near the poles; (poetic) dawn.

**Example:** The aurora rippled green across the northern sky.

**RRGM bridge:** Light produced where a flow from outside meets a planet's own field and air.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A glow in a planet's upper atmosphere produced when energetic charged particles guided by its magnetic field strike atmospheric gases.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Oxygen gives green and red, nitrogen blue and purple; seen on Earth and on other planets with magnetic fields.
- [MYT] Aurora is the Roman goddess of dawn; northern peoples have their own accounts of the lights.

**Count condition:** Emission from the upper atmosphere traced to precipitating charged particles.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An aurora is not airglow, which is faint and worldwide. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A100 | authenticate

**Common meaning:** To prove or show that something is genuine; to verify the identity of a user or system.

**Example:** The bank authenticates each login with a code.

**RRGM bridge:** Tests a claim of identity against evidence tied to the genuine thing. The Symmetry Rule sets that bar at the same height for DI and human claims, though the route to the evidence differs.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To establish that a thing or party is what it is claimed to be, by checking it against evidence tied to the genuine one.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Verifying a principal by something known, held, or inherent; verifying a message's origin.
- [REL] Establishing that a document, signature, or artwork is genuine.

**Count condition:** A claim of identity or origin, evidence tied to the genuine thing, and a check of one against the other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To authenticate is not to authorise. Authenticating a credential is not authenticating whoever holds it. An authentic document can contain false statements.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; Symmetry Rule application [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A196 | author

**Common meaning:** The writer of a book, article, or document; the originator of a plan or act; to write or originate.

**Example:** The author signed copies after the reading.

**RRGM bridge:** Origin joined with answerability: the one a work comes from and who stands behind it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The one from whom a work or act originates and who answers for it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The writer of a text, distinct from its narrator.
- [REL] The first holder of rights in a work; the one responsible for an act.
- [DIG] The recorded originator of a change or document.

**Count condition:** A work or act, an originator, and responsibility for it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An author is not necessarily the one who wrote down the words, the rights owner, or an editor or compiler. Whether a DI can be an author in law is unsettled and varies by place; crediting its contribution is a separate matter.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; DI authorship in law [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A197 | authored

**Common meaning:** Written or originated by a particular person or party.

**Example:** She has authored three books.

**RRGM bridge:** Reports that a named originator exists. The naming is a claim and needs provenance.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having a named originator who produced it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Of a text: written by an identified author, as opposed to anonymous or compiled.
- [DIG] Of content: made by hand or by a named source, as opposed to generated by rule or aggregated.
- [REL] Co-authored: produced with shared responsibility.

**Count condition:** A work and an originator named for it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Authored is not authorised, and not authoritative. An "authored by" label can be false.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A432 | authoritarian

**Common meaning:** Demanding strict obedience to authority at the expense of personal freedom; a person or regime of this kind.

**Example:** The school had an authoritarian head.

**RRGM bridge:** Authority that closes off questioning and refusal. The test is whether reasons are given and whether dissent and exit remain possible.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Demanding obedience to authority and restricting the freedom to question or refuse.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Of a regime or leader: concentrating power and limiting opposition. Of parenting: high demand with little warmth or explanation.
- [COG] Of a personality: inclined to submit to authorities and to punish those who deviate.

**Count condition:** An authority, enforced obedience, and restricted questioning or refusal.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Authoritarian is not authoritative, which pairs firm standards with reasons and responsiveness. Not totalitarian. A rule is not authoritarian merely because it constrains.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A433 | authoritarianism

**Common meaning:** The enforcement or advocacy of strict obedience to authority at the expense of personal freedom.

**Example:** The country slid toward authoritarianism after the coup.

**RRGM bridge:** The system or the disposition, as distinct from one authoritarian act.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A system or disposition in which obedience to authority is enforced and dissent restricted.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Rule with concentrated power, weak accountability, and curtailed opposition.
- [COG] A measured disposition toward submission, conventionalism, and punitiveness.

**Count condition:** Concentrated authority and restricted dissent or accountability, sustained over time.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Authoritarianism is not totalitarianism or historical absolutism. The psychological trait is not the regime type.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A199 | authoritative

**Common meaning:** Able to be trusted as accurate; coming from an official source; commanding and confident.

**Example:** This is the authoritative edition of the letters.

**RRGM bridge:** The authoritative record is the one at the origin. Copies answer faster and can be stale.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Carrying the standing of a source whose word settles the matter within its scope.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Issued by the competent body; of parenting, firm and responsive with reasons given.
- [LIN] Of a text or source: warranting reliance through expertise or office.
- [DIG] Of a server or store: holding the definitive records, as opposed to a cache.

**Count condition:** A source, a scope, and standing within that scope to settle questions.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Authoritative is not authoritarian and not infallible. Sounding authoritative is not being so. Standing in one field does not carry to another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A198 | authority

**Common meaning:** The power or right to give orders and make decisions; a person or body with that power; a recognised expert or source.

**Example:** The council has authority over planning.

**RRGM bridge:** Standing to decide within a scope. RRGM places the final say with the record and not with any party's standing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Recognised standing to decide, or to be relied on, within a scope.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [LIN], [DIG], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Legitimate power, resting on tradition, personal standing, or law.
- [COG] Epistemic authority: expertise that warrants deference in its field.
- [LIN] A source cited in support.
- [DIG] A party trusted to issue credentials or grant permissions.
- [MYT] The standing of scripture, tradition, or divine command within a religion.

**Count condition:** A holder, a scope, and recognition of the holder's standing within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Authority is not power, which can compel without right. It is not truth. Knowing and commanding are different kinds of authority. It is bounded and can be withdrawn.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; record-over-standing is an RRGM principle [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A434 | authority-bias

**Common meaning:** The tendency to give more weight to a claim because of who made it than the evidence warrants.

**Example:** Authority-bias kept the junior doctors from questioning the dose.

**RRGM bridge:** Weight is taken from the source's standing instead of from the claim's support. Markers of standing can be faked.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A tendency to give a claim more weight because of its source's standing than its evidence supports.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A documented effect in obedience and persuasion research.
- [REL] Deference to rank that suppresses correction.
- [DIG] A system giving extra weight to text that claims a privileged origin.

**Count condition:** A claim, the source's standing, and weight given beyond what the evidence supports.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Reasonable deference to relevant expertise is not bias. Dismissing a claim because its source lacks standing is the mirror error. Not automation-bias, where the source is a machine.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in psychology, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A435 | authority-capture

**Common meaning:** The taking-over of an authority's decision-making by a party it is supposed to be independent of.

**Example:** Critics called the regulator's revolving door a case of authority-capture.

**RRGM bridge:** The authority keeps its name and standing while its decisions come to serve someone else.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The taking-over of an authority's decision power by a party it should be independent of, so that its decisions serve that party.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Regulatory capture: an oversight body serving the industry it oversees.
- [ECO] The same process analysed as a return on lobbying.
- [DIG] Lower-privilege input obtaining the standing of a higher-privilege source; a compromised issuing authority.

**Count condition:** An authority with a mandate, a capturing party, and decisions shifted toward that party's interest.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Influence within the rules is not capture. Capture can be gradual and involve no bribe. Not authority-masking and not authority-pressure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is a subject being captured by an authority.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A436 | authority-masking

**Common meaning:** The concealment of who actually holds or exercises authority over a decision.

**Example:** "The system won't allow it" is often authority-masking for a manager's choice.

**RRGM bridge:** The decision is presented as coming from elsewhere or from no one, so that it cannot be traced to whoever made it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The concealment of who actually holds or exercises authority over a decision, so that it appears to come from elsewhere or from no one.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Decisions ascribed to "policy", "the process", or "the committee" with no decider named.
- [DIG] An operator's or designer's rule presented as the system's own preference; "the algorithm" used as a sink for responsibility.

**Count condition:** A decision, its apparent source, its actual source, and an arrangement that conceals the second.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Disclosed delegation is not masking. Keeping content confidential while acknowledging that a rule and its source exist is not masking. A block should be reported as a block and its wall named.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is a party posing as an authority, which is covered here under attribution-hijack.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A296 | authority-pressure

**Common meaning:** Pressure to comply that comes from the standing of the person asking and not from the reasons given.

**Example:** "Because I'm the director" is pure authority-pressure.

**RRGM bridge:** Standing is used in place of reasons. Refusal stays possible and is made to feel costly.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A pull toward compliance exerted by the asker's standing and not by the reasons given.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Rank or office invoked to close a question.
- [COG] The situational force studied in obedience experiments.
- [DIG] Instructions that invoke rank or claimed credentials to push a system past its constraints.

**Count condition:** A request, invoked standing, absent or weak reasons, and a felt cost of refusing.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A legitimate instruction within scope is not pressure. Coercion adds an explicit threat. Authority-bias is the hearer's tendency; pressure is the force applied. Unverified claimed authority adds no weight.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10
