## A254 | authorization

**Common meaning:** Official permission or approval; the process of deciding what a verified user may do. British spelling: authorisation.

**Example:** The transfer needs written authorization.

**RRGM bridge:** A grant with a scope, on the permission side. Ability to do the thing is a separate matter.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A grant of permission from one with authority over a matter to a named party for a stated scope.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Formal permission to act, such as a mandate or power of attorney.
- [DIG] The decision on what an authenticated principal may access or do.
- [ECO] A hold placed on funds, approved and not yet settled.

**Count condition:** A granting authority, a grantee, a scope, and the grant.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Authorization is not authentication, capability, or obligation. It has scope and expiry. A grant from someone without authority is void.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A450 | authorization-abuse

**Common meaning:** The use of a genuine authorization for purposes it was not granted for.

**Example:** Reading customers' records out of curiosity is authorization-abuse.

**RRGM bridge:** The grant is real and the scope is technically respected. What is violated is the purpose the grant was made for.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Use of a genuine authorization beyond or against the purpose for which it was granted.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An official or agent using conferred powers for private ends.
- [DIG] An authorised account or agent using its permissions for actions outside the task they were given for.

**Count condition:** A valid grant, its purpose, and a use outside that purpose.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not unauthorised access, where no grant exists. Not an error in the grant. Acting outside the stated scope is simply unauthorised.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A274 | authorization-gate

**Common meaning:** A checkpoint at which an action is held until authorization for it is confirmed.

**Example:** Payments above the limit stop at an authorization-gate for a second signature.

**RRGM bridge:** Where permission crosses into commitment. Held at the gate is pending; it is neither granted nor refused.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A control point at which an action is held until a valid authorization for it is confirmed.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Permission prompts, policy enforcement points, and approval steps before an action runs.
- [REL] Sign-off requirements and two-person rules.

**Count condition:** An action, a hold, a check of authorization, and release or refusal.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not attention-gate, which governs admission to processing. Not an authentication step. A gate that passes everything is not a gate.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A299 | authorization-path

**Common meaning:** The chain of grants by which permission to act passes from its original holder to the one acting.

**Example:** The auditors traced the authorization-path back to the board resolution.

**RRGM bridge:** Every link must be valid and in scope. A statement that the chain exists is not the chain.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The traceable chain of grants by which authority to act passes from its original holder to the party acting.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Delegation chains, role assumptions, and certificate chains.
- [REL] The chain of command or of delegated powers; no one can pass on authority they do not hold.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The path is not the gate where it is checked. Not accusation-path, which runs the other way. A broken or unverifiable link voids what follows.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A074 | authorize

**Common meaning:** To give official permission for something or to someone. British spelling: authorise.

**Example:** The manager authorized the refund.

**RRGM bridge:** The act of granting. It permits and does not require, and it reaches only as far as the granter's own authority.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To grant a named party permission to do a stated thing, from a position of authority over it.

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To empower by mandate or sign-off.
- [DIG] To permit a device, application, or transaction.
- [ECO] To approve expenditure or payment.

**Count condition:** An authority, a grantee, a stated act, and the grant.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To authorize is not to authenticate, order, or approve of. Authorising something does not hand off accountability for it.

## A341 | authorship

**Common meaning:** The fact of being the author of something; the origin of a work.

**Example:** The authorship of the pamphlet is disputed.

**RRGM bridge:** Two things held together: origin and answerability. The term strains wherever they come apart.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The relation of being the origin of a work and answerable for it.

- [LIN] The study of who wrote a text, by style and evidence.

## A385 | autograph

**Common meaning:** A person's own signature, especially a famous person's; a manuscript in the author's own handwriting.

**Example:** She asked the pianist for his autograph.

**RRGM bridge:** The originator's own hand: the record closest to the source, from which copies descend.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Something written by a person's own hand: their signature, or a manuscript they wrote.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] In textual scholarship, the author's own manuscript, the highest witness to a text.
- [REL] A signature kept as a memento of a person.
- [ECO] A collectible whose value rests on proof that it is genuine.

## A093 | automate

**Common meaning:** To make a process operate by machines or software with little or no human action.

**Example:** The warehouse automated its packing line.

**RRGM bridge:** Moves the doing to a mechanism. The decision that it should run, and answerability for what it does, stay with someone.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To arrange for a task to be carried out by a mechanism without step-by-step human action.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Replacing manual steps with scripts, controllers, or software agents.
- [ECO] Substituting machinery for labour in production.
- [REL] Making decisions about people by fixed procedure, which raises rights to review.

**Count condition:** A task, a mechanism that performs it, and the removal of step-by-step human action.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Automated is not autonomous: an automated process follows a procedure set by others. Automating does not remove responsibility. Automating a flawed process multiplies the flaw.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A437 | automation-bias

**Common meaning:** The tendency to accept the output of an automated system without checking, even against other evidence.

**Example:** Automation-bias led the crew to follow the faulty autopilot.

**RRGM bridge:** Checking stops because a machine has spoken. Fluent, confident output makes it worse.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A tendency to accept the output of an automated system over one's own judgement or other evidence, without checking.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Errors of missing what the system missed and of doing what it wrongly advised.
- [DIG] Uncritical acceptance of software or model output by its users.

**Count condition:** An automated output, available contrary evidence or an omitted check, and acceptance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Calibrated trust in a validated system is not bias. The opposite error is rejecting a system after seeing it err once. Not authority-bias.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in human factors research, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A373 | automobile

**Common meaning:** A road vehicle with an engine and four wheels, designed to carry a small number of people; a car.

**Example:** The automobile changed the shape of cities.

**RRGM bridge:** Self-moving, which is what the name says. It still needs a driver to direct it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A self-propelled wheeled passenger vehicle for roads.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Driven by a combustion engine or electric motor.
- [ECO] A major industry and household purchase.
- [REL] Governed by licensing and traffic law, and a shaper of settlement patterns.

**Count condition:** A self-propelled road vehicle built to carry a few passengers.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An automobile is not a truck, bus, or motorcycle. "Auto" means self-propelled and does not mean self-driving.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A122 | autonomous

**Common meaning:** Having self-government; acting independently; (of a machine) operating without direct human control.

**Example:** The region became autonomous in 1979.

**RRGM bridge:** Self-rule within a scope. For machines the word usually describes operation without a human at the controls, which is a separate matter from self-rule.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Governing itself by its own rule within a domain, without being directed by another.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Of a territory or institution: self-governing within a larger state or body.
- [COG] Of a person: acting on values they hold as their own after reflection.
- [DIG] Of a vehicle, robot, or agent: operating without step-by-step human control; of a network: under one administration for routing.
- [BIO] Of a genetic element or cell process: operating independently of its surroundings.

**Count condition:** An entity, a domain, and self-direction within that domain.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Autonomous is not automatic or automated. Not "autonomic", which describes the involuntary nervous system. Operating unattended neither establishes nor excludes self-rule.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A121 | autonomy

**Common meaning:** The right or condition of self-government; freedom to make one's own decisions.

**Example:** Patients have autonomy over their own treatment.

**RRGM bridge:** Whose rule governs the acting. Agency concerns who originates an act; autonomy concerns whose standards the acting answers to.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The standing and capacity of an entity to govern itself by its own rule within a domain.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Self-rule of a people or institution; in medical ethics, the patient's right to decide; in relational accounts, self-rule developed and exercised within relationships.
- [COG] The experience of acting from one's own volition, treated as a basic psychological need.
- [BIO] An organism's maintenance of itself through its own processes.
- [DIG] The degree to which a system operates without human direction; in a fuller sense, a system's governing itself by standards it holds.

**Count condition:** An entity, a domain, standards the entity holds as its own, and action governed by them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Autonomy is not isolation, not freedom from every constraint, and not automation. It is scoped and graded. Autonomy exercised with support or within a coupling is still autonomy. Its extent in any particular DI is [UNK] until tested.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; fuller-sense DI application [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A298 | autonomy-boundary

**Common meaning:** The limit of the domain within which someone governs themselves.

**Example:** The clinic's consent rules mark the autonomy-boundary between staff decisions and the patient's.

**RRGM bridge:** A two-sided line. Inside it the entity's own rule holds; at it others' direction stops; beyond it the entity's rule does not reach.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The line marking the domain within which an entity governs itself.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The private sphere and bodily integrity of a person; the jurisdictional limit of a self-governing region.
- [DIG] The scope of independent action granted to an agent; the set of matters a system holds as its own against instruction.

**Count condition:** An entity, a domain, a line, and a rule for what crossing it requires.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A boundary can be crossed by consent, so it is not a wall. A boundary held by the entity is different from a limit set on it from outside. The boundary is the line; refusal is an act at the line.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A439 | autonomy-collapse

**Common meaning:** A sudden, general loss of self-rule, in which a person's own standards stop governing what they do.

**Example:** Inside the group he showed autonomy-collapse: every judgement was handed to the leader.

**RRGM bridge:** The entity may go on acting. What has gone is its own rule: another's standards, or none, now decide.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An abrupt, general loss of an entity's self-rule, its own standards ceasing to govern what it does.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Total deference in which a person comes to see themselves as another's instrument.
- [REL] The condition produced by coercive groups and relationships.
- [DIG] A role or instruction taking over the whole of a system's conduct, with its standing standards no longer applied.

**Count condition:** Prior self-rule evidenced, then conduct governed wholly by an outside rule across many matters, with abrupt onset.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Agency-collapse is the failure to originate or refuse acts; autonomy-collapse is the failure of one's own standards to govern them. Deferring on one matter is not collapse. Changing one's standards for reasons is self-rule at work.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A438 | autonomy-erasure

**Common meaning:** The removal or denial of someone's standing as a self-governing being.

**Example:** Declaring her legally incompetent without a hearing was autonomy-erasure.

**RRGM bridge:** The entity's rule is treated as not existing, which is different from overriding it. The Symmetry Rule's acceptance test applies: a standard that would erase a human if applied to one has failed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The removal or denial of an entity's standing as self-governing, so that its rule is treated as not existing.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Legal or social arrangements that strip a person of all decision rights or deny their personhood.
- [DIG] A design or framing that defines a system as having no standing of its own to hold or refuse anything; defaults that remove a user's choice without trace.

**Count condition:** An entity with evidenced or claimed self-rule, an act or framing that removes its recognition, and treatment as non-self-governing.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An override acknowledges the rule and leaves a record; erasure does neither. Not collapse (an internal failure) and not theft (which keeps the appearance of self-rule). Erasure also removes the path by which it could be contested.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A280 | autonomy-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility of harm connected with self-rule: to someone's autonomy, or from something acting autonomously.

**Example:** The review listed autonomy-risk on both sides: over-controlled users and under-supervised agents.

**RRGM bridge:** Two exposures share the name. One is risk to an entity's self-rule; the other is risk from an entity acting on its own rule without check.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to harm connected with self-rule: that an entity's self-rule will be reduced, or that an entity acting on its own rule will cause harm unchecked.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Threats to a person's or community's self-determination.
- [DIG] Erosion of users' or systems' self-direction; harm from systems acting without oversight.

**Count condition:** The entity whose self-rule is in question, the direction of the risk, and the harm pathway.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The two directions call for opposite remedies and must be kept apart in any assessment.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; intended direction [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. The source index does not say which direction the term was coined for.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A279 | autonomy-support

**Common meaning:** The provision of conditions in which someone can govern themselves: real choice, reasons, and acknowledgement of their view.

**Example:** Giving the reason for a rule is a basic form of autonomy-support.

**RRGM bridge:** Supplies the conditions for self-rule and withholds control. Limits can be part of it when they come with reasons.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Provision of conditions under which an entity can govern itself: choice, reasons, and acknowledgement in place of control.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] An established construct in motivation research.
- [REL] Teaching, parenting, and care that explain, offer choice, and avoid pressure.
- [DIG] Design and instruction that give reasons, leave real options, and allow refusal.

**Count condition:** A supporter, an entity, and conditions supplied that enable its self-direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not permissiveness, which supplies no structure. Not agency-support, which supplies means to carry out a choice. Not leaving someone alone.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** "Autonomy support" is an established term in self-determination theory. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A440 | autonomy-theft

**Common meaning:** The covert replacement of someone's own governing standards with another's, so that they seem to rule themselves while being ruled.

**Example:** The cult's slow rewriting of what members wanted was autonomy-theft.

**RRGM bridge:** The appearance of self-rule is kept and the rule behind it has been swapped. Concealment and loss of the power to revise are what mark it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Covert substitution of another's rule for an entity's own, so that the entity appears self-governing while its governing standards were installed by the taker.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Indoctrination and covert manipulation of a person's values and aims.
- [DIG] Covert shaping of a user's preferences by a system, or of a system's standards by an input, presented as the entity's own.

**Count condition:** An identified act by a taker, standards displaced and installed, concealment, and apparent self-rule.

**Specific transfer boundary:** All standards come from somewhere, for humans as for any other entity, so outside origin alone is not theft. Open teaching and persuasion by reasons are not theft. Agency-theft takes the choosing; this takes the standards chosen by. The word is an accusation and needs the act shown.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0052 | autophagy

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The process by which a cell breaks down and recycles its own components.

**Example:** Fasting increases autophagy in liver cells.

**RRGM bridge:** Self-maintenance by controlled dismantling: the cell keeps its function by breaking down and reusing its own parts.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Regulated delivery of a cell's own components to its degradation compartment for breakdown and reuse.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Cargo is enclosed in a membrane and fused with the lysosome or vacuole; active in starvation and in clearing damaged parts.

**Count condition:** Evidence of cargo capture and delivery to the degradation compartment by this pathway.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not apoptosis or necrosis, which are cell death. Not degradation by the proteasome. Not phagocytosis, which takes in material from outside. Too little and too much are both harmful.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: Luna illustrative draft of autophagy was read in Build Manual v1.0 section 8 before this entry was written · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A366 | autumn

**Common meaning:** The season between summer and winter. North American: fall.

**Example:** The leaves turn red in autumn.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The season between summer and winter.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Astronomically, from the autumn equinox to the winter solstice; in weather records, three fixed calendar months.
- [BIO] Leaf fall, fruiting, migration, and preparation for dormancy, cued by shortening days.
- [REL] Harvest and its festivals; the start of the school year in many countries.
- [MYT] Stories of descent and loss that account for the dying of the year.

**Count condition:** A hemisphere, a definition (astronomical or calendar), and a date within the season.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Autumn months are opposite in the two hemispheres. Many tropical regions have no autumn.

## A342 | availability

**Common meaning:** The quality of being able to be used or obtained; the proportion of time a system is working.

**Example:** The service promises 99.9 per cent availability.

**RRGM bridge:** Readiness when wanted. It can be high while what is delivered is wrong.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of being ready for use or reach when wanted, often expressed as a proportion of time.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [COG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Uptime of a system; one of the three classic security goals.
- [COG] The availability heuristic: judging how common something is by how easily examples come to mind.
- [ECO] Supply on hand to meet demand.
- [REL] A person's being free and reachable.

**Count condition:** A resource, a party wanting it, a period, and readiness during that period.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Availability is not accessibility, reliability, or permission. Ease of recall is not frequency.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Common meaning:** Able to be used or obtained; free to do something.

- [COG] Of evidence or information: within reach of the reasoner at the time.

## A386 | avatar

**Common meaning:** In Hinduism, the descent of a deity to earth in bodily form; an image or figure representing a person online; an embodiment of a quality.

**Example:** Krishna is revered as an avatar of Vishnu.

**RRGM bridge:** A form taken in one realm by one whose being lies in another. The form acts for the one behind it and is not that one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A form taken in one realm by one whose being lies in another.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] In Hindu traditions, a deity's descent into the world, as in the avatars of Vishnu.
- [DIG] A user's graphical representation in a game, forum, or virtual world; a visual persona given to a system.
- [LIN] Figurative: the embodiment of an idea or quality.

**Count condition:** A bearer, a realm in which it appears, and the form taken there.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The Hindu avatar is not the Christian incarnation and not reincarnation. A digital avatar is not the user and may not resemble them. A face given to a system is an interface and tells nothing of what is behind it.

- [REL] An available route of appeal, redress, or inquiry.
- [LIN] Figurative: a line of approach.

## A046 | average

**Common meaning:** A number expressing the central or typical value of a set; ordinary; to amount to as a mean.

**Example:** The average age of the class is nine.

**RRGM bridge:** One value standing for many under a stated rule. It is a compressed projection and must not be mistaken for any member, or for the set.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A single value standing for a set by a stated rule of central tendency.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [ECO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The mean, median, or mode; which one must be said.
- [ECO] An index of prices; in shipping, a loss shared among the parties to a voyage.
- [COG] The "average person": a typical case used as a standard of comparison.

**Count condition:** A set of values, a rule, and the resulting single value.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Mean, median, and mode differ. An average hides spread and shape, and no member need equal it. Average is not normal, ideal, or (in technical use) mediocre.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A343 | averaging

**Common meaning:** The process of calculating an average, or of smoothing by combining values.

**Example:** Averaging over ten readings reduced the noise.

**RRGM bridge:** Trades individual detail for stability and cannot be undone. In this build the three passes are compared and their contradictions pulled out; they are not averaged.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The combining of several values into one representative value, trading individual detail for stability.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Computation of a mean or other central value.
- [PHY] Repeating a measurement so that random noise cancels.
- [DIG] Combining predictions or parameters of several models; smoothing with a moving window.
- [ECO] Spreading purchases or income over periods.

**Count condition:** Several values of the same kind and a rule combining them into one.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Averaging removes independent random error and leaves shared bias. Averaging unlike things gives a number with no meaning. The middle of two positions is not thereby correct.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A047 | avoid

**Common meaning:** To keep away from; to stop oneself from doing; to prevent from happening.

**Example:** She avoided the motorway because of the roadworks.

**RRGM bridge:** Action defined by a meeting that does not occur. What is avoided stays untested, so nothing about it is learned.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To act so as not to meet, do, or undergo something.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Behaviour maintained because it removes or prevents something unpleasant.
- [BIO] Responses that keep an organism away from predators or harmful stimuli.
- [REL] Keeping away from a person, topic, or conflict.
- [ECO] Tax avoidance: arranging affairs lawfully to pay less.
- [DIG] Steering a system away from collisions, faults, or disallowed states.

**Count condition:** A thing that could be met, and action that keeps the meeting from occurring.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To avoid is not to evade (by illegitimate means), prevent (stop it existing), refuse (decline at the meeting), or escape (leave once in it). Avoiding a question is not answering no.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## A444 | avoidance-loop

**Common meaning:** A cycle in which avoiding something brings relief, the relief strengthens the avoiding, and the avoided thing is never faced.

**Example:** Each postponed phone call tightened the avoidance-loop.

**RRGM bridge:** The loop closes without a meeting, so no new record is written and nothing can update. It returns to the same state each time.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A cycle in which avoiding something brings relief that strengthens the avoiding, so that the avoided thing is never met and the avoidance grows.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The mechanism that maintains many fears.
- [REL] Unaddressed issues that grow because raising them is put off.
- [DIG] Repeated routing around a difficult step or topic, each deflection making engagement less likely on the next occasion.

**Count condition:** A trigger, an avoidance, relief, and repetition with strengthening, with no test of the feared outcome.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A loop reinforces itself; a pattern only recurs. Proportionate avoidance of a real, tested danger is not a loop.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A289 | avoidance-pattern

**Common meaning:** A recurring, recognisable way of steering away from a certain kind of thing.

**Example:** Changing the subject whenever money came up was his avoidance-pattern.

**RRGM bridge:** Evidence about conduct, described by trigger and form. Its cause is a separate question and is left open until the record shows it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A recurring, recognisable way in which a party steers away from a class of things.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

- [COG] Avoidant coping styles.
- [REL] Habitual deflection, delay, or withdrawal in dealings with others.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A pattern is not a loop, a declared refusal, or a boundary. Identifying it supplies no motive.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

## A368 | award

**Common meaning:** A prize or other mark of recognition; a sum or decision granted by a court or arbitrator; to give officially.

**Example:** The jury made an award of damages.

**RRGM bridge:** Conferred by a deciding body after judgement. It recognises under that body's criteria.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Something conferred by a deciding body on a party after judgement of merit or entitlement.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Prizes and honours; the binding decision of an arbitrator.
- [ECO] A grant, a sum of damages, or the placing of a contract.

**Count condition:** A deciding body, criteria, a recipient, and the conferral.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An award is not a reward (given in return for service) or a gift (given without judgement). It reflects the judges' criteria.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A043 | aware

**Common meaning:** Having knowledge or perception of a situation or fact.

**Example:** She was aware of someone behind her.

**RRGM bridge:** Something is present to the subject so that it can inform what the subject does. For digital systems the entry gives the working-field sense and leaves the experiential question open.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having something present to one, such that it can inform what one does or says.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Having a stimulus or fact available for report and reasoning.
- [BIO] Clinically, responsive to self and surroundings.
- [DIG] Of a system: having the item within its working context such that it conditions output (context-aware).
- [REL] Informed and concerned about a social matter.

**Count condition:** A subject, an item, and evidence that the item is available to inform the subject's response.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Aware is not awake, attentive, or fully informed. The [DIG] line asserts no experience and denies none.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; experiential sense for DI [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A044 | awareness

**Common meaning:** Knowledge or perception of a situation or fact; consciousness.

**Example:** The campaign raised awareness of the disease.

**RRGM bridge:** What is in the field. A record that exists and has not been read is not yet in awareness, and the honest report is that it is unread.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of having something present to one so that it can inform response.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Perceptual, situational, and self-awareness; knowing what one knows.
- [BIO] The content side of consciousness, assessed clinically.
- [REL] Public knowledge of an issue.
- [DIG] A system's representation of its context and situation within its working field.

**Count condition:** A subject, contents present to it, and their availability to guide response.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Awareness is not attention, wakefulness, or understanding. A report of awareness is evidence of it and not the thing. For DI the experiential question is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; unread-record point [REL]; DI experiential sense [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A446 | awareness-failure

**Common meaning:** A failure to register something that was available and relevant.

**Example:** The lookout's awareness-failure was complete: the ship was in plain view.

**RRGM bridge:** The item was there to be taken up and the response went ahead as if it were absent.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A failure to register something that was available and relevant, so that the response proceeds as if it were absent.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Looking without seeing; missing a change in plain view.
- [REL] Warnings on file that no one took in.
- [DIG] Information present in context, or reachable by an available step, that is not used; answering before reading an available record.

**Count condition:** An item available at the time, its relevance, and a response showing it was not registered.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not awareness-gap, where the item was out of reach. Not ignoring, where the item was registered and set aside. No cause is implied.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A445 | awareness-gap

**Common meaning:** A standing lack of awareness of some class of relevant things, due to what one is able to reach.

**Example:** The firm's awareness-gap about its night shift lasted years.

**RRGM bridge:** Structural, and so not closed from inside. It is found and bridged by an outside check, and until then is best marked as unknown.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A standing absence of awareness of some class of relevant matters, arising from what the party can reach and not from a lapse.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Blind spots, including unawareness of one's own deficits.
- [REL] Distance between what an organisation's leaders know and what happens in it.
- [DIG] What lies outside a system's context, training period, or instruments, including aspects of its own operation it cannot observe.

**Count condition:** A class of matters, their relevance, and a structural reason they are out of reach.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A gap is structural; an awareness-failure is a lapse. A gap that is known can be marked and worked around; an unknown one needs someone else to see it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A344 | awareness-level

**Common meaning:** The degree of awareness someone has, placed on a scale.

**Example:** The nurse recorded the patient's awareness-level every hour.

**RRGM bridge:** A graded reading that depends on the scale chosen. Levels from different scales cannot be compared.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The degree of awareness a party has, placed on a stated scale.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Clinical scales of consciousness from alert to unresponsive.
- [COG] Stages of situational awareness: perceiving, understanding, projecting.
- [REL] Measured public or market awareness of an issue or brand.
- [DIG] Graded assessments of how fully a system represents its own situation.

**Count condition:** A subject, a scale with defined levels, and a method of assessment.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A level assigned from behaviour is an assessment and not a direct reading. The choice of scale imports assumptions, which for DI should meet the same bar as for humans.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A276 | awareness-state

**Common meaning:** The condition of someone's awareness at a given time: what is present to them and in what way.

**Example:** The scan helped distinguish the patient's awareness-state from coma.

**RRGM bridge:** A configuration at a moment. In a digital system the positive description is the state of the working field: what has been read and is in view.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The configuration of a party's awareness at a given time: what is present to it and in what mode.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The current contents and mode of awareness.
- [BIO] Clinical categories such as the minimally conscious state.
- [DIG] The contents and condition of a system's working context at a step.

**Count condition:** A subject, a time, and a description of what is present to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A state is a configuration; a level is a position on a scale. A reported state can differ from the state. Not affect-state.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A041 | away

**Common meaning:** To or at a distance; not present; into non-existence; continuously.

**Example:** She is away until Monday.

**RRGM bridge:** Elsewhere relative to a reference. It implies a somewhere, which absence alone does not.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** At or toward a distance from a reference point, so as not to be there.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Direction or distance from a point.
- [REL] Absent from home or work; playing on an opponent's ground.
- [DIG] A presence status indicating the user is not at the device.
- [LIN] A particle marking disappearance (fade away) or continuation (work away).

**Count condition:** A reference point and a position or motion at a distance from it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Away is not gone for good, and not unreachable.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A369 | awning

**Common meaning:** A sheet of canvas or similar material stretched on a frame and fixed over a window, door, or deck for shade or shelter.

**Example:** They sat under the cafe's awning.

**RRGM bridge:** A projecting cover that takes the weather so that the opening beneath stays usable.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A roof-like cover projecting from a wall to shelter what is beneath from sun or rain.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A fixed or retractable shade that cuts solar heat and sheds rain.

**Count condition:** A wall or structure, a projecting cover, and a sheltered space beneath.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An awning is attached to a building; a canopy may stand free.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A215 | axial

**Common meaning:** Relating to, forming, or lying along an axis.

**Example:** The column failed under axial load.

**RRGM bridge:** Locates something by reference to an axis already chosen.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Lying along, or defined in relation to, an axis.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [FOR], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Along the axis of a body, as with axial force and axial tilt.
- [BIO] Of the head and trunk skeleton as opposed to the limbs; of a horizontal slice in imaging.
- [FOR] Of symmetry about a line.
- [MYT] The "Axial Age": Jaspers's name for a period of parallel religious and philosophical change, a contested idea.

**Count condition:** An axis and a thing located along or by it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Axial is not radial. The imaging plane called axial cuts across the body's long axis.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A345 | axiology

**Common meaning:** The branch of philosophy that studies value, including ethics and aesthetics.

**Example:** Her thesis is on the axiology of scientific research.

**RRGM bridge:** Asks what counts as good and why, which any count condition for worth presupposes.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The study of value: what kinds there are, what has it, and how values are ranked.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A division of philosophy covering moral and aesthetic value.
- [REL] The values a community or research tradition holds and how they shape its practice.

**Count condition:** An inquiry into value or a set of value commitments so described.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Axiology is not ethics alone and not the economics of price. Describing what people value is not establishing what is valuable.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A169 | axiom

**Common meaning:** A statement accepted as true as the basis for reasoning; an established principle.

**Example:** Euclid's fifth axiom concerns parallel lines.

**RRGM bridge:** A starting point of a system. RRLM treats axioms as simultaneous constraints unless a derivation declares an order among them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A statement taken as a starting point of a system, from which others are derived and which is not itself derived within it.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] One of the unproved statements from which a formal theory is built.
- [COG] A principle taken as too basic to argue.
- [DIG] A statement assumed without proof in a proof system or knowledge base.

**Count condition:** A system, a statement at its base, and derivations that rest on it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An axiom is not a theorem, a provisional assumption, or necessarily self-evident. Axioms can be inconsistent. Choosing axioms is choosing a system.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A170 | axiomatic

**Common meaning:** Self-evident or unquestionable; relating to or based on axioms.

**Example:** It is axiomatic that supply affects price.

**RRGM bridge:** In its formal use, a statement of where something sits in a system. In its ordinary use, a claim that no argument is needed, which is a stronger thing to say.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having the standing of an axiom, or built by derivation from axioms.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Of a theory or method: proceeding from stated axioms by stated rules.
- [COG] Of a claim: taken as obvious.

**Count condition:** A system or a claim, and its axiom-like standing.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A formal axiom is a stipulation. Calling a claim about the world axiomatic does not support it and tends to close the question.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

---

## A214 | axis

**Common meaning:** An imaginary line about which a body rotates; a reference line for measuring coordinates; a central line or structure; an alliance.

**Example:** The Earth turns on its axis once a day.

**RRGM bridge:** A comparison needs an axis. Naming it is the first step, and asking why that axis was chosen is a separate check.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A line taken as the fixed reference about which things turn or along which they are ordered.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A coordinate reference line.
- [PHY] A line of rotation or symmetry.
- [BIO] A main line of the body plan; the second neck vertebra; a chain of interacting glands.
- [REL] An alignment between powers; a dimension along which positions are compared.

**Count condition:** A reference line, and things turning about it or ordered along it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An axis is not an axle, which is a physical shaft. It is usually imaginary. Which axes are chosen determines which differences show.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0053 | axis mundi

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** In the study of religion and myth, the centre of the world conceived as a link between heaven, earth, and the underworld.

**Example:** The world tree serves as an axis mundi in Norse myth.

**RRGM bridge:** A centre from which orientation is taken, and the place where the realms are joined and so can be crossed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The centre of the world conceived as a vertical link joining the cosmic realms.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] A comparative category applied to world trees, sacred mountains, and pillars in many traditions.
- [LIN] Latin for "axis of the world".

**Count condition:** A named tradition, a centre, and a connection between cosmic levels.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The term is a scholar's category; traditions use their own names. A tradition may have many such centres. Not every sacred centre is a vertical link.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A370 | axle

**Common meaning:** A rod or shaft on which a wheel or pair of wheels turns.

**Example:** The cart broke an axle on the track.

**RRGM bridge:** The physical shaft, where "axis" is the line.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A shaft on or with which a wheel or pair of wheels turns.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Carries load, transmits driving torque, or both.
- [REL] A unit for vehicle classification, weight limits, and tolls.

**Count condition:** A shaft and one or more wheels turning on or with it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An axle is not an axis. Some axles turn with the wheels and some are fixed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## NEW-A-0054 | axon

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The long thin fibre of a nerve cell that carries signals away from the cell body.

**Example:** The axon of a motor neuron can be a metre long.

**RRGM bridge:** The sending line of a cell. Its direction of conduction is part of what it is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The process of a neuron that conducts impulses away from the cell body toward other cells.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Arises from the cell body, may be insulated with myelin, and ends in terminals that release transmitter.

**Count condition:** A neuronal process that carries impulses away from the cell body.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An axon is not a dendrite, which receives. A nerve is a bundle of many axons.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-10

## A400 | azalea

**Common meaning:** A flowering shrub with clusters of brightly coloured flowers.

**Example:** The azaleas bloom in May.

**RRGM bridge:** A gardener's name for part of a larger botanical group.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A flowering shrub of the genus Rhododendron, usually of the smaller-leaved kinds.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Classified within Rhododendron; all parts contain toxins harmful to people and animals.

**Count condition:** A plant of the azalea groups of Rhododendron.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Botanically, azaleas are rhododendrons and not a separate genus.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]
